## management-governance — module synthesis

_Source: `99_management-governance-synthesis.md`_

# Management-Governance Module — INDIAMART (Synthesis)

## Abstract

Two founder-controllers who built IndiaMART from 1996/1999 and still hold roughly 49% of it run a company with clean audits, near-zero related-party leakage, no debt, and candid quarterly disclosure of a real supplier-churn problem it has mostly failed to fix on schedule. Capital allocation is mixed: disciplined, all-shareholder-fair buybacks and dividends sit next to a decade of diffuse minority-stake investing whose FY26 return on newly deployed capital turned negative. Pay rewards an undisclosed scorecard, not a proven per-share metric, and 2026 saw executive pay rise while median employee pay fell. The one finding that overrides all of this: the FY26 Annual Report never discloses an unresolved 2021 criminal FIR (IPC Section 295, IT Act Section 66D) against the founder-CEO and co-founder-WTD — the non-disclosure, not the underlying allegation, fails this module's Non-Negotiable Gate. Verdict: Serious governance concerns, held there by one closable disclosure gap sitting on top of an otherwise reasonably clean governance architecture.

## 1. Stewardship Verdict

- **Verdict: Serious governance concerns** (gate-forced floor; the underlying operating and ownership picture is closer to "Standard / mixed," but the hard lock governs).
- **NON-NEGOTIABLE GATE: FAIL.** Tripping fact: gate condition 4 — "a material undisclosed legal/regulatory matter was found (A9-10 / A16-04 Red on the undisclosed prong)." `12_regulatory-legal-and-compliance` Section 8 (finding 12-009, A9-10) graded **Red**: a dated, five-outlet-corroborated 2021 criminal FIR (IPC Section 295 "deliberately outraging religious feelings" + IT Act Section 66D) against founder-CEO Dinesh Chandra Agarwal and co-founder-WTD Brijesh Kumar Agrawal — filed 07-Feb-2021, Govardhan police station, Mathura, over a third-party seller's sale of "Govardhan Shila" stones on the IndiaMART platform — is **absent from every legal/proceedings disclosure in the FY26 Annual Report** (a full-text search of the 17,695-line filing for "criminal," "IPC," "Section 295," "Govardhan," "police," "66D" and "proceedings against director" returned nothing beyond an unrelated SEBI/MCA-debarment boilerplate certificate). Red Flag ID **RF-CMP-001**. Per the Person-Level Integrity Protocol's allegation-versus-conviction rule, the underlying FIR is an unresolved allegation (High materiality, not Critical, and does not itself fail the gate) — but the Hard Rule's stated exception applies exactly here: "an allegation-stage matter that the company CONCEALED... fails the gate on the concealment, not the allegation." Locks applied: Governance risk floored at 80 (set at **82**, reflecting additional negative factors below); Governance Rating capped at no better than **Weak**; stewardship verdict capped at no better than **Serious governance concerns**.
- **Hard disqualifier flagged (business-model/01)?** **N.** `business-model/01_disqualifier-scan.md` found no hard disqualifier (auditor unqualified, no going concern, RPT well under threshold, promoter pledge undisclosed/immaterial). It logged a soft, non-hard-locking integrity note (two self-reported Secretarial Audit PIT lapses) and routed it downstream; `01_management-and-track-record` investigated it and did **not** emit RF-MGT-005 (the lapses are procedural and self-disclosed, not adverse to character). This deference is reported verbatim — it does **not** cover the FIR finding above, which this module discovered independently through its own person- and company-level sweeps (07 and 12).

- Capital allocation /100: **65**
- Incentive alignment /100: **41**
- Shareholder friendliness /100: **69**
- Disclosure candor /100: **50** (capped — pre-cap component read 79/100; see Section 4)
- People & network integrity /100: **50** (capped — raw component sum 65/100; see Section 4)
- Audit & assurance quality /100: **78**
- RPT & leakage risk /100 *(higher = worse)*: **27**
- Contingent-liability risk /100 *(higher = worse)*: **28**
- Accounting-forensics risk /100 *(higher = worse)*: **17**
- Legal & regulatory risk /100 *(higher = worse)*: **24**
- Governance risk /100 *(higher = worse)*: **82** (gate-forced floor 80, set above floor)
- Data quality /100: **88**
- Overall usefulness /100: **85**
- **Checklist coverage:** 167/179 registry items answered (Green/Amber/Red) = **93.3%** — 120 Green, 37 Amber, 10 Red, 12 Not Applicable / Insufficient Data
- Insider ownership (one line): 49.11% Promoter & Promoter Group (18 holders); founders' combined 46.99% direct stake and share count unchanged year-on-year (the 6bp % decline is ESOP-dilution arithmetic, not a sale); no promoter-level pledge disclosed [`04_ownership-and-insider-behavior`].
- Biggest governance signal (one line): an unresolved 2021 criminal FIR against both founder-controllers is invisible anywhere in the FY26 Annual Report — the non-disclosure, not the allegation itself, is what fails the Non-Negotiable Gate [`12_regulatory-legal-and-compliance` Section 8, RF-CMP-001].
- **Checklist Risk** = max(RPT & leakage 27, Contingent-liability 28, Accounting-forensics 17, Legal & regulatory 24) = **28** (Contingent-liability risk is the binding one).
- **Governance Score /100** = 0.14×65(CapAlloc) + 0.11×41(Incentive) + 0.11×69(ShFriendliness) + 0.10×50(Candor) + 0.11×57(MgmtQuality) + 0.09×78(AuditAssurance) + 0.11×50(PeopleNetworkIntegrity) + 0.11×(100−82)(GovRisk) + 0.12×(100−28)(ChecklistRisk) = 9.10 + 4.51 + 7.59 + 5.00 + 6.27 + 7.02 + 5.50 + 1.98 + 8.64 = **55.6 → 56**.
- **Confidence-Adjusted Governance Score /100** = 56 × 76/100 = **42.6 → 43**.
- **Governance Rating: Weak** — overridden down from the score's raw "Watchlist" band (55–69) by the Gate-FAIL hard lock ("no better than Weak" regardless of the arithmetic). This mismatch between the numeric score and the rating band is intentional and required by the MODULE_RULES override, not an error — flagged here explicitly per the self-check.
- **Confidence Score /100: 76** — strong primary-filing coverage overall (full Annual Report, 22 transcripts, CIQ exports, postal-ballot result, secretarial audit, all present; triage verdict "Sufficient," no partial-data caps triggered) offset by real, named coverage gaps on the single most decision-relevant open question: the FIR's current procedural status (eCourts Mathura / Allahabad HC portals captcha-gated, not queried), plus DIN confirmation gaps for the two founders, an unreachable ICAI disciplinary database, a deferred trademark-proprietor search, and a geo-blocked wilful-defaulter database.
- **Red-Flag Count / Critical Red-Flag Count: 6 / 1.**

## 1A. Module Disconfirmation

- **Strongest bear point:** the FY26 Annual Report's own text — 17,695 lines, searched exhaustively — contains no mention of a dated, five-outlet-corroborated 2021 criminal FIR against the two people who together control ~49% of the company and run it day to day. The only related disclosure is a narrower "not debarred by SEBI/MCA" boilerplate that does not cover a pending criminal matter [`12_regulatory-legal-and-compliance` Section 8].
- **Strongest bull point (steelman):** strip out that one finding and the governance architecture reads well by evidence, not adjectives — unqualified audit opinions and zero non-audit fees across the whole KPMG-network tenure [`08`], Beneish M=−3.29 and Dechow F=0.76, both comfortably Green with cash flow exceeding profit in every year read [`11`], RPT at 0.24–4.14% of revenue with no round-tripping across three years [`09`], net cash throughout with zero debt ever drawn [`02`, `11`], founders' share count unchanged and no pledge [`04`], and a functioning postal-ballot channel that actually recorded a 21.72%-against vote rather than manufacturing consensus [`05`]. Of the 33 people swept by `07`, 31 are Clean with no other adverse cross-linkage anywhere in the network.
- **Single killer risk:** if the Govardhan Shila FIR is confirmed still open and ultimately produces a chargesheet or conviction against either founder, that moves the person grade from Material concerns to Disqualifying and trips the Non-Negotiable Gate on **proof**, not concealment — a materially harder lock than the current one, and one that would sit directly on the two people who run the company and hold roughly half of it.
- **Disconfirming evidence already visible:** this sweep could not determine whether the FIR is still live or was quietly closed by police (a negative final report generates no reported judgment); no chargesheet, discharge, quashing order, or conviction was found on Indian Kanoon or in dated media through 2026, and the underlying allegation is a religious-offense/third-party-marketplace-content matter, not an economic-fraud charge against the founders' own conduct [`07` Section 2.1-2.2; `12` Section 8].

## 3. Reconciliation

Two genuine disagreements-in-emphasis surfaced across the specialists, both reconciled the same way -- toward the more conservative read, per CLAUDE.md Section 4:

**Candor (06) vs. disclosure integrity (12).** `06_candor-and-disclosure-quality` read the company as "candid / high-trust" (79/100) based on verbatim earnings-call transcripts -- no non-GAAP overlay, misses owned in real time, zero deflected questions across the two most recent calls. `12_regulatory-legal-and-compliance`, working from a different lens (the company's own primary filing text against public court and media records), found that the FY26 Annual Report never discloses an unresolved 2021 criminal FIR against the two founder-controllers. Both reads are correct on their own evidence -- verbal candor about operating misses on earnings calls is real and well-documented, and it is a separate question from whether the company's own statutory filing discloses a material legal matter. The two do not actually contradict each other (one is about how management talks, the other about what the filing omits), but per the Score Cap Rules the more decision-relevant, more conservative finding governs the composite: **Disclosure candor is capped at 50** in the Governance Score regardless of 06's own 79 tone-based read, and 06's own score is reported alongside, uncapped, so a reader can see both.

**Allegation cap (07) vs. concealment gate (12).** `07_people-integrity-dossiers` capped People & network integrity at 50 and conviction at no better than "Watchlist" -- the FIR is an unresolved allegation, which per the Person-Level Integrity Protocol does not by itself fail the Non-Negotiable Gate. `12_regulatory-legal-and-compliance` separately found that the company's own filing conceals that same allegation, which -- per the Hard Rule's own stated exception -- does fail the gate, on the concealment rather than the allegation. This is not a disagreement to resolve; it is the two-track design working exactly as intended (allegation caps conviction; concealment fails the gate outright), and both locks are carried forward together in this synthesis.

No other material disagreements were found: `02` (capital allocation), `04` (ownership), `05` (board), `08` (audit), `09` (RPT), `10` (contingent liabilities), and `11` (forensics) all point the same direction on their respective questions, and none contradicts the FIR-related findings above.

If a hard disqualifier had been flagged, the stewardship verdict could not exceed "Serious governance concerns" -- this run has no hard disqualifier, but the Gate FAIL (condition 4, an equally binding Hard Rule lock) produces the identical ceiling through a different, evidence-specific route. No Section 24 rejector-filter score penalty applies beyond the Material-concerns and undisclosed-matter caps already applied above.

## 5. Stewardship Summary

Have these people delivered on what they promised? Mixed to weak: three of the CEO's own dated, specific targets on the core business (margin, supplier net-adds, growth aspiration) were missed, two by a wide margin, against one narrow target roughly met and one management-transition promise delivered exactly on schedule [`01`]. Has capital been allocated to create per-share value? Also mixed: two disciplined, all-shareholder-fair tender buybacks and a consistently-paid, well-covered dividend sit against a decade of diffuse minority-stake investing with no disclosed aggregate return, one struck-off subsidiary, and a FY26 incremental return on newly deployed capital that turned negative for the first time in the five-year window [`02`]. Do incentives reward per-share value or empire-building? Neither cleanly: the annual bonus rewards an undisclosed scorecard, the only long-term incentive vests on time served rather than a return hurdle, the CEO and senior Whole-Time Director received zero equity compensation, and FY2026 saw every executive's pay rise in the same year median employee pay fell 2.17% [`03`]. Are minority shareholders protected, and is management candid when results are bad? On operating results, genuinely yes -- management named its own misses in the same units it guided, in real time, with no non-GAAP overlay to hide behind [`06`]. On legal disclosure, no: the company's own Annual Report omits an unresolved 2021 criminal FIR against the two people who together control roughly half the company and run it day to day, and the one related-party resolution actually put to a shareholder vote this year drew 21.72% opposition with no visible board response [`05`, `09`, `12`]. The single most important reason not to extend this team full trust today is that undisclosed matter -- not because the underlying allegation is proven (it is not), but because a board and a management team that will not put a known, dated, five-outlet-corroborated legal matter about its own two controllers in front of shareholders is not yet the fully transparent steward the rest of this record otherwise supports.

## 5A. Red-Flag Register

| Red Flag ID | Trigger | Severity (High / Critical) | Evidence | Source + Date | Score Impact | Follow-up |
|---|---|---|---|---|---:|---|
| RF-CMP-001 | Undisclosed material litigation -- the Govardhan Shila FIR is absent from the FY26 Annual Report's full text | Critical (gate-tripping) | 5-outlet dated media (Feb-2021), full-text AR search returning no hit, Indian Kanoon court-record search returning no result | `12_regulatory-legal-and-compliance` Sec8, 2026-08-22 | Non-Negotiable Gate FAIL; Governance risk floor 80; Disclosure candor max 50; verdict capped at "Serious governance concerns" | Confirm eCourts (Mathura) / Allahabad HC case-status; confirm whether an FIR (vs. arrest) legally triggers the Board's Report disclosure obligation |
| RF-PPL-001 | Unresolved 2021 criminal FIR (IPC Section 295 + IT Act Section 66D) against founder-CEO Dinesh Chandra Agarwal and co-founder-WTD Brijesh Kumar Agrawal | High (allegation-stage, not proven) | Same 5-outlet media sweep; no chargesheet/discharge/quashing/conviction found | `07_people-integrity-dossiers` Sec2.1-2.2, 2026-08-22 | People & network integrity capped at 50; Governance risk floor 55 (superseded by the gate-fail floor of 80); no rating above "Watchlist" until resolved | Track resolution status; hand to 12 for the canonical company-level check (done -- see RF-CMP-001) |
| RF-SHR-001 | 21.72% votes against the Amit Agarwal related-party office-of-profit resolution, with no disclosed board response | High | 33,955,911 votes polled; 7,374,238 against | `05_board-and-shareholder-rights` Sec3, `09_related-party-and-group-forensics` Sec5, 2026-08-22 (event: 29-Aug-2025) | Shareholder friendliness component reduced (05's own score already reflects this); flagged for the master synthesizer's kill-criteria list | Obtain 26th/27th AGM scrutinizer reports; confirm whether the board ever responded to the dissent |
| RF-CMP-003 | Five self-reported, self-penalised SEBI PIT Code violations across 2020, 2021, and FY26 -- repeated but transparent | High (per `12`'s own explicit call, not Critical -- no SEBI order, no promoter/KMP involvement, no UPSI finding) | BSE/NSE filing dated 24-May-2021 (2 events); FY26 Secretarial Audit Report (2 further events) | `12_regulatory-legal-and-compliance` Sec5, 2026-08-22 | Legal & regulatory risk component increased | Confirm identities of the two unnamed FY26 Designated Persons; watch for a sixth recurrence |
| RF-MGT-003 | Management stopped giving numeric guidance (net-adds, margin) after repeated misses, without resetting an achievable target; KPI-adjacent | Medium-High | Q4 FY24 transcript ("we would come back with a better guidance"); no numeric target reintroduced through Q1 FY27 | `01_management-and-track-record` Sec4, `06_candor-and-disclosure-quality` Sec1/Sec4A, 2026-08-22 | Management quality and Disclosure candor components reduced (already reflected in 01's and 06's own scores) | Watch for any return to specific numeric targets set at an achievable level |
| RF-CAP-001 | Livekeeping Private Limited (intermediate subsidiary layer of the Finlite/Livekeeping deal) struck off Oct-2024; goodwill of Rs420.38mn on the parent CGU remains carried | Medium | AR Note 33(a); goodwill balance unchanged FY25 to FY26 | `02_capital-allocation-scorecard` Sec2, 2026-08-22 | Capital allocation component reduced (already reflected in 02's own score) | Confirm goodwill treatment/impairment status for the Livekeeping CGU in the next filing |

Red-flag count: **6**. Critical: **1** (RF-CMP-001).

**Gate-tripping flags are Critical.** RF-CMP-001 is the flag that trips the Non-Negotiable Gate (condition 4) and is recorded above at Critical severity accordingly. The gate FAIL and this Critical row are the same fact, viewed from the gate side and the red-flag side -- a gate FAIL with zero Critical rows would be a self-contradiction; that is not the case here.

## 5B. Peer Governance Benchmark

No full peer governance dataset was available in this module's pool. `business-model/08_competitive-map` was not directly consumed by this module for a governance-metric peer set, and individual specialists that attempted a peer comparison found either "No peer set" (the standard state across most checklist items) or a single, unverified, web-sourced comparison:

| Metric | Company | Peer Median | Peer Verdict (Better / In-line / Worse) |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| Board independence % | 62.5% (55.6% FYE) | Not assessed -- no peer governance dataset in pool | Not assessed |
| Insider / promoter holding % | 49.11% | Not assessed | Not assessed |
| Pledge % | 0% (not disclosed) | Not assessed | Not assessed |
| Non-audit / audit fee ratio | 0% | Not assessed | Not assessed |
| RPT intensity (% of revenue) | 0.24-4.14% | Not assessed | Not assessed |
| AGM votes-against (key resolutions) | 21.72% (the one RPT-adjacent vote available) | Not assessed | Not assessed |
| CEO total compensation (single web-sourced comparable, unverified) | ~Rs83.3mn (FY26) | ~Rs26.9mn (Info Edge/Naukri CEO, one comparable, n=1) | Worse than peer (higher pay), low confidence (`03` Sec3) |

Relative governance is not assessed on any structural metric -- the single available comparison (CEO pay vs. one Indian internet-platform peer) is web-sourced, unverified, and n=1; it is directional context only, not a benchmark to rely on.

## 5C. Governance Change Since Last Run

This run executed under `NOSTRA_EXACT_MODULE_RESUME=1` (confirmed by `07` and `12`'s own resume notes). Per the Hard Rule: **Historical delta not read in exact scoped resume -- current checkpointed snapshot only.** The cockpit has fingerprinted and read-locked only the staged current run root, and this synthesis may not add an uncheckpointed prior-dated input after the paid scope was approved. `07` and `12` each independently confirm they ran a **full sweep dated 2026-08-22** rather than a delta-refresh against any prior dossier -- the FIR non-disclosure finding in this run is therefore a first-time discovery within this module's own history, not a change from a previously-reported clean state.

## 5D. Analyst Follow-Up Questions

- **The FIR (RF-CMP-001 / RF-PPL-001):** Is the Govardhan Shila FIR still procedurally open, or was it closed by police with a negative final report that generates no reported judgment? Does an FIR (as opposed to an arrest or chargesheet) legally trigger the Companies Act "proceedings against directors" disclosure obligation in India -- and if the company's own counsel concludes it does not, why is that legal position itself not stated anywhere in the filing? Is this company-specific or would a peer company with an unrelated third-party seller's marketplace-content dispute face the same non-disclosure question?
- **RPT dissent (RF-SHR-001):** Has the board responded to the 21.72%-against vote in any subsequent communication not captured in this pool? Is the underlying office-of-profit role (Amit Agarwal) still active, and at what remuneration level in FY27?
- **PIT lapses (RF-CMP-003):** Who are the two unnamed FY26 Designated Persons, and were they employees, KMP-adjacent, or genuinely below that layer? Is the recurrence (5 events across 6 years) accelerating or a stable low-level base rate for a company of this size?
- **Guidance withdrawal (RF-MGT-003):** Is the churn problem behind the FY24 miss actually resolving (per FY27 supplier-net-add trend), and would that make a return to numeric guidance appropriate, or is silence now the more honest posture?
- **Capital allocation (RF-CAP-001, incremental ROIC):** Is the FY26 negative incremental ROIC a one-year artefact of new venture-investment timing, or the start of a trend? Does management have a disclosed aggregate-return metric for the ~19-deal minority-investment portfolio it could publish?
- **Incentive design (A6-05/A6-06):** Would the company adopt a clawback/malus policy, and would it disclose the Balanced Scorecard's actual metrics and weights?

## 6. What Would Change The Stewardship Verdict?

| Current Verdict | What Would Strengthen It | What Would Weaken It | Data Needed |
|---|---|---|---|
| Serious governance concerns (gate-forced floor) | Primary confirmation the FIR was formally closed (police closure report, discharge, or quashing) and/or that Indian company law does not treat an FIR alone as triggering the Board's Report disclosure obligation -- either would lift the Non-Negotiable Gate FAIL and allow re-scoring toward "Standard / mixed" or better, given the otherwise clean audit, RPT, forensics, and ownership picture. A subsequent Annual Report that addresses the matter directly (even to state it was investigated and closed) would also resolve the concealment prong regardless of the underlying merits. | Confirmation of a chargesheet or conviction against either founder would escalate the person grade from Material concerns to Disqualifying and trip a harder, proof-based gate lock. Discovery of a second undisclosed material legal matter, or evidence the company was aware of and deliberately withheld the FIR (rather than an omission), would deepen rather than resolve the concern. | eCourts (Mathura district) and Allahabad High Court case-status confirmation (both currently captcha/browser-gated); a direct legal opinion or company statement on the Board's Report disclosure-trigger question; the next Annual Report's own treatment (or non-treatment) of the matter |

## 7. Note To The Final Synthesizer

- **Stewardship verdict: Serious governance concerns**, held there by one specific, closable finding -- the FY26 Annual Report's non-disclosure of an unresolved 2021 criminal FIR against the founder-CEO and co-founder-WTD -- sitting on top of an otherwise genuinely clean governance architecture (unqualified audits, zero non-audit fees, no leverage, low RPT, aligned founder ownership with no pledge, a functioning postal-ballot channel, Beneish/Dechow both comfortably Green).
- **Non-Negotiable Gate result: FAIL.** Tripping fact: gate condition 4 (a material undisclosed legal/regulatory matter, A9-10 Red per `12`). Locks applied: Governance risk floor 80 (set at 82); Governance Rating capped at "Weak"; verdict capped at "Serious governance concerns." This is a concealment-based gate failure, not a proof-based one -- the underlying FIR remains an unresolved allegation, not an adjudicated fact, and no controller/CEO/CFO/chair is graded Disqualifying.
- **The capital-allocation record:** mixed, not value-destructive. Clean, fair, debt-free distribution mechanics (two pro-rata tender buybacks, a 5-year-consistent dividend) sit against a decade of diffuse, largely unproven minority-stake venture investing whose FY26 incremental return on newly deployed capital turned **negative** for the first time in the 5-year window read.
- **Incentives and ownership:** ownership alignment is strong and genuine (founders' 46.99% direct stake, share count unchanged, no pledge) -- the real alignment mechanism at this company is pre-existing equity, not the pay plan. The pay plan itself is mixed-to-weak: the annual bonus scorecard is undisclosed, the only long-term incentive is time-vested not return-vested, the founders received zero equity comp, there is no clawback policy, and FY2026 saw every executive's pay rise while median employee pay fell.
- **The people read:** of 33 people swept, 31 are Clean; 2 (the founder-CEO and co-founder-WTD) carry Material concerns on their own record -- the unresolved FIR above. No other person carries any adverse finding, and no register person or entity carries a live hop-1 cross-link to an adverse network (A17-07 Green). This is the open Section 24 Filter 1 item: not "buzz" (RF-MGT-005 was correctly not emitted for a separate, cleared, procedural PIT matter), but a real, dated, corroborated allegation now compounded by non-disclosure.
- **The checklist read:** 93.3% coverage (167 of 179 items answered Green/Amber/Red; 12 genuinely Not Applicable or Insufficient Data). 10 Red items total; the worst of the four inverted Checklist Risk components is Contingent-liability risk (28), driven mainly by the item-level posture-deterioration finding on the GST TRAN-1 dispute (one appellate loss sustained while the headline total stayed flat) rather than by size (total contingent liabilities are just 2.17% of net worth).
- **The biggest governance risk / red flag:** RF-CMP-001 (Critical, gate-tripping) -- the undisclosed FIR. Second: RF-PPL-001 (High) -- the same underlying allegation at the person level. Third: RF-SHR-001 (High) -- a real, above-threshold minority-shareholder dissent vote that drew no visible board response.
- **Hard disqualifier from `business-model/01_disqualifier-scan`:** None flagged. Verbatim: "no disqualifier triggered" -- auditor unqualified, no going concern, RPT well under threshold, no material restatement, no confirmed enforcement action. A separate soft integrity note (self-reported Secretarial Audit PIT lapses) was investigated and found non-adverse (RF-MGT-005 not emitted). This module's own gate-fail finding (the FIR non-disclosure) is independent of, and not covered by, that deference -- it was discovered by this module's own person- and company-level sweeps (07, 12), not inherited from business-model/01.
- **Section 24 rejector filters:** Filter 1 (crooks/integrity) -- the underlying trigger is now a documented allegation plus concealment, not mere buzz; captured via RF-PPL-001 + RF-CMP-001, not RF-MGT-005. Filter 2 (turnaround) -- not applicable, no penalty. Filter 3 (leverage) -- net cash, treated as a strategic asset, no penalty. Filter 4 (serial acquirer) -- assessed and below the hard-cap threshold, though the diffuse minority-investment pattern is a real, ongoing drag reflected in the Capital allocation score. Filter 6 (unaligned owner) -- clean, aligned owner-operator, no cap.
- **Partial-data caps applied:** Disclosure candor capped 79 to 50 (RF-CMP-001); People & network integrity capped 65 to 50 (Material-concerns person cap, superseded in effect by the gate-fail floor on Governance risk). Confidence Score capped at 76 by named, specific coverage gaps (DIN confirmation for both founders, ICAI disciplinary database unreachable, eCourts/Allahabad HC portals gated, trademark-proprietor search deferred) -- not by a blanket "sweep did not run" cap, since the sweep plainly ran in depth on every axis it could reach.
- **Biggest missing data point (single highest-value next request):** confirm the current procedural status of the Feb-2021 Govardhan Shila FIR -- chargesheeted, discharged, quashed, or still formally open -- via a browser-driven eCourts (Mathura district) and Allahabad High Court case-status search, and confirm whether Indian company law treats an FIR alone as triggering the Board's Report "proceedings against directors" disclosure obligation. This single fact governs the person-level grade for the two most powerful people at the company, the company-level gate finding, and the master synthesizer's own read of the concealment question.
- **Explicit handoff:** this module supersedes the `business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance` quick-read. The master synthesizer should treat THIS module's governance verdict, gate result, and scores as the primary governance read -- in particular, the Non-Negotiable Gate FAIL and its locks (Governance risk >=80, Rating <=Weak, verdict <="Serious governance concerns") are a harder, more specific finding than anything in the business-model quick-read and should govern the headline.

## 8. Simple Summary

- Management has mostly **not** delivered on the specific growth and margin targets it set itself -- three misses, one target roughly met, one operational promise (a CFO handover) kept exactly.
- Capital has been allocated fairly to shareholders (clean buybacks, a growing dividend, no debt) but its deployment into a decade of small minority-stake bets has not yet proven itself -- and the newest year's return on that new capital turned negative.
- The pay plan rewards an **undisclosed** scorecard, not a proven per-share metric; the founders get no equity comp at all, and there's no clawback policy.
- Skin in the game is real: the two founders hold ~49% directly, bought through founding, not granted, and their share count did not move all year -- but a company-owned insider (a Secretarial Audit lapse) briefly pledged shares without pre-clearance, self-reported.
- Minority shareholders are **not fully protected right now**: a related-party pay resolution drew nearly a quarter of votes against with no visible response, and the company's own Annual Report never mentions an unresolved criminal case against its two controllers.
- Management **is** candid about bad operating results in real time -- no adjusted-earnings games, no dodged questions on the two most recent earnings calls.
- **No hard disqualifier was flagged** by the business-model scan -- but this module's own sweep found something the scan did not: an undisclosed material legal matter, which independently fails this module's own gate.
- This module is highly useful for the master synthesizer: it surfaces one specific, well-evidenced, closable finding that changes the headline verdict, sitting on top of an otherwise genuinely well-run governance operation.

## management-governance / 00_governance-data-triage.md

# Governance Data Triage — INDIAMART

No `ciq_facts.json` and no `relationships.json` sidecar exists for this run under `analyses/INDIAMART_2026-08-20/_pool_extracts/` — every figure below is this agent's own sourced read of the CIQ workbook extracts and the primary filings. ## 6. Sufficiency Verdict

- **Verdict:** Sufficient
- **Reason:** A full India statutory Annual Report (AGM Notice, Corporate Governance Report, MR-3 Secretarial Audit Report, quantified related-party note, contingent-liabilities note, auditor's report and fee disclosure, and a Postal Ballot voting result) plus current CIQ board/committee/compensation/ownership exports and a multi-year capital-allocation and compensation history are all present, satisfying every leg of the Sufficient rule.
- **Specialists that can run:** management track record, capital allocation, incentives, ownership, board, candor, people dossiers (07, seeded per 5E), audit quality (08), RPT/group forensics (09), contingent liabilities (10), accounting forensics (11, ≥2 years financials available), regulatory/legal sweep (12, with the 27th-AGM-result and credit-rating caveats below)
- **Hard disqualifier already flagged by business-model/01_disqualifier-scan?** N — no disqualifier triggered (`business-model/01_disqualifier-scan.md`, Section 3: "Any disqualifier triggered: N"). A soft integrity note is logged and relayed for weighing, not locking: an unnamed Designated Person's share-pledge pre-clearance lapse and a closed-window one-share trade (Secretarial Audit Report), plus the 21.72%-against Postal Ballot vote on the Amit Agarwal related-party office-of-profit appointment (`business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance`) — neither meets the §24 Filter 1 hard-lock bar per the disqualifier scan's own adjudication, but both are live inputs for agents 03/05/06/09.
- **Active partial-data caps:** None triggered at Sufficient. Two narrow gaps are carried forward for individual specialists to record as Not Available on their own line, without capping the overall module verdict: (1) no declared voting result/scrutinizer report specifically for the 27th AGM (29-Jun-2026) — 05 uses the FY26 Postal Ballot result (which does exist, with a quantified 21.72%-against vote) as its dissent evidence instead; (2) no rating-agency report in the pool, explained by the company's own "Not Applicable" disclosure (debt-free balance sheet) — 12 records rating conduct as Not Applicable, not Not Available.
- **Critical missing items:** None blocking.
- **Single highest-value missing document:** The 27th AGM's (29-Jun-2026) own scrutinizer's report / declared e-voting result — would let 05 quantify minority-shareholder dissent on the current-year AGM resolutions specifically (director re-appointments, auditor ratification), complementing the FY26 Postal Ballot result already in hand.

# Management & Track Record — INDIAMART

## 1. Key Executives

| Role | Name | Appointed | Tenure | Relevant Background | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CEO, MD & Director (Founder) | Dinesh Chandra Agarwal | 13-Sep-1999 (company founding) | ~27 years | Founded IndiaMART in 1999; prior roles at HCL Technologies (USA), HCL Hewlett-Packard, C-DOT and CMC Ltd; B.Tech (Computer Science), Harcourt Butler Technological Institute, Kanpur University | [CIQ Board Members.xls, "Agarwal, Dinesh Chandra"]; [FY26 Annual Report (Jun-02-2026), Note 33(b) KMP list] |
| CFO | Jitin Diwan | 15-Jun-2024 | ~2.2 years | Qualified Chartered Accountant (ICAI); previously Head of Finance (VP) at Upstox Securities, with prior roles at Amazon India, Bharti Airtel, Vodafone | [CIQ Professionals.rtf]; [FY26 Annual Report, signature block] |
| Whole-Time Director (Co-Founder) | Brijesh Kumar Agrawal | 13-Sep-1999 (company founding) | ~27 years | Co-founded IndiaMART in 1999; served as COO; prior Supply Chain Consultant at Miebach Logistics GmbH, Bangalore; master's in Management Science, University of Lucknow | [CIQ Board Members.xls, "Agrawal, Brijesh Kumar"] |
| Group General Counsel, Company Secretary & Whole-Time Director | Manoj Bhargava (DIN 08267536) | Associated with company since 28-Dec-2017; WTD title since 2025 | ~8.6 years with company (~1 year in WTD title) | Qualified Company Secretary (ICSI); B.Com, LLB (Delhi University), LLM (GGSIP University); prior roles at HT Media, Varun Beverages, Barista Coffee, Integrated Databases India (India Today group) | [CIQ Board Members.xls, "Bhargava, Manoj"]; [FY26 Annual Report signature block] |
| Compliance Officer | Vasudha Bagri | 22-Jan-2025 | ~1.6 years | Role split from Manoj Bhargava's prior Compliance Officer function, coinciding with the Jan-2025 SEBI PIT-regime role change | [FY26 Annual Report, Note 33(b) KMP list]; [CIQ Public Company Profile] |

**Founder / owner-operator status.** IndiaMART is founder-run, not professionally-hired-CEO-run: both the CEO/MD (Dinesh Agarwal) and the Whole-Time Director (Brijesh Agrawal) have led the company continuously since its 1999 incorporation and together hold 49.08% of the company directly (29.09% + 19.99%) [CIQ Public Ownership Summary — Top Holders, position date 31-Mar-2026]. This is a genuine owner-operator structure — 27 years of continuous operating tenure at the top, with a professional CFO/CS layer beneath the two founders, not a purely family-only decision chain (ownership/control depth is scored in full by `04_ownership-and-insider-behavior`; this agent notes it only as context for tenure).

## 2. Management Stability

| Signal | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Senior departures (last 3 years) | One CFO transition: Prateek Chandra (CFO for ~9 years, 30-Apr-2015 to 14-Jun-2024) moved internally to Chief Strategy Officer, focused on inorganic growth — announced on the Q4 FY24 call roughly six weeks before it took effect, not a sudden exit, and Chandra remains with the company today. Two Independent Directors completed statutory terms and rotated off (Rajesh Sawhney, second-term completion, Sep-2025; Aakash Chaudhry, Jan-2025); three new Independent Directors joined in 2025 (Sandeep Barasia, Manish Vij, Vasuta Agarwal). One non-independent founder-family-linked director, Dhruv Prakash, ceased at the Jun-2026 AGM (term-related, no stated concern). | [Q4 FY24 transcript, Apr-30-2024, prepared remarks]; [FY26 Annual Report, Note 33(b); director-cessation disclosures]; [Corporate Timeline.xls, 29-Jun-2026 entry] |
| CFO turnover | One change in the last decade (2015→2024→present), executed as a pre-announced, orderly internal succession, not a resignation under pressure. During FY2026 there were "no appointments/resignation of KMP" [FY26 Annual Report]. This does NOT meet the A13-04 red band ("serial exits at one role, e.g. 3 CFOs in 4 years") | [Q4 FY24 transcript]; [FY26 Annual Report, Note 33(b), KMP table: "Chief Financial Officer (upto 14 June 2024)" / "(with effect from 15 June 2024)"] |
| CEO succession / continuity | No CEO succession event in the review window; both founders remain in place since 1999. No disclosed succession plan for a founder-CEO exit was located in this pool (a board-level item, scored under `05_board-and-shareholder-rights` A1-13) | [CIQ Board Members.xls]; [FY26 Annual Report] |
| Second-tier (SVP-level) churn | Below the formal KMP layer, several Senior Vice Presidents show compensation only in the FY2023/FY2024 CIQ columns and none in FY2025 — Chief Product Officer Amarinder Singh Dhaliwal, and SVPs Amit Jain, Sunil Parolia, Devendra Singh, Abhishek Thard, Vikas Deep Verma, plus a prior Chief Information Officer (Vivek Agrawal) — while the CIO role was refilled by a new hire (Nikhil S. Prabhakar) only in the FY2025 column. This pattern (six-plus named SVPs disappearing from consecutive-year compensation data within roughly one to two years) is a real bench-turnover signal, though the company's own AR states no *formal KMP* changes occurred, meaning these departures sit one level below the statutory KMP layer this module's checklist item targets. **This is not confirmed as departure vs a CIQ-data-lag artefact from the primary filing itself** — the annual report does not name or discuss SVP-level exits — so it is recorded as an observed pattern in the vendor compensation data, not a filing-sourced fact, and flagged for follow-up. | [CIQ Compensation Summary Compensation.xls, rows for Dhaliwal, Jain, Parolia, Singh, Thard, Verma, Agrawal (Vivek), Prabhakar]; Inference (pattern read), not from filings for the "departure" interpretation specifically |

Frequent CFO turnover is not present here — one change in a decade, disclosed in advance and executed as planned, is a stability signal, not a warning. The SVP-layer churn noted above IS a warning worth tracking, even though it does not touch the CEO/CFO/Company-Secretary layer the canonical red-flag list (RF-MGT-001/002) targets.

## 3. Stated Strategy & Targets

Management describes the core marketplace strategy as deepening monetisation of the existing paying-supplier base — moving suppliers from the entry-level Silver package into higher-value Gold/Platinum tiers via category-based pricing — while working through an above-normal churn problem on newer, first-year Silver-tier suppliers that has suppressed net supplier additions since FY24 [Q1 FY27 transcript, Jul-21-2026, Q&A, Dinesh Agarwal]. Alongside the core marketplace, the group is investing in a second growth leg, the Busy Infotech accounting-software subsidiary (acquired 2022 for ~₹500 crore / $66.93mm), and has just incorporated a new lending subsidiary, IndiaMART Finance Limited (certificate of incorporation 04-Aug-2026), aimed at MSME working-capital financing for platform users [Corporate Timeline.xls, 04-Aug-2026 and 21-Jul-2026 entries]. The company does not issue formal numeric guidance for consolidated revenue, EBITDA, or EPS [`earnings/04_guidance-consensus.md` §2, citing Q1 FY27 board-outcome press release]; the checkable targets in the record instead come from specific, dated statements management has made on quarterly calls — these are what Section 4 tests.

## 4. Promise vs Delivery

| Prior Promise / Target | Period Set | Actual Outcome | Kept / Missed | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| EBITDA margin: "target to reach 30% by the end of this year" (FY24); separately guided "28% to 29% EBITDA margins in FY24" | Q4 FY23 call, 28-Apr-2023, for FY24 (year ended Mar-2024) | FY24 EBITDA margin (consolidated): 26.54% | **Missed** — ~200-350bps below both the guided range and the stated target | `Q4 2023 Earnings Call, Apr 28, 2023` (promise) vs `earnings/03_margin-drivers.md` §5, citing FY26 Annual Report ratio note (outcome, EBITDA margin series 39.43%/26.57%/26.54%/37.03%/33.18% FY22-FY26) |
| Paying-supplier net additions: "8,000 definitely should be maintainable... anything upwards of 8,000 should be maintainable at least for this year" | Q4 FY23 call, 28-Apr-2023, for FY24 quarters | Net additions fell to "1,800 suppliers" (a quarter the company itself called its lowest ever) then partially recovered to "2,700 suppliers" by Q4 FY24 — roughly 66-77% below the guided 8,000-9,000/quarter range | **Missed** — large, multi-quarter shortfall; management itself stopped giving quarterly net-add guidance after this ("As soon as we see improvement in the churn... we would come back with a better guidance") | `Q4 2023 Earnings Call, Apr 28, 2023` (promise) vs `Q4 2024 Earnings Call, Apr 30, 2024` (outcome, same transcript as the withdrawal of guidance) |
| Standalone collection/revenue growth aspiration "20% to 25%"; consolidated aspiration "33%" | Q4 FY24 call, 30-Apr-2024, forward-looking aspiration ("over the next 2, 3 years") | Consolidated revenue growth: 16.01% (FY25), 13.02% (FY26); standalone revenue growth: +9% (FY26, ₹13,200.58mn→₹14,428.03mn) | **Missed** — both years landed well below the low end of the stated aspiration, in both years measured so far | `Q4 2024 Earnings Call, Apr 30, 2024` (promise) vs `earnings/01_historical-financials.md` §6 and `business-model/02_business-identity.md` §2 (outcome) |
| Collection growth "continue to target around 10%" | Q4 FY25 call, 29-Apr-2025, for FY26 | Standalone revenue growth FY26: +9% | **Roughly kept** — within ~1 percentage point of the stated target, the one checkable target in this record that was approximately met | `Q4 2025 Earnings Call, Apr 29, 2025` (promise) vs `business-model/02_business-identity.md` §2 (outcome) |
| Busy Infotech acquisition (2022, ~₹500cr): "don't lose money... over the next 2, 3 years as long as they are cash positive and continue to improve" — no specific margin target set deliberately | Q4 FY23 call, 28-Apr-2023 | Busy Infotech segment margin improved from −17.47% (FY25) to −2.23% (FY26), a 1,524bps narrowing, still loss-making but trending toward the stated "don't lose money" bar | **Partially kept / improving, not yet delivered** — directionally on the path management described, has not yet crossed into profit | `Q4 2023 Earnings Call, Apr 28, 2023` (promise) vs `earnings/03_margin-drivers.md` §7a (outcome, citing FY26 Annual Report Note 32) |
| CFO succession: Prateek Chandra (9-year CFO) to transition to Chief Strategy Officer; Jitin Diwan to become CFO from 15-Jun-2024 | Announced Q4 FY24 call, 30-Apr-2024 | Executed exactly as announced, effective 15-Jun-2024, confirmed in the FY25 and FY26 Annual Reports | **Kept** — a rare fully-checkable operational promise in this record, delivered on the stated date | `Q4 2024 Earnings Call, Apr 30, 2024` (promise) vs `FY26 Annual Report, Note 33(b)` (outcome) |

**Reading the record.** Three of the specific, dated targets management set on the core marketplace business (margin, supplier net-adds, revenue-growth aspiration) were missed, in two cases by a wide margin; one narrower target (10% collection growth) was approximately met; the Busy Infotech deal's own deliberately low bar ("don't lose money") is being approached but not yet cleared. The one fully mechanical promise in the record — a management-transition date — was delivered exactly as stated. This is not a "no checkable promises" situation; the record is thin on formal guidance (the company does not issue any) but rich in specific, dated qualitative targets from the CEO on quarterly calls, and those targets have mostly been missed on growth and margin, while transition-execution promises have been kept.

## 4A. Turnaround & Integrity Tests (CLAUDE.md §24, Filters 1 & 2)

| Test | Finding | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Is the thesis a turnaround (struggling business + new management / new plan)? | **No.** IndiaMART is a continuously profitable, founder-run company since 1999, with net cash of ~₹31bn and no debt [`business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance.md` §1]. The current supplier-churn and growth-deceleration issue is being managed by the SAME 27-year founder team using the same operating playbook (mix-shift pricing, selective spend pullback), not a new management team executing a turnaround plan. This is not a governance-turnaround thesis. | [CIQ Board Members.xls]; [`business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance.md`] |
| If yes: years of proven operating inflection actually delivered | Not applicable — no turnaround thesis in play | — |
| Résumé / plan / consultant deck cited as the reason to believe? | Not applicable — no such narrative found in the pool; the churn issue is discussed candidly as an ongoing operating problem, not sold via a turnaround pitch | — |
| Routed integrity "buzz" from `business-model/01_disqualifier-scan` | **Investigated, not escalated.** The disqualifier scan routed a "soft signal, not a lock": the FY26 Secretarial Audit Report records two Designated-Person compliance lapses under SEBI Insider Trading (PIT) regulations — one unnamed Designated Person pledged company shares without required Compliance-Officer pre-clearance, and another executed a one-share trade during a closed trading window; both were self-reported by the company to the exchanges [`business-model/01_disqualifier-scan.md`, citing FY26 Annual Report, Secretarial Audit Report Annexure-A]. Both individuals are unnamed in this pool, so full person-level resolution is not possible from the data available (that identity gap is itself recorded, not glossed over) — but the substance is a self-disclosed, immaterial procedural lapse (one-share trade; pledge magnitude not disclosed as material and self-reported proactively), not an external allegation or "buzz" about a named person's character. Because the company itself surfaced and reported these lapses to the exchanges (a candor signal, not concealment), and the matters are procedural rather than integrity-adverse in nature, this does **not** meet the bar for an unresolved adverse integrity signal under §24 Filter 1 — **RF-MGT-005 is NOT emitted**. It is recorded here as a minor compliance-control finding for the audit/assurance and regulatory-legal specialists (08, 12) to size further, consistent with the scan's own routing instruction. | [`business-model/01_disqualifier-scan.md`]; [FY26 Annual Report, Secretarial Audit Report, Annexure-A] |

**Turnaround rule.** Not triggered — see above; no base-rate penalty applies under this test.

**Integrity rule.** Chased as far as this pool allows; concluded non-adverse-integrity in nature and self-disclosed. If a future run's `07_people-integrity-dossiers` or `08_audit-and-assurance-quality` identifies the unnamed Designated Person(s) or finds a broader pattern, this read should be revisited.

## 5. Management Read

Leadership stability at the very top is real: both founders have run this company for 27 years, hold roughly 49% of it directly, and the one CFO change in a decade was announced in advance and executed on schedule — this is not a management team in flux. But the checkable promise record on the metrics that matter most (EBITDA margin, paying-supplier net-adds, revenue-growth aspiration) shows three material misses against specific, dated targets the CEO himself set on quarterly calls, against one target approximately met and one management-transition promise delivered exactly as stated — a mixed-to-weak record on delivery. The single biggest signal, on balance, is the pattern of specific misses followed by management withdrawing formal guidance rather than resetting a lower, achievable one — a real accountability gap sitting alongside genuinely candid, non-defensive real-time disclosure of the underlying problems (the CEO repeatedly told analysts he was "not confident" to give a number rather than papering over the churn issue).

## management-governance / 02_capital-allocation-scorecard.md

_Source: `02_capital-allocation-scorecard.md`_

# Capital-Allocation Scorecard — INDIAMART

**Jurisdiction / regime:** India, NSE (INDIAMART) / BSE (542726). Reporting standard: Ind AS. Reporting currency: **INR**, stated in ₹ million unless noted; fiscal year ends 31 March ("FY26" = year ended 31-Mar-2026). Deal sizes sourced from the Capital IQ M&A/Private-Placement transaction export are shown in **USD million ($mm)** as CIQ itself reports them — labelled wherever used, never silently converted. This scorecard covers the 5 fiscal years FY22–FY26 (multi-year history is available; no single-period cap applies per the Partial-Data Rule).

This report deepens and, where they overlap, supersedes `business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance.md` per `MODULE_RULES.md` — this module owns Governance Checklist items **A10-01** (dividend policy & consistency) and **A10-05** (buyback-vs-dividend conduct).

---

## 1. Uses of Capital (FY22–FY26)

| Use | Cumulative Amount | Currency | % of 5-yr CFO (₹27,547.4mn) | Source |
|---|---:|---|---:|---|
| Acquisitions — Busy Infotech control deal (the only "Cash Acquisitions" cash-flow line in the period) | 5,068.4 | ₹mn | 18.4% | [CIQ export, `Financials (1).xls`, Cash Flow tab, "Cash Acquisitions" row, FY23 column] |
| Minority/venture-stake investment book (balance-sheet growth in "Long-term Investments," a proxy for the ~19-deal minority portfolio — see §2) | 4,120.3 | ₹mn | 15.0% | [CIQ export, `Financials (1).xls`, Balance Sheet tab, "Long-term Investments" row: ₹4,209.32mn FY22 → ₹8,329.60mn FY26] |
| Buybacks (2 tender offers, FY23 + FY24) | 7,394.5 | ₹mn | 26.8% | [CIQ export, `Financials (1).xls`, Cash Flow tab, "Repurchase of Common Stock": −₹1,232.6mn FY23, −₹6,161.9mn FY24] |
| Dividends paid (cash basis) | 5,326.9 | ₹mn | 19.3% | [CIQ export, `Financials (1).xls`, Cash Flow tab, "Common Dividends Paid," FY22–FY26 sum: 455.1+61.0+611.5+1,198.9+3,000.4] |
| Capex | 511.6 | ₹mn | 1.9% | [CIQ export, `Financials (1).xls`, Cash Flow tab, "Capital Expenditure," FY22–FY26 sum] |
| Debt (lease-principal) repayment | 619.3 | ₹mn | 2.2% | [CIQ export, `Financials (1).xls`, Cash Flow tab, "Total Debt Repaid," FY22–FY26 sum — 100% lease-liability principal; the company carries **no bank borrowings** in any of the 5 years] |
| **Total mapped uses** | **23,041.0** | ₹mn | **83.6%** | Sum of rows above |

The unmapped ~16.4% of 5-year CFO (≈₹4,506mn) was retained and added to the treasury book (cash + short-term investments + trading securities grew from ₹23,773.4mn at FY22-end to ₹31,202.65mn at FY26-end — a strict-basis reconciliation of this residual against the treasury-book delta was not separately performed and is not claimed here). **No debt was issued in any of the 5 years** (Total Debt Issued = nil, FY22–FY26) [CIQ export, `Financials (1).xls`, Cash Flow tab] — every use of capital above was self-funded from operating cash flow.

**Cross-check against the company's own disclosed distribution table.** The FY26 Annual Report discloses its own "Amount distributed to shareholders" chart (dividends + buybacks combined, as % of CFO), on a cash-paid basis: FY22 ₹455mn (11%), FY23 ₹1,304mn (27%), FY24 ₹6,810mn (**122%** — exceeded that year's CFO), FY25 ₹1,200mn (19%), FY26 ₹3,000mn (43%); cumulative ≈₹13,000mn [FY26 Annual Report, "Capital Returned to Shareholders" section]. This reconciles closely with the CIQ dividend+buyback cash-flow lines used above (FY23: ₹61.0+1,232.6=₹1,293.6mn vs company's ₹1,304mn; FY24: ₹611.5+6,161.9=₹6,773.4mn vs company's ₹6,810mn — small residual gaps of ~1% are not resolved from this pool and are flagged, not silently closed). **FY24 is the one year total shareholder distributions (dividend + buyback) exceeded CFO (122%) and FCF** — funded from the net-cash balance sheet, not debt (see §3/§4).

## 2. M&A Scorecard

| Busy Infotech Pvt. Ltd. (control acquisition, accounting-software SaaS) | Announced 24-Jan-2022, closed 06-Apr-2022 | $66.93mm (≈₹500 crore / ₹5,068.4mn per the standalone cash-flow "Cash Acquisitions" line) | Enter the MSME accounting-software category; management's own bar on the FY23 call was explicitly modest — "don't lose money… over the next 2, 3 years as long as they are cash positive and continue to improve," with no specific margin/revenue target set | **Partially — improving, not yet profitable.** Segment (Busy Infotech + Livekeeping) revenue nearly doubled FY25→FY26 (₹681.91mn → ₹1,261.04mn, +84.9%) and segment loss narrowed from −17.47% margin (FY25, −₹119.16mn) to −2.23% margin (FY26, −₹28.15mn) — trending toward the stated "don't lose money" bar after ~4 years of ownership, not yet across it. Goodwill of ₹4,122.34mn sits on the balance sheet and is a named Key Audit Matter (impairment-test judgement) | [`earnings/03_margin-drivers.md` §6, citing FY26 Annual Report Note 32]; [`management-governance/01_management-and-track-record.md` §4, citing Q4 FY23 transcript]; [Transaction Summary export, IQTR1765317719] |

(accounting-software bolt-on) | 2023–2024 window | Not separately broken out in this pool ($mm not disclosed in the Transaction Summary export as a standalone control deal) | Bolt-on to the Busy Infotech accounting-software ecosystem, "Mobile first" Tally-adjacent product | **No — struck off.** Livekeeping Private Limited was struck off (dissolved) in October 2024 [`business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance.md` §1, cross-module]. Goodwill of ₹420.38mn tied to this deal remains on the consolidated balance sheet per the same source | [`business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance.md` §1, citing FY26 Annual Report Note 33] |
| Ten Times Online Pvt. Ltd. | Bought (nominal sum, prior to this window); wound down/divested for $0.03mm (2020-09-01) and again for $0.01mm (2023-03-16), both entries recording IndiaMART as **Seller** | Nominal (<$0.05mm both legs) | Not disclosed in this pool | **No — wound down** at a nominal residual value | [Transaction Summary export, IQTR686433187, IQTR1829815407] |
| ~19 minority/venture-style private-placement checks (Fleetx Technologies — fleet management; Mobisy, Simply Vyapar, Legistify, Zimyo — HR/legal/accounting SaaS; Agillos, Instant Procurement, Baldor — e-commerce/procurement; Mynd Solutions, IB Monotaro, Finlite, and others), FY16–FY26 | Ongoing, ~2–3 new/follow-on rounds per year | Individually small: single-digit to low-double-digit $mm each (e.g. Fleetx $6.78mm Jul-2026, Agillos $12.71mm Mar-2026, Baldor no size disclosed Feb-2026, Mobisy $12.02mm Dec-2024); cumulative estimate ~$250–300mm over 10 years [`business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance.md` §1 — cross-module estimate, labelled] | Stated rationale is broad and repeated across deals: "fostering a comprehensive and digitally integrated ecosystem, catering to the needs of small and medium businesses" [FY26 Annual Report, Strategic Investments section] — this is the kind of bare "strategic fit / ecosystem" language CLAUDE.md §24 Filter 4 says is not itself evidence of value creation | **Mixed / largely unproven.** No aggregate, disclosed return or synergy metric exists across the ~19-deal portfolio. FY26 Annual Report discloses only per-investment ownership % (e.g. Fleetx 22.2%, Simply Vyapar 28.6%, Mobisy 32.5% — all minority stakes), not returns. An ₹23.81mn impairment and a further ₹12.04mn diminution provision were recorded against these investments in FY2025 [`business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance.md` §1] | [FY26 Annual Report, Strategic Investments section]; [Transaction Summary M&A/Private Placements export, IndiaMART InterMESH Limited (NSEI:INDIAMART), 10-yr history] |

**Serial-acquirer + opportunity-cost test (CLAUDE.md §24, Filter 4).** IndiaMART made roughly 27 M&A/private-placement transactions in the CIQ 10-year export — a genuine serial pattern by transaction count. Against that, three mitigating facts hold: (1) **zero debt was drawn** for any of it (Total Debt Issued = nil, FY22–FY26); (2) individual deal sizes are small — only Busy Infotech ($66.93mm) is a control acquisition, and it is roughly 4–5% of the company's own market equity value (₹107,319.4mn / ~$1.3bn as of 11-Aug-2026 [`business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance.md` §3]), well below "near or above own market value"; (3) the flagship control deal (Busy Infotech) is tracked with real, disclosed, improving segment financials, not a black box. Against that, the **opportunity cost is real and understated by looking at returns alone**: management attention and roughly ₹4.1bn of balance-sheet capital (the Long-term Investments growth, §1) are spread across ~19 small, largely unrelated categories (fleet management, HR SaaS, legal tech, e-commerce reconciliation, procurement) with no disclosed aggregate return, one struck-off subsidiary (Livekeeping), one nominal divestiture (Ten Times Online), and impairment/diminution provisions already booked in FY2025. This is a genuine, recurring drag on capital-allocation quality — but it does **not** clear the materiality bar (debt-funded, near/above own value) that would trigger the RF-CAP-004 hard cap (Capital allocation max 50). It is scored as a real, ongoing penalty to the M&A-discipline component below, not as a disqualifier.

## 3. Buyback Scorecard

Intrinsic-value discipline not assessable from this module (valuation has not run). Judgment below rests on observable evidence only: net share-count change, price vs par/book, tender-offer premium structure, promoter participation, and leverage at the time.

| Year | Amount Spent | Currency | Shares Bought | Implied Avg Price | Share-Count Change | Offset SBC Dilution? | Price-Discipline Evidence | Judgment | Source |
|---|---:|---|---:|---:|---:|---|---|---|---|
| FY23 (announced Apr-2022, closed Jun–Sep 2022) | 1,232.6 (incl. buyback tax + expenses); ₹1,000mn gross tender size ("₹100 crore") | ₹mn | ~160,000 (estimated: ₹1,000mn gross ÷ ₹6,250/share — **estimate, labelled**; not independently confirmed against the FY23 Annual Report's own buyback note, which is not in this pool) | ₹6,250/share (fixed tender price, disclosed) | Total shares outstanding on balance-sheet date: 61.129mn (FY22) → 61.229mn (FY23, still net UP that year due to ESOP issuance exceeding the small buyback) | No — FY23 shares outstanding rose slightly despite the buyback | Fixed-price tender offer (India's standard buyback mechanism), price set at a premium to induce tendering — not an opportunistic "buy the dip" purchase; no leverage was used (net cash throughout); CFO stated on the Q4 FY22 call that "promoters' participation would be to the extent of their entitlement… pro rata to the shareholding" — i.e. promoters received **no preferential price or allocation** | **Fair conduct, small scale.** All-shareholder pro-rata tender at a disclosed fixed price; did not net-reduce share count this year because ESOP issuance offset it | [Q4 FY22 transcript, 29-Apr-2022, prepared remarks + Q&A]; [CIQ export, `Financials (1).xls`, Cash Flow + Balance Sheet tabs] |
| FY24 (Board approval + execution, year ended 31-Mar-2024) | 6,161.89 (incl. tax on buyback of ₹1,161.89mn + expenses ₹36.95mn; ₹5,000mn gross tender size, "not exceeding INR 5,000mn, 2.04% of paid-up capital") | ₹mn | 1,250,000 (disclosed exactly: "bought back 12,50,000 equity shares… and extinguished the equity shares") | ₹4,000/share (fixed tender price, disclosed) | Total shares outstanding on balance-sheet date: 61.229mn (FY23) → 59.949mn (FY24) — a **net fall of ~1.28mn shares (−2.1%)** | No — this year's reduction more than offset the year's ESOP issuance (net fall confirms the buyback dominated) | Fixed-price tender offer, funded "from its free reserves including Securities Premium" per Section 68 of the Companies Act 2013 — no debt; CRR (capital redemption reserve) created for exactly ₹12.50mn = 1,250,000 shares × ₹10 par, reconciling to the disclosed share count | **Fair conduct, real net reduction.** The only year in this record where the buyback demonstrably reduced net share count | [FY25 Annual Report standalone notes, Note 12(2) buyback note, verbatim: "the Board of Directors approved a proposal to buy-back upto 12,50,000 equity shares… at INR 4,000 per equity share… funded the buyback from its free reserves"]; [CIQ export, Balance Sheet tab, Total Shares Out.] |
| FY25 / FY26 | Nil | — | 0 | — | Total shares outstanding rose: 59.949mn (FY24) → 60.005mn (FY25) → 60.083mn (FY26) — entirely ESOP-driven | N/A — no buyback to offset dilution against; ESOP issuance alone drove the ~0.22% rise over 2 years | No buyback executed despite continued strong FCF (₹6,153.5mn FY25, ₹6,872.2mn FY26) and a growing net-cash pile (~₹31.2bn at FY26-end) | **No capital return via buyback for 2 straight years while cash kept building** — not a discipline failure per se (dividends stepped up instead, see §4), but a break in what management called an annual "policy of distributing return to the shareholders" reviewed "every year" | [CIQ export, Balance Sheet + Cash Flow tabs] |

**Buyback price vs earnings — a crude but citable cross-check.** The FY23 tender (₹6,250/share, Apr-2022) priced against FY22's own diluted EPS of ₹48.42 implies a ~129x trailing P/E; the FY24 tender (₹4,000/share) against FY23's diluted EPS of ₹46.32 implies a ~86x trailing P/E [`earnings/01_historical-financials.md` §1]. Both are very rich multiples by any conventional standard — but Indian tender-offer buybacks are structurally a **fixed-price, all-shareholder capital-return mechanism set at a premium to induce tendering**, not an opportunistic low-price purchase the way a US open-market buyback can be; judging these prices against "cheap vs a defensible value" is therefore not meaningful without the valuation module's own fair-value range (not yet run). No RF-CAP-002 trigger: over the full FY22–FY26 window, total shares outstanding fell from 61.129mn to 60.083mn (a net ~1.05mn-share, ~1.7% reduction), i.e. the two buybacks were **not** fully offset by ESOP dilution on a cumulative basis, even though FY23 alone and FY25–FY26 alone were dilution-dominated.

## 4. Dividends & Organic Reinvestment

| Metric | Value | Source |
|---|---:|---|
| Payout ratio (FY26, dividend declared ÷ net income) | ~76% (₹60/share declared for FY26 [final ₹30 + special ₹30] ÷ FY26 net income ₹4,746.82mn, ≈₹3,600mn ÷ ₹4,746.82mn) | [`business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance.md` §1, citing FY26 Annual Report + AGM Notice]; [CIQ export Cash Flow tab, Net Income FY26] |
| Payout ratio (FY26, dividend declared ÷ FCF) | ~52% (₹3,600mn ÷ ₹6,872.19mn FY26 FCF) | Calculated from [`earnings/01_historical-financials.md` §1] and dividend figure above |
| Dividend coverage — dividend PAID (cash basis) ÷ FCF, each year | FY22: 11.4% · FY23: 1.3% · FY24: 11.2% · FY25: 19.5% · FY26: 43.7% | Calculated: [CIQ export Cash Flow tab, "Common Dividends Paid"] ÷ [`earnings/01_historical-financials.md` §1, FCF row] |
| Documented dividend policy exists? Followed for ≥4 years? (A10-01) | **Yes / Yes.** "In line with the Company's Dividend Distribution Policy and financial performance, the Company has maintained a consistent record of dividend payments over the years" [FY26 Annual Report, Dividend section]. A "Dividend Distribution Policy" is listed as document #9 in the company's own governance-policy index. Dividends paid every year FY22–FY26 (5 consecutive years), plus a special dividend layered on top in FY25 and FY26 | [FY26 Annual Report, Dividend section + governance policy index] |
| Incremental ROIC (Δ NOPAT / Δ invested capital) | See derivation below — **volatile, and negative in the most recent year (FY26: ≈ −5.1%)** | Calculated (methodology below) |

**Incremental ROIC derivation (Inference — approximate, labelled).** Because IndiaMART sits on a treasury book (cash + short-term investments + trading securities) larger than its total capital, a textbook "equity + net debt" invested-capital base is negative and not meaningful. Instead, **operating invested capital** is built as Net PP&E + Goodwill + Other Intangibles + Long-term Investments (the M&A/venture book) — i.e. capital genuinely committed to the operating and investing franchise, excluding the treasury book [CIQ export, Balance Sheet tab]. NOPAT = EBIT × (1 − effective tax rate); the effective tax rate is the company's own disclosed figure for FY24 (23.69%), FY25 (21.44%) and FY26 (24.38%) [FY25 & FY26 Annual Reports, Income Tax notes]; FY22/FY23 use an assumed 25% (not disclosed in this pool — **flagged as an estimate**).

```
Operating invested capital (₹mn): FY22 4,771.7 | FY23 10,649.9 | FY24 10,601.4 | FY25 11,818.9 | FY26 13,269.9
NOPAT (₹mn, approx.):             FY22 2,219.3 | FY23 1,806.6  | FY24 2,250.1  | FY25 3,866.7  | FY26 3,793.0

Incremental ROIC FY23 vs FY22: ΔNOPAT −412.7 / ΔIC +5,878.1  = −7.0%  (Busy Infotech goodwill/investment added, NOPAT fell)
Incremental ROIC FY24 vs FY23: ΔIC ≈ −48.4 (roughly flat)     = not meaningful (near-zero denominator)
Incremental ROIC FY25 vs FY24: ΔNOPAT +1,616.6 / ΔIC +1,217.5 = +132.8% (operating-leverage snapback, not capital-driven)
Incremental ROIC FY26 vs FY25: ΔNOPAT −73.7 / ΔIC +1,451.0    = −5.1%  (NEGATIVE — capital kept growing into the
                                                                        long-term-investment book while operating NOPAT fell)
```

**Reading it.** The FY26 print is the one that matters most for the "is capital still creating per-share value" question: operating invested capital grew by ₹1,451mn (mostly the venture-investment book, §2) while NOPAT on the operating base actually *fell*, producing a negative incremental return. The FY25 figure (+132.8%) is not a capital-allocation signal at all — it reflects the FY24→FY25 EBITDA-margin snap-back (cost discipline / operating leverage, per `earnings/01_historical-financials.md` §6), not returns on newly deployed capital. Company-reported absolute "Return on Capital %" (a CIQ ratio using the full, treasury-inflated capital base) ran 10.3% (FY22) → 7.5% (FY23) → 9.5% (FY24) → 15.4% (FY25) → 13.5% (FY26) [CIQ export, Ratios tab] — directionally consistent (a dip, then a snap-back, then a pullback) but understated in absolute magnitude by the huge treasury-cash denominator, so it is shown only as a cross-check, not the primary read.

**Reinvestment / capex.** Capex is trivial and falling (₹44.2mn FY22 → ₹70.0mn FY26, well below D&A every year) — consistent with an asset-light marketplace model, not with under-investment signalling distress [`business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance.md` §1]. The real "growth reinvestment" line for this business is the M&A/venture-investment book scored in §2, not PP&E capex.

---

## 4A. Distribution Conduct (A10-05)

| Test | Finding | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Mechanism rationale stated (dividend vs buyback, who benefits)? | **Stated in general terms, not deal-by-deal.** Management describes a standing "policy of distributing return to the shareholders" that the Board revisits annually — buybacks and dividends are both used, sized by that year's cash generation ("depending upon the cash flow generation and the cash requirements as to the Board will visit this every year") — but no filing in this pool sets out a specific decision rule for choosing buyback over dividend in a given year | [Q4 FY22 transcript, 29-Apr-2022, CFO Prateek Chandra] |
| Promoter participation in any buyback (tender/open-market) | **Pro rata only — no preferential treatment.** CFO, on the record, in response to a direct analyst question: "promoters' participation too… would be to the extent of their entitlement, that would be pro rata to the shareholding to the buyback issue size" — i.e. promoters tendered (or could tender) only in proportion to their existing stake, capturing no price or allocation advantage over minority holders | [Q4 FY22 transcript, 29-Apr-2022, Q&A] |
| Buyback timing vs promoter pledge / stake changes | **No adverse pattern found.** No promoter-level share pledge is disclosed in this pool at any point in the FY22–FY26 window [`management-governance/01_management-and-track-record.md` §4A, cross-module]; the only pledge-related item on record is an unrelated FY26 Secretarial Audit finding — an unnamed Designated Person pledged shares without required Compliance-Officer pre-clearance, self-reported to the exchanges — which is unconnected to either buyback and post-dates both by 2+ years | [`business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance.md` §1, citing FY26 Annual Report Secretarial Audit Report] |

**A10-05 verdict: Green.** Both tender-offer buybacks were priced and allocated on all-shareholder economics (fixed disclosed price, pro-rata participation for every tendering shareholder including promoters), funded entirely from free reserves with no debt, and there is no evidence of promoter-favourable timing, pricing, or pledge interaction. No RF-CAP red flag triggered on distribution conduct.

---

## 5. Capital-Allocation Read

Capital allocation at IndiaMART is **mixed**: disciplined and genuinely shareholder-fair on the distribution side, diffuse and largely unproven on the deployment side. The best use of capital has been the combination of near-zero capex (asset-light model, capex consistently below D&A) with disciplined, all-shareholder-economics buybacks (two fixed-price tender offers, promoters capped at pro-rata participation, funded from reserves with zero debt) and a documented, consistently-paid, well-covered dividend (never above 44% of FCF on a cash-paid basis in any single year, and only exceeding CFO in the one year — FY24 — that combined a large tender buyback with the prior year's dividend, funded from the net-cash balance sheet, not debt). The worst use of capital is the recurring pattern of small minority-stake venture investments (~19 deals across fleet management, HR SaaS, legal tech, e-commerce and procurement software, an estimated $250–300mm cumulative over a decade) justified with bare "ecosystem" language and carrying no disclosed aggregate return — a pattern that has already produced one struck-off subsidiary (Livekeeping), one nominally-divested position (Ten Times Online), and FY2025 impairment/diminution charges, set against one moderately-sized control deal (Busy Infotech) that is improving but still loss-making after four years. The single biggest capital-allocation signal is the **FY26 incremental ROIC turning negative** (≈−5.1% on an operating-capital base that excludes the treasury book) — for the first time in this five-year window, capital kept flowing into the investment book while the operating return on that growing capital base fell, which is exactly the "growth for its own sake" pattern this scorecard exists to catch, even though it has not yet reached the scale (debt-funded, near/above own market value) that would trigger a hard serial-acquirer cap under CLAUDE.md §24 Filter 4. Conclusion: **mixed** — not value-destructive (the balance sheet, dividend and buyback conduct are genuinely clean), but not yet "disciplined" either, given the diffuse venture-investment program and the FY26 return signal.

## Capital Allocation Score

| Component | Score | Max Score | Evidence |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| Organic reinvestment returns | 14 | 25 | Incremental ROIC volatile across the window and negative in FY26 (≈−5.1%, 02-013); FY25's +132.8% reflects opex-leverage snapback, not capital-driven returns; capex trivial and asset-light (not a penalty on its own), but the operating-capital base (PP&E+goodwill+intangibles+long-term investments) is not yet generating a proportionate return |
| M&A discipline | 9 | 20 | One control deal (Busy Infotech) improving but not yet profitable after 4 years (02-002); one bolt-on struck off (Livekeeping, 02-003); ~19 minority venture deals with no aggregate disclosed return, "ecosystem" rationale flagged as bare per §24 Filter 4 (02-004); serial pattern does not clear the RF-CAP-004 hard-cap threshold (02-005) but is charged its real opportunity cost here |
| Buyback discipline | 13 | 20 | Both executed buybacks were fair, disclosed, pro-rata, all-shareholder-economics tender offers funded from reserves with no debt (02-006, 02-007); net share count fell over the full 5-year window (02-008); but capital return via buyback has been inconsistent — none in FY25 or FY26 despite growing cash (02-009) |
| Dividend sustainability | 13 | 15 | Documented policy, 5 consecutive years paid, dividend-only payout never exceeded 44% of FCF in any year (02-010); FY26 payout stepped up to ~76% of net income / ~52% of FCF, still comfortably covered (02-011); the one year total distribution exceeded CFO (FY24) was buyback-driven, not dividend-driven, and was cash-funded not debt-funded (02-012) |
| Balance-sheet prudence | 10 | 10 | Zero bank borrowings in any of the 5 years; only lease liabilities on the debt line; net cash grew every year to ~₹31.2bn at FY26-end; all capital-allocation activity (M&A, buybacks, dividends) was self-funded from operating cash flow |
| Per-share value creation | 6 | 10 | Diluted EPS grew ₹48.42→₹78.77 (FY22-FY26) but a material share of that is lumpy treasury-investment gains, not operating earnings (`earnings/01_historical-financials.md` §3); book value/share grew ₹306.57→₹399.51 (+30% over 4 years, ~6.8%/yr); revenue growth decelerated sharply over the same window (30.8%→13.0%) with 3 of management's own stated growth/margin targets missed (`01_management-and-track-record.md` §4) |
| **Total** | **65** | **100** | Mixed-to-strong band: clean, fair, debt-free distribution mechanics offset by a diffuse, largely unproven deployment pattern and a weakening organic-return signal in the latest year |

No partial-data cap applies — 5 years of cash-flow, balance-sheet, and primary-filing evidence (annual reports, transcripts, the M&A transaction export) support this scorecard. The single labelled estimate (FY23 buyback implied share count, and the FY22/FY23 effective-tax-rate assumption in the incremental-ROIC calc) is flagged inline where used, per the Partial-Data Rule.
