## management-governance / 03_incentives-and-compensation.md

_Source: `03_incentives-and-compensation.md`_

# Incentives & Compensation — INDIAMART

## 1. Compensation Structure

Reporting currency: Indian Rupees (₹). Period: FY2026 (year ended 31 March 2026), Ind AS, standalone company (IndiaMART InterMESH Limited). Multi-year context (FY2022–FY2025) is drawn from a Capital IQ compensation export and cross-checked against the FY2026 Annexure-4 disclosure, which reconciles well (CIQ's FY2025 CEO total of ₹7.49cr + the annexure's disclosed 11.17% FY2026 increase ≈ ₹8.33cr, matching the annexure's stated figure).

IndiaMART is founder-run: Dinesh Chandra Agarwal (Managing Director & CEO) and Brijesh Kumar Agrawal (Whole-Time Director) are co-founders who together hold 29.09% and 19.99% of shares outstanding respectively (combined ~49.1%, market value ~₹5,266cr as of 31-Mar-2026) [Capital IQ Public Ownership Summary, IndiaMART InterMESH Limited, position date 31-Mar-2026]. Their comp-plan pay (₹14.5cr combined in FY2026) is roughly 0.3% of the value of their existing shareholding — the dominant alignment mechanism for these two executives is pre-existing ownership, not this year's pay packet. This module's job is the design of the pay plan itself; ownership and insider dealing are covered in `04_ownership-and-insider-behavior`.

| Executive | Base / Fixed | Annual Bonus (PLVC) | Long-Term Incentive | Cash vs Equity Mix | Source |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|---|
| Mr. Dinesh Chandra Agarwal, Managing Director & CEO | ₹5,96,12,268 | ₹2,37,07,856 (28.5% of total) | None disclosed — holds no reported ESOP/SAR grant | 100% cash (71.5% fixed / 28.5% variable); 0% equity component in FY2026 | [FY26 Integrated Annual Report, Annexure-4 "Remuneration of Directors"] |
| Mr. Brijesh Kumar Agrawal, Whole-Time Director | ₹4,34,03,016 | ₹1,83,29,208 (29.7% of total) | None disclosed | 100% cash (70.3% fixed / 29.7% variable); 0% equity | [FY26 Integrated Annual Report, Annexure-4] |
| Mr. Manoj Bhargava, Whole-Time Director, Group General Counsel & Company Secretary | ₹1,31,54,362 | ₹33,09,278 (20.1% of total, excl. ESOP) | Holds ESOP 2018 options: 5,770 outstanding at FY26-end; exercised ₹32,97,058 of ESOP/SAR perquisite value in FY26 | Cash 100% of the ₹1.65cr reported total (fixed 79.9% / variable 20.1%) plus a separately-disclosed ESOP perquisite | [FY26 Integrated Annual Report, Annexure-4 and "Stock Options held by Executive Director"] |
| Mr. Jitin Diwan, Chief Financial Officer (KMP, not a director) | Fixed/PLVC split not separately disclosed | Not separately disclosed | Not separately disclosed | Total remuneration ₹1,78,83,763 (+24.85% YoY, the sharpest KMP increase in FY2026); ratio to median employee pay 35.39× | [FY26 Integrated Annual Report, Annexure-4, Section 197(12)/Rule 5(1) table] |

Non-Executive/Independent Directors are paid sitting fees only (₹1 lakh cap per Board/Committee meeting under the Nomination & Remuneration Policy) — no commission was paid in FY2026, and Independent Directors are explicitly barred from stock options ("An Independent Director shall not be entitled to any stock option of the Company") [FY26 Integrated Annual Report, "Criteria of making Payment to Non-Executive Directors"]. One Non-Executive Director, Mr. Dhruv Prakash, separately received ₹47,06,250 (sitting fees ₹17,00,000 + ₹30,06,250) under a shareholder-approved Reg 17(6) professional-services agreement (executive coaching, senior-management assessment, business-strategy advice) [FY26 Integrated Annual Report, "Pecuniary Relationship of Non-Executive Directors" and sitting-fee table]; the person dossier (07) grades him Clean with a professional background (ex-Intel Capital, ex-Korn Ferry) and no family linkage found [07_people-integrity-dossiers.md, row 20].

The annual bonus (Performance Linked Variable Compensation, "PLVC") is described only as "based on their performance measured by their Balance Score Card for the previous financial year as recommended by the Nomination and Remuneration Committee and approved by the Board" [FY26 Integrated Annual Report, "Remuneration to Executive Director"]. No table of Balanced-Scorecard metrics or weights is published anywhere in the annual report reviewed.

## 2. Performance Metrics

| Plan | Metric | Weight | Per-Share/Returns or Size-Based? | Source |
|---|---|---:|---|---|
| Annual bonus (PLVC) — CEO, WTDs | "Balance Score Card," metrics and weights not disclosed | Not disclosed | **Cannot be classified from filings** — not proven from available data whether the scorecard weights ROIC/EPS/FCF-per-share vs revenue/paying-supplier/collection growth | [FY26 Integrated Annual Report, "Remuneration to Executive Director"] |
| Long-term incentive — CEO & WTD (Dinesh Agarwal, Brijesh Agrawal) | None granted | N/A | N/A — no LTI exists for the two controlling founder-executives in the period reviewed | [FY26 Integrated Annual Report, Annexure-4; "Stock Options held by Executive Director" table lists only Mr. Bhargava] |
| Long-term incentive — other KMP/employees (ESOP 2018, ESOP 2022, SAR) | Time-based vesting only ("vest upon completion of upto 72 months of service from the grant date… no individual has a contractual right to participate") | N/A (service-vested, not performance-conditioned) | **Size/tenure-based, not returns-based** — vesting is service-time, not tied to any ROIC/EPS/TSR performance condition | [FY26 Integrated Annual Report, Note 28 "Share based payment plans"] |

Because the Balanced Scorecard's metrics are not published, this agent cannot confirm — and does not assume — that the annual bonus rewards per-share value creation. The one thing that IS confirmed from the filing is that the long-term incentive plan (ESOP/SAR) vests on time served, not on any performance hurdle, and that the two largest pay packets (CEO, senior WTD) carry no equity component at all in FY2026. Company-disclosed KPIs on earnings calls and in the MD&A (paying-supplier count, collections, billing, revenue) are volume/size metrics; whether any of these — rather than ROIC, EPS, or FCF/share — feed the PLVC scorecard is not proven from available data.

## 3. Pay vs Performance

| Signal | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| CEO total pay (latest, FY2026) | ₹8,33,20,124 (~₹8.33cr), +11.17% YoY; ratio to median employee remuneration 164.86× | [FY26 Integrated Annual Report, Annexure-4] |
| CEO pay vs one listed internet-platform peer | Web-sourced (unverified), single-peer comparison, dated 2026-08-22: Info Edge (India) Ltd. CEO Hitesh Oberoi's disclosed total compensation is cited at ~₹26.93mn for a comparable recent period, against IndiaMART's ~₹74.95mn for the same aggregator's comparable period — roughly 2.8× — and the same source states IndiaMART's CEO compensation is "252% higher than the average for the industry" (≈3.5× an unnamed industry-average basis). This is a single third-party aggregator's figure, not a filing-sourced multi-peer benchmarking study; treat directionally, not as a precise multiple | [Web: simplywall.st, "IndiaMART InterMESH" and "Info Edge (India)" management pages, accessed 2026-08-22 (unverified, n=1 peer + one unnamed industry average)] |
| Profit trend vs pay trend — one-year view | Consolidated PAT fell 14% YoY in FY2026 (₹4,747mn vs ₹5,507mn FY2025; standalone PAT ₹5,251.81mn vs ₹6,072.24mn, also -14%), while CEO cash pay rose 11.17% and the WTD's pay rose 13.25% in the same year | [FY26 Integrated Annual Report, Annexure-4 (pay); Note "Net Profit… (14%)" MD&A summary and Note 12451 EPS computation (PAT)] |
| Profit trend vs pay trend — three-year view (the number that disagrees) | Over FY2023→FY2026, CEO total pay compounded at ~12.3%/yr (₹58.93mn → ₹83.32mn) while consolidated PAT compounded faster, at ~18.7%/yr (₹2,838.27mn → ₹4,746.82mn). On this three-year lens, CEO pay growth has **not** outrun PAT growth — the opposite of the one-year read. Both figures are shown because they disagree: the one-year divergence (pay up, profit down) is real and worth watching for a second consecutive year, but it is not yet a multi-year pattern | [Capital IQ Compensation Summary Compensation.xls, FY22–FY25 columns; Capital IQ Income Statement, FY22–FY26 "Net Income to Company"; FY26 Integrated Annual Report, Annexure-4] |
| Median employee pay trend | Company discloses median remuneration of employees **fell 2.17%** in FY2026, attributed to "increase in number of employees engaged by the Company at various levels" (i.e., hiring diluted the median) | [FY26 Integrated Annual Report, Annexure-4, Section B] |
| Weighted-average employee increase vs managerial increase | Weighted-average salary increase for employees other than managerial personnel was 9.7% in FY2026, vs 8.3% for managerial personnel — the broader workforce's raise outpaced the managerial-personnel average, which is a mitigating, minority-friendly data point sitting alongside the falling-median finding above | [FY26 Integrated Annual Report, Annexure-4, Section D] |
| Say-on-pay support (%) | **Not found in the reviewed pool.** The FY2024 AGM (June 2024) approved revised remuneration for the MD/CEO and WTD by ordinary/special resolution, but the pool's Jun-02-2026 Annual Report (which covers FY2026) does not carry that AGM's scrutinizer/voting-percentage table, and no other filing in the pool discloses the votes-against on any remuneration resolution | [FY26 Integrated Annual Report, "General Body Meetings" table — lists resolution subject only, not vote percentages] — Insufficient Data |

## 3B. Human Capital (A12-01 … A12-04)

| Test | Raw value | Band applied | Verdict | Source |

| Attrition vs industry median | Turnover rate (BRSR, permanent employees, excludes <6-month joiners): Sales & Servicing — 34% FY2026 (vs 29% FY2025, 27% FY2024); Others (product/tech/corporate) — 20% FY2026 (vs 17% FY2025, 16% FY2024). Both segments rose for a third consecutive year. No industry-median figure is disclosed in the pool for a direct comparison (Indian tele-sales/BPO-style sales-force attrition commonly runs 25–35%, so the 34% Sales & Servicing figure is not clearly outside the sector range on that unsourced general knowledge alone) | Amber band: not clearly ">1.5× industry" on the evidence available, but the three-year rising trend across BOTH segments is the "spiking" condition in the same test | **Amber** | [FY26 Integrated Annual Report, BRSR Section, "Turnover rate for permanent employees and workers"] |
| Median-employee pay level & change vs executive change | Median employee remuneration fell 2.17% in FY2026 while CEO pay rose 11.17%, WTD-1 pay rose 13.25%, and CFO pay rose 24.85% in the same year — the divergence is explicit in the filing's own language ("median remuneration… has reduced") | Red band: "Median pay falling while executive pay rises (the divergence itself is the flag)" | **Red** | [FY26 Integrated Annual Report, Annexure-4, Sections A and B] |
| ESOP pool breadth (broad-based vs top-concentrated); promoters excluded? | FY2026 SAR grant of 4,06,730 units went "to the eligible employees of the Company including KMP's and of it's subsidiaries" — broad-based, not limited to a handful of executives. The two controlling founder-executives (CEO, senior WTD) hold and were granted NO ESOP/SAR in the period reviewed — consistent with promoter exclusion from cheap insider equity (A3-05). Total overhang (SAR 4,27,751 + ESOP2018 5,770 outstanding, plus a newly authorised 5,50,000-share pool for a new scheme) ≈ 1.6% of the 6,00,87,148 shares outstanding; FY2026 burn (77,586 shares issued on exercise) ≈ 0.13% of shares outstanding | Green band: broad-based, burn <2%/yr, overhang well under 10%, promoters excluded, fully fair-value expensed (₹347.36mn standalone cost recognised through P&L in FY2026) | **Green** | [FY26 Integrated Annual Report, Note 28 "Share based payment plans"; Note 15 "Share Capital"] |
| Workplace-conduct record | 2 Sexual Harassment (POSH) complaints filed in FY2026 (0 in FY2025), representing 0.19% of female employees; both upheld; 0 pending resolution at year-end. No discrimination, forced-labour, or child-labour complaints reported | Green band: disclosed and resolved — small, disclosed count with zero pending at year-end | **Green** | [FY26 Integrated Annual Report, BRSR Section, "Complaints filed under… POSH… 2013"] |

## 4. Incentive Alignment Read

The formal comp plan is cash-heavy, with the annual bonus tied to an undisclosed "Balanced Scorecard" (weights never published — not proven from available data whether it rewards ROIC/EPS/FCF-per-share or revenue/volume), and the only long-term incentive that exists at all vests purely on time served, never on a return metric — while the CEO and the senior Whole-Time Director, the two people running the company, received zero equity compensation in FY2026. What the plan actually pays for cannot be confirmed as per-share value creation because the metric behind roughly 20-30% of cash pay is not disclosed; what it does NOT do is reward size for its own sake either (no revenue/deal-count/headcount metric is named, and dilution/burn are low). The real alignment mechanism at this company is the founders' pre-existing ~49% combined ownership stake (~₹5,266cr), which dwarfs their annual pay by roughly 350×, not the incentive-plan design this agent evaluated. Set against that, FY2026 shows a genuine, filing-documented divergence a minority holder should watch: median employee pay fell 2.17% while every executive's pay rose (CFO +24.85%), and CEO/WTD pay rose in a year consolidated profit fell 14% — a one-year signal, not yet a confirmed multi-year pattern, since the 3-year CEO-pay CAGR (~12.3%) has run behind the 3-year PAT CAGR (~18.7%). **Conclusion: mixed** — not misaligned (no size-based metric identified, dilution modest, statutory caps cleared with wide headroom, no golden parachute, no problematic practice found), but not well-aligned either (metric opacity on the bonus, no performance condition on the LTI, no clawback policy, above-peer CEO pay by a web-sourced ~2.8-3.5× on one comparable, and the median-pay-vs-executive-pay divergence in the latest year).

## Incentive Alignment Score

| Component | Score | Max Score | Evidence |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| Returns / per-share metrics | 7 | 25 | Bonus metric ("Balance Score Card") weights never disclosed (03-005); LTI is purely time-vested, never performance-conditioned (03-006) — no confirmed link to ROIC/EPS/FCF-per-share anywhere in the plan |
| Metric clarity and weights | 6 | 20 | A named, NRC-recommended/Board-approved framework exists (process disclosed), but the substantive KPIs and weights are never published (03-005) |
| Pay-for-performance alignment | 11 | 20 | ~28-30% of cash pay is variable and framework-linked for the top two executives (03-001/002); aggregate pay is a modest 3.08% of net profit (03-008); but FY2026's one-year profit-down/pay-up divergence (03-011 vs the FY2026 raw figures in §3) is a real, if not yet multi-year, negative |
| Long-term orientation | 8 | 15 | No equity LTI at all for the CEO or senior WTD (03-007); other KMP/employee ESOP-SAR vests over up to 6 years but on service time only, not a return metric (03-006); founders' de facto long-term alignment runs through pre-existing ~49% ownership, outside this plan's design |
| Pay magnitude vs peers/performance | 3 | 10 | CEO pay is ~2.8-3.5× a single web-sourced comparable Indian internet-platform CEO and above an unnamed "industry average" per that same source (03-010, low confidence, n=1) |
| Minority-holder alignment | 6 | 10 | Statutory caps cleared with wide headroom (03-008/009); no golden parachute (03-013); no problematic practice found (03-017); broad-based, low-dilution, fully-expensed ESOP (03-021, 03-023); offset by no clawback policy (03-014) and the median-pay-vs-executive-pay divergence (03-020) |
| **Total** | **41** | **100** | Mixed band (41-60 threshold; sits at the low end) — see §4 for the narrative read |

No proxy/comp-disclosure gap applies here: a full India-regime remuneration disclosure (Board's Report Annexure-4 under Sec 197(12)/Rule 5) was available and used, so the "no proxy" partial-data cap in `MODULE_RULES.md` does NOT apply to this run.

## management-governance / 04_ownership-and-insider-behavior.md

_Source: `04_ownership-and-insider-behavior.md`_

# Ownership & Insider Behavior — INDIAMART

## 1. Ownership

IndiaMART InterMESH Limited (NSEI:INDIAMART) has ONE class of equity shares (face value ₹10 each, one vote per share) — no dual-class or super-voting structure [FY26 Annual Report (Jun-02-2026), Note 12(a) — "The Company has only one class of equity shares... Each holder of equity is entitled to one vote per share"].

| Holder | % Held | Share Class | Bought or Granted? | Source |
|---|---:|---|---|---|
| CEO / founder — Dinesh Chandra Agarwal | 28.01% (16,827,523 sh) | Common (single class) | Founding equity — co-founded the company in 1999, holding since incorporation; no promoter ESOP/SAR grants or preferential allotments found in the pool | [FY26 Annual Report, Note 12(b) — Shareholding of promoters, as at 31-Mar-2026] |
| Co-founder / WTD — Brijesh Kumar Agrawal | 18.98% (11,403,046 sh) | Common (single class) | Founding equity — co-founded 1999, held since incorporation; same profile as above | [FY26 Annual Report, Note 12(b)] |
| Rest of Promoter Group (16 named individuals/HUFs/family trusts: Chetna Agarwal, Pankaj Agarwal, Anand Kumar Agrawal, Meena Agrawal, Dinesh Chandra Agarwal HUF, Naresh Chandra Agrawal, Prakash Chandra Agrawal, Gunjan Agarwal, Vijay Jalan, Naresh/Anand/Prakash HUFs, Hamirwasia Business/Family Trusts, Nanpara Business/Family Trusts) | 2.12% combined | Common (single class) | Family shareholding, largely static year-on-year (no % change in 12 of 16 lines) | [FY26 Annual Report, Note 12(b)] |
| **Total Promoter & Promoter Group (18 holders)** | **49.11%** (29,514,888 sh) | Common (single class) | See above | [FY26 Annual Report, Corporate Governance Report, "A. PROMOTERS HOLDINGS"; Note 12(b)] |
| Largest institutional/PE holder — Nalanda Capital Pte Ltd | 7.71% (4,636,599 sh) | Common (single class) | Purchased/accumulated in the market (PE/VC-firm-style holding, listed as a "VC/PE Firms >5% stake" category) | [CIQ Public Ownership Summary, Top Holders, position date 21-Jul-2026] |
| Largest domestic mutual-fund holder — ICICI Prudential Asset Management Co. Ltd | 6.47% (3,891,506 sh) | Common (single class) | Purchased in the market (mutual-fund holding) | [CIQ Public Ownership Summary, Top Holders, position date 31-Mar-2026] |
| UTI Asset Management Company Limited | 3.48% (2,091,031 sh) | Common (single class) | Purchased in the market | [CIQ Public Ownership Summary, Top Holders, position date 31-Mar-2026] |
| Institutions (aggregate) | 25.48% (15,320,520 sh) | Common (single class) | Purchased in the market (49 traditional investment managers + 1 sovereign wealth fund + 1 unclassified) | [CIQ Public Ownership Summary — Ownership Summary] |
| Individuals/Insiders (CIQ classification, aggregate) | 50.44% (30,332,710 sh, 9 holders) | Common (single class) | Mixed — includes the founders above plus a small number of other individual insiders | [CIQ Public Ownership Summary — Ownership Summary] |
| Corporations (Private) | 1.65% (992,122 sh) | Common (single class) | Not disclosed in this extract | [CIQ Public Ownership Summary — Ownership Summary] |
| Public and Other (residual float) | 14.72% (8,851,607 sh) | Common (single class) | N/A (dispersed public float) | [CIQ Public Ownership Summary — Ownership Summary] |

**Total insider (Promoter & Promoter Group) ownership: 49.11% as at 31 March 2026** (down 6bps from 49.17% at 31 March 2025) [FY26 Annual Report, Note 12(b); Corporate Governance Report]. The two co-founders alone directly hold 46.99% (28.01% + 18.98%). Both founders (Dinesh Chandra Agarwal — CEO, MD & Director; Brijesh Kumar Agrawal — Co-Founder & Whole-Time Director) have led the company continuously since its September 1999 incorporation, ~27 years, and their combined share count (28,230,569 shares) was **unchanged year-on-year** — the 6bp % decline is arithmetic (total shares outstanding rose from ~60.03mm to ~60.09mm on ESOP issuance), not a promoter sale [FY26 Annual Report, Note 12(b) — share counts identical FY25→FY26 for both founders].

**Data-reconciliation flag (CLAUDE.md §5/§15).** The Capital IQ "Public Ownership Summary" Top Holders table, position-dated 31-Mar-2026, reports materially different figures for the same two individuals: Dinesh Chandra Agarwal 29.09% (17,492,084 shares) and Brijesh Kumar Agrawal 19.99% (12,022,004 shares) — roughly 664,561 and 618,958 shares (≈1.1pp each) MORE than the audited FY26 Annual Report's Note 12(b) figures for the identical position date. Both figures internally reconcile against their own respective total-shares-outstanding bases (AR: 16,827,523/60,087,148=28.01%; CIQ: 17,492,084/60,133,558=29.09%), so this is not a rounding slip — it is two different underlying share counts for the same person as of the same date. Per the source hierarchy (§4), the **audited FY26 Annual Report figure (28.01% / 18.98%) is used as the primary ownership figure in this report**; the CIQ figures are cited here as vendor data, labeled, not silently substituted, and the gap is an open reconciliation item (possibly CIQ aggregating additional HUF/trust/beneficial holdings under the founders' names, or a stale vendor snapshot) that a future run should resolve against the SAST shareholding-pattern filing directly. [FY26 Annual Report, Note 12(b)] vs [CIQ Public Ownership Summary, Top Holders, position date 31-Mar-2026].

## 2. Insider Transactions (last 12 months)

No named, per-individual Form-4/SAST-PIT insider-transaction disclosures are present in the pool at the individual-trade level (no separate SAST Reg 29 disclosure exhibits or PIT trading-window filings were extracted). The only insider-transaction data available is the CIQ "Ownership Activity" panel, which aggregates transactions but does not name most counterparties:

| Insider | Buy / Sell | Amount | Date | Notes (10b5-1?) | Source |
|---|---|---:|---|---|---|
| Individuals/Insiders (CIQ-tracked, unnamed breakdown) | Buy | 513,115 shares (2 transactions) | 13-May-2026 to 13-Aug-2026 | Not disclosed whether open-market purchase or ESOP/SAR exercise — no 10b5-1-equivalent mechanism applicable in India; follow-up required | [CIQ Public Ownership Summary — Ownership Activity, "Individuals/Insiders" panel] |
| Individuals/Insiders (CIQ-tracked, unnamed breakdown) | Sell | 0 shares (0 transactions) | 13-May-2026 to 13-Aug-2026 | — | [CIQ Public Ownership Summary — Ownership Activity] |
| Individuals/Insiders — 2 holders | Decreased position | (18,872) shares combined | 31-Dec-2025 to 31-Mar-2026 | Includes a partial sale by an individual the CIQ data identifies as "Agrawal, Madhup" — (18,072) shares sold from a 779,792-share base (≈2.3% of that holder's disclosed position). This name does **not** appear among the 18 named entities in the FY26 Annual Report's Promoter & Promoter Group table (Note 12(b)) — the person's relationship to the company (KMP, employee-insider, or a promoter-family member below the AR's disclosure threshold) is **not established from this pool** | [CIQ Public Ownership Summary — Ownership Activity ("Individuals/Insiders," Dec-2025–Mar-2026) and Top Holders Activity ("Sellers": Agrawal, Madhup)] |

**Net insider activity: modestly net buying**, per the CIQ-tracked data — 513,115 shares bought vs 0 sold in the most recent ~3-month window (13-May-2026 to 13-Aug-2026), against a small ~18,872-share net decrease in the prior quarter. This CIQ feed does not break out buyer identities for the 513,115-share buy figure, and does not distinguish open-market purchases from ESOP/SAR exercises for either the buys or the "Madhup Agrawal" sale — **this is a genuine data-coverage gap, not a finding of clean or dirty insider behavior**, and it caps the "Recent insider behavior" score component below (Score Cap Rules / partial-data rule). Per the F20 red-flag gating rule, this CIQ pool export does **not** establish grounds for an RF-OWN-003 (stake-sale-before-bad-news) trigger — the sale is small (≈0.03% of shares outstanding), the identity of the seller is unconfirmed as an insider under the AR's promoter definition, and no adverse announcement timing was found to correlate with it in this pool.

## 3. Pledging & Encumbrance

| Insider | Shares Pledged | % of Their Holding | Source |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| Promoter & Promoter Group (aggregate) | Not disclosed / no pledge reported | 0% (no pledge column in the shareholding-pattern table; no separate SAST/PIT pledge disclosure in the pool) | [FY26 Annual Report, "A. PROMOTERS HOLDINGS"; Note 12(b) — no encumbrance column present]; [`business-model/01_disqualifier-scan.md`, disqualifier #2 — "no promoter-pledge amount or % is disclosed anywhere in the pool"] |

**No promoter/promoter-group share pledging is disclosed.** The only pledge-related fact in the pool is a **Secretarial Audit finding** that "a Designated Person[l] created pledge on equity shares of the Company without seeking pre-clearance from the Compliance Officer," reported by the company to the stock exchanges as a self-disclosed compliance lapse under the SEBI PIT regulations [FY26 Annual Report, Secretarial Audit Report, Annexure-A]. This individual is **unnamed** in the pool, the pledge magnitude is **not quantified**, and the person is **not identified as a promoter or promoter-group member** — it cannot be attributed to Dinesh Chandra Agarwal, Brijesh Kumar Agrawal, or any of the 16 other named promoter-group holders from the evidence available. Because the magnitude and identity are both undisclosed, this does **not** clear the A3-02 red band (≥25% of promoter holding, or ≥50%/≥20%-of-capital) and is **not** graded as an RF-OWN-001/002 promoter-pledge red flag — the gating rule (F20) requires a pool filing to name the fact with enough specificity to fire a red flag, and identity/magnitude are both missing here. It is recorded as a governance-hygiene item (an insider-trading pre-clearance control gap) for the audit/assurance and regulatory-legal specialists to size further, consistent with `business-model/01_disqualifier-scan`'s own routing instruction, and is reflected as a modest deduction in the Pledge/Encumbrance score below rather than a red flag.

## 4. Control Structure

| Feature | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Dual-class / super-voting | None — single class of equity, one share = one vote | [FY26 Annual Report, Note 12(a)] |
| Controlled-company status | Founder-controlled in substance but not in the SEBI regulatory sense (promoter group holds 49.11%, below the 50% "controlled company" convention, though the two founders remain the two most senior executives with 27 years of continuous tenure and no control contest is evident) | [FY26 Annual Report, Note 12(b); Corporate Governance Report] |
| Shareholder bloc with board rights | No disclosed board-nomination or veto rights held by any non-promoter shareholder (no investor-rights agreement, no PE-nominee director found on the current board roster); Nalanda Capital (7.71%) holds no board seat per the current Board Members roster | [CIQ Board Members.xls — current roster: 2 founder-executives, 1 executive (Company Secretary/WTD), 5 independent directors, 1 observer (Amit Behl, ex-Intel Capital) — no Nalanda nominee] |
| Controlling-owner objective alignment *(Filter 6)* | **Aligned owner-operator, not a structurally unaligned control.** The controlling bloc is two founder-executives who built the business from 1999, hold the largest personal economic stake (46.99% combined), have run the company continuously with no professional-CEO handoff, and receive dividends pro-rata with all other shareholders (no disproportionate cash-flow rights, no pyramid). This is the opposite of the three §24 Filter 6 conflict patterns — it is not government-controlled, not a listed subsidiary of a value-maximizing parent, and not a sprawling unrelated-diversified conglomerate. RF-OWN-004 does **not** apply. | [FY26 Annual Report, Note 12(b)]; [CIQ Board Members.xls]; [`business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance.md` §1] |

**Unaligned-owner test (CLAUDE.md §24, Filter 6).** None of the three conflict structures is present: (a) not government-controlled; (b) IndiaMART is the ultimate corporate parent of its own subsidiary tree (Busy Infotech, Tradezeal, Livekeeping, Finlite, PlayCez, Biztradeshows), not a listed subsidiary of anyone else [CIQ Corporate Structure Tree — "Ultimate Corporate Parent: IndiaMART InterMESH Limited"]; (c) the group is a single-sector B2B marketplace business plus one accounting-software subsidiary (Busy Infotech) and a nascent MSME-lending subsidiary being incorporated — not a sprawling unrelated conglomerate. **No structural conviction cap applies here**, and this is not a value-trap-under-a-misaligned-owner situation for the valuation module to price.

## 4A. Market Conduct & Stock Characteristics (A15-01 … A15-04)

| Signal | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Insider buys/sells vs subsequent results (timing) | No correlation between the CIQ-tracked insider buy activity (513,115 sh, May–Aug 2026) or the small "Agrawal, Madhup" sale (18,072 sh, Dec 2025–Mar 2026) and any subsequent weak or strong results announcement was found in the pool's earnings-call transcripts or Key Developments feed | [IndiaMART-InterMESH Key Developments.xls]; [CIQ Public Ownership Summary] |
| Unusual price / volume before announcements (A15-04) | No pattern of unusual price/volume runs ahead of results or material announcements was identified across the reviewed earnings-call transcripts (FY22–FY27, all quarters) and Key Developments feed; no exchange clarification requests were found in the pool | [Key Developments.xls]; [Q1 FY22–Q1 FY27 earnings-call transcripts] |
| Exchange clarification sought for a price move | None found in the pool | [Key Developments.xls] |
| Exchange surveillance status (India: ASM/GSM lists, 24 months) (A15-01) | **Insufficient Data.** No NSE/BSE Additional Surveillance Measure (ASM) or Graded Surveillance Measure (GSM) list disclosure, and no mention of any surveillance-framework placement, was found anywhere in the pool (annual report, key developments, analyst coverage, interim reports, or 24 quarters of earnings-call transcripts) — this is a coverage gap (an independent NSE/BSE ASM/GSM-list check was not performed), not a confirmed clean result | [Absence check across all pool documents, 2026-08-22] |
| Liquidity / average daily traded value — adequate for institutional exit? (A15-02) | Avg. 3-month daily volume ≈0.11mm shares (~110,000 sh/day) at a ~₹1,784–1,798 quote ≈ **₹19.6–19.8 crore (~US$2.2–2.3mm) of daily traded value**; market cap ₹107,314mn (~US$1.2bn); multiple institutional holders already run meaningful stakes (Nalanda 7.71%, ICICI Pru 6.47%, UTI 3.48%) — liquidity supports institutional entry/exit at this scale | [CIQ Public Company Profile, data as of 13-Aug-2026 — "Volume (mm) 0.09 / Avg 3M Dly Vlm (mm) 0.11"] |
| Independent research coverage (count) (A15-03) | **≥15 independent sell-side analysts** carry active coverage with recommendations (Ambit Capital, Anand Rathi, Avendus Spark, Centrum Broking, Choice Equity Broking, Daiwa Securities, DAM Capital, Dolat Capital, 360 ONE Capital, and others), spanning Sell/Neutral/Add ratings — well above the ≥3 green band | [CIQ Analyst Coverage, data as of pool date] |
| Institutional (FII/DII/MF) holding trend; any sharp high-quality-holder exit | Net institutional accumulation in the most recent tracked quarter (Dec-2025→Mar-2026): 21 institutions increased positions, 5 new institutional entrants (4,641,152 sh combined), vs 9 decreased and 1 fully "sold out" (The Vanguard Group, Inc., −1,222,307 sh). That sole full exit is offset almost exactly by a **new** position of nearly identical size from a differently-named CIQ entity, "Vanguard Capital Management, LLC" (+1,230,690 sh) — most likely an internal fund-family reclassification rather than a genuine institutional exit, though this is unconfirmed and flagged as a follow-up. Nalanda Capital (the largest non-promoter holder) and ICICI Prudential AMC (the largest domestic mutual-fund holder) are both **net buyers** in the same window (+1,245,860 and +697,484 shares respectively) | [CIQ Public Ownership Summary — Ownership Activity, Top Buyers and Sellers] |

Given the above, no market-conduct red flag is triggered (A15-04 Green on available evidence, with the caveat that a systematic price/volume-vs-announcement statistical study was not run within this pool).

## 4B. Ownership-Structure Hygiene (A3-08, A3-09, A3-10)

| Test | Finding | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Promoter-group classification (any reclassification to "public"? persons-acting-in-concert complete?) | No reclassification found — the same 18-entity Promoter & Promoter Group roster (2 founders + 16 named family individuals/HUFs/trusts) is disclosed consistently between the FY25 and FY26 comparative columns of Note 12(b), with only routine ESOP-dilution-driven % changes, not membership changes | [FY26 Annual Report, Note 12(b)] |
| Beneficial-ownership chain (ultimate owners identifiable? trusts/offshore layers?) | Ultimate beneficial owners are directly identifiable — named individuals, Hindu Undivided Families (a standard Indian personal-estate legal structure, disclosed by name), and two small named family trusts (Hamirwasia Business/Family Trust, Nanpara Business/Family Trust), each holding 0.00% (≤400 shares). No offshore entity or opaque holding-company layer sits between the founders and the listed shares — founders hold directly in personal capacity [Corporate Structure Tree confirms IndiaMART is its own ultimate corporate parent, with no promoter investment company upstream] | [FY26 Annual Report, Note 12(b)]; [CIQ Corporate Structure Tree] |
| Takeover-code disclosure hygiene (India: SAST Reg 29 timeliness, creeping-acquisition limits, promoter ≤75%) | Promoter & Promoter Group holding (49.11%) is comfortably below the 75% ceiling and below any open-offer trigger threshold; no adjudicated SAST violation, late-disclosure penalty, or open-offer dispute was found in the pool. The specific per-filing SAST Reg 29 timeliness (5% initial / ±2% incremental disclosure within 2 working days) could not be independently verified — no dated SAST filing log is in the pool — so that narrow sub-test is **Insufficient Data**, while the ≤75%-ceiling and no-open-offer-trigger tests are clear on available evidence | [FY26 Annual Report, Note 12(b) — 49.11% promoter holding]; [`business-model/01_disqualifier-scan.md`] |

## 5. Ownership / Insider Read

Skin in the game is real and it is founding equity, not option grants: the two co-founders have run IndiaMART continuously since 1999, hold 46.99% of the company directly between them, and their combined share count did not move year-on-year — the entire promoter-group % decline (49.17%→49.11%) is arithmetic dilution from ESOP issuance, not a sale. The net insider signal from the CIQ-tracked data is modestly positive (513,115 shares bought vs 0 sold in the most recent quarter) but the buyer identities and the open-market-vs-ESOP-exercise distinction are not resolved in this pool, so that signal carries limited weight. The biggest control-side risk to minority holders is not pledging (none is disclosed at the promoter level, and the one pledge-related fact in the pool is an unnamed, non-promoter, unquantified Designated-Person compliance lapse self-reported to the exchanges) — it is the unresolved ~1.1-percentage-point gap between the audited Annual Report's founder shareholding figures and Capital IQ's vendor figures for the same position date, which this report has flagged rather than silently resolved, and which should be checked against the underlying SAST shareholding-pattern filing before being relied on for any precision beyond "roughly 49% founder-controlled, one share one vote, no pledge disclosed."

## management-governance / 05_board-and-shareholder-rights.md

_Source: `05_board-and-shareholder-rights.md`_

# Board & Shareholder Rights — INDIAMART

**Company:** IndiaMART InterMESH Limited (NSE: INDIAMART; CIN L74899DL1999PLC101534) — India / SEBI-LODR + Companies Act 2013 regime. Proxy-equivalent documents used: FY26 Integrated Annual Report (Board's Report + Corporate Governance Report + Notes to Accounts), filed 02-Jun-2026 for FY ended 31-Mar-2026 ("FY26 AR"), and the FY25 Annual Report (29-Apr-2025) for prior-period comparison. As-of date for the board snapshot: 22-Aug-2026 (post the 27th AGM, held 29-Jun-2026).

**Upstream inputs consumed:** `01_management-and-track-record.md` (team vintage/bench, A13-04/05), `07_people-integrity-dossiers.md` (per-person grades, A1-05, A16/A17) — this agent does NOT re-run 07's sweeps, only consumes its grades. `business-model/01_disqualifier-scan.md` (no hard disqualifier triggered). `business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance.md` (context: no debt-funded/near-value serial-acquisition pattern; flags the same 21.72% RPT postal-ballot dissent this report treats in depth). `04_ownership-and-insider-behavior.md` has not yet run in this batch (parallel layer) — where its verdict would matter (RF-OWN-004 structurally-unaligned-owner test), this report notes the input is not yet available rather than assuming a result.

## 1. Board Composition

| Feature | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Board size | 8 directors as of 22-Aug-2026 (post 27th AGM); 9 directors at FY26 year-end (31-Mar-2026), before Mr. Dhruv Prakash retired by rotation at the AGM held 29-Jun-2026 | [FY26 Annual Report (Jun-02-2026), Corporate Governance Report, p.107] |
| Independent directors (%) — vs the applicable floor | 5 of 8 = **62.5%** currently; 5 of 9 = **55.6%** at FY26-end. Applicable floor: the company has **no designated Chairperson** at all (see below), which under SEBI LODR Reg 17(1B) triggers the "≥50% independent" floor rather than the ⅓ floor that applies with a non-independent/executive chair. Both the current and FY26-end ratios clear the applicable 50% floor, with limited headroom at FY26-end (55.6% vs 50%) | [FY26 AR, CG Report, p.106, "There is no designated Chairman of the Board... at least 50% of its Board members were Independent Directors"] |
| Chair / CEO split? Chair non-executive, non-promoter? Designated (not rotating)? | **No designated Chairman.** The Board elects a different Independent or Non-Executive Director to chair EACH board meeting; this rotating chair is confirmed "always" separate from the MD/CEO. A **Lead Independent Director** (Ms. Pallavi Dinodia Gupta, since 24-Mar-2023) substitutes for a fixed chair — she presides over IDs-only sessions and is the accountability anchor the rotating structure otherwise lacks. This is the LODR-permitted alternative structure, not a bespoke defense, but it is genuinely weaker than a designated non-executive chair (Amber, not Green — see 1B) | [FY26 AR, CG Report, p.106 & p.127, "Discretionary Requirements" §1-2; p.106 CG narrative] |
| Woman director / independent woman director where mandated | 2 of 8 directors are women — Ms. Vasuta Agarwal and Ms. Pallavi Dinodia Gupta — **both Independent**, satisfying the top-1000 "independent woman director" mandate (not a promoter relative counted as independent) | [FY26 AR, CG Report, p.107-108] |
| Meeting count, longest gap, ID present in every quorum (A1-14) | 4 Board meetings in FY26 (29-Apr-2025, 18-Jul-2025, 17-Oct-2025, 20-Jan-2026); maximum gap did not exceed the statutory 120 days; "the requisite quorum was present for all the Board and Committee meetings held during FY 2026" including the statutory ID-inclusive quorum requirement. Attendance was **100%** for every director across the meetings held during their tenure | [FY26 AR, CG Report, p.113, "Board and Committee Meetings"; p.108-109 attendance table] |
| Audit / Comp (NRC) / Nominating committee independence | Audit Committee: 3 of 4 members Independent (75%, chaired by an ID) — clears the ⅔ statutory floor but is not all-independent. NRC: 3 of 4 members Independent (75%), ID-chaired. Both compliant with Reg 18/19 composition; see 1B for meeting-frequency hygiene | [FY26 AR, CG Report, p.114-115] |

**A1-05 consumption note (owned by 07, not re-run here):** two of the company's four current controllers/senior officers — Founder-CEO-MD **Dinesh Chandra Agarwal** and Co-Founder-WTD **Brijesh Kumar Agrawal** — are graded **Material concerns**, not Clean, by 07's own-record sweep (an unresolved 2021 criminal FIR, allegation-stage, not adjudicated). Per the Person-Level Integrity Protocol's allegation != conviction rule this does **not** trip the Non-Negotiable Gate on its own, but it DOES fail the A1-05 Red band ("Any controller / CEO / CFO / chair graded Material concerns or Disqualifying") and caps conviction — no rating above Watchlist until resolved. Since the two individuals carrying this finding are also the company's two largest shareholders and its only two founder-directors, this is a direct constraint on how much weight the "board is a real check on management" read can carry (see Section 5).

## 1B. Board Process & Renewal (A1-08 ... A1-17)

| Test | Finding | Verdict | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| Skills matrix — per-director mapping, gaps (A1-08) | The AR discloses a skills matrix mapping directors to **4** competency categories: (1) IT/telecom/database/digitalisation, (2) finance/taxation/compliance/corporate governance, (3) interpersonal skills/HR management, (4) sales/marketing/corporate strategy. Finance and the company's core industry (IT/digital) are both covered by named directors including the Audit Committee chair; no core-competency gap. But the disclosed set (4 categories) is below the Green band's >=5 distinct competencies | **Amber** | FY26 AR, CG Report, p.109-110 |
| ID resignations, 3 years — reasons held up? (A1-09) | Two ID exits in the review window: Rajesh Sawhney ceased 22-Sep-2025 on **completion of his second (statutory-maximum) term** — not a resignation; Aakash Chaudhry resigned 21-Jan-2025 with 07's sweep confirming "no other material reason" beyond the stated one. No pattern of concern-citing exits, no >=2 unexplained mid-term exits in 24 months | **Green** | FY26 AR, CG Report, p.110-111; 07 dossier 1 rows 20-22 |
| Reappointments/removals — evaluation-based? against dissent? (A1-10) | Only one director-related postal ballot vote in the window (Vasuta Agarwal's appointment, 99.9968% in favour) — no reappointment pushed through against dissent, no dissenting ID removed. No evidence either way of a merit-based reappointment process tied to individual evaluation outcomes for this cycle's continuing directors, since none were up for reappointment this year | **Green** (no adverse pattern; Insufficient Data on the disclosed-evaluation-link specifically) | FY26 AR, CG Report, p.122-123 (postal ballot) |
| Board evaluation — method + outcomes, or recycled boilerplate? (A1-11) | An annual structured-questionnaire evaluation is described (Board, Committees, and each Director individually, IDs evaluated by the Board excl. the promoters, Non-Independent Directors evaluated by IDs). The AR states generically that "Both NRC and the Board were satisfied with the evaluation process" and "Directors expressed their satisfaction with the entire evaluation process" — no specific findings, scores, or resulting actions are disclosed, which is the pattern the registry treats as boilerplate-adjacent (only one year's AR is in this pool, so the "3+ years recycled" Red threshold cannot be tested) | **Amber** | FY26 AR, CG Report, p.114-115 |
| Diversity & refreshment — median tenure, additions (A1-12) | 2 independent women directors (mandate met at the stronger bar). Median ID tenure among the 5 current IDs is approximately **1.6 years** (Gour 8.3y, Gupta 3.8y, Vij 1.6y, Barasia 1.3y, Vasuta Agarwal 1.1y) — well under the 9-year Green threshold, with 3 new IDs added within the last 16 months (staggered: Jan-2025, Apr-2025, Jul-2025) | **Green** | FY26 AR, CG Report, p.107-108 |
| Succession planning (A1-13) | No explicit CEO or board succession-planning policy or process is disclosed in this pool. Founder-CEO Dinesh Chandra Agarwal is 56 — below the >=60 threshold that makes non-disclosure a hard Red under the registry, so this is a genuine gap to flag rather than a banded failure | **Insufficient Data / Amber** (no disclosure found; not yet at the mandatory-disclosure age trigger) | No hit found across the FY26 AR text pool |
| Permanent / age-exempt seats — shareholder renewal within 5y; 75+ NED special resolution (A1-15) | No director is aged >=75 (oldest disclosed age is Dhruv Prakash at 74, who has since retired by rotation at the 27th AGM — a routine, shareholder-voted rotation, not a permanent seat). No promoter patriarch holds a non-retiring seat outside a shareholder vote | **Green** | FY26 AR, CG Report, p.108 (age column) |
| ID appointment/removal minority gate — public-majority carried? vacancies <=3 months? (A1-16) | The one instance with full vote-count disclosure — Vasuta Agarwal's appointment as ID, by **Special Resolution** via Postal Ballot (29-Aug-2025) — passed 99.9968% in favour (51,201,833 of 51,203,464 votes polled), an overwhelming margin that would clear a majority-of-public-shareholders test even though the pool does not separately break out promoter vs. public votes. Barasia's and Vij's appointment votes (Apr-2025/Jan-2025) are not separately disclosed in this pool (likely approved at the 26th AGM, 16-Jun-2025, whose own resolution-level results are not in the pool) | **Green (partial)** — the one fully-disclosed instance clears the bar cleanly; the other two appointments' vote breakdowns are Not Available | FY26 AR, CG Report, p.122-123 (postal ballot); 00 triage 5D |
| IDs-only session & D&O cover (A1-17) | A separate Committee-of-Independent-Directors meeting was held 25-Apr-2025 (1 meeting, all IDs present, no Non-Independent Director or management present) — satisfies the IDs-only session requirement. **D&O (Directors' & Officers' Liability) insurance is not mentioned anywhere in this pool's AR text extract.** IndiaMART's market capitalisation places it well within the top-1000-by-market-cap universe where D&O cover is mandatory under LODR Reg 25(10) — the absence of any disclosure (rather than confirmation either way) is a genuine gap, not a confirmed breach | **Amber** — IDs-only session Green; D&O cover **Insufficient Data** (not disclosed, follow-up required given likely top-1000 status) | FY26 AR, CG Report, p.117-118 (CID); CIQ Key Stats (Market Cap — see note below) |

*Market-cap figure note: the CIQ Key Stats export field reads "Market Capitalization 107314.347606" without an explicit currency-scale label in this extract; cross-checked against the FY26 AR's disclosed 60,087,148 shares outstanding, this figure is most consistent with Rs million (roughly Rs 10,731 crore / ~$1.2-1.3bn) rather than Rs crore — still comfortably inside India's top-1000-listed-by-market-cap set at either scale, so the D&O-mandate read above holds either way. Flagged as a unit-verification item, not a material change to the finding.*

## 2. Tenure & Refreshment

| Signal | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Average director tenure | Executives: ~26.9 years (Dinesh Agarwal, Brijesh Agrawal, founders since incorporation) pulls the average up sharply; IDs alone average ~3.2 years, median ~1.6 years | FY26 AR, CG Report, p.108 |
| Overboarding / staleness signs | No director breaches the statutory cap (7 listed directorships for an ID, 3 as ID) or the module's <=4-listed-board norm; company states affirmatively "No Independent Director serves as an Independent Director in more than 7 (seven) listed companies" and that no director exceeds Reg 26 committee-membership/chairmanship caps. The one genuine staleness signal pre-dates this AR: **Vivek Gour held Chair of Audit, Compensation (NRC), Corporate Governance-equivalent (SRC), and Nominating committees simultaneously before a 18-Jul-2025 reshuffle redistributed NRC-chair to Pallavi Gupta and SRC-chair to Sandeep Barasia** — the CURRENT state (post-reshuffle, as reported in this FY26 AR) is Green on chair concentration (Gour now chairs 2 of 8 committees: Audit + CSRS), but the pre-reshuffle concentration is worth noting as the direction the board was drifting before the July 2025 correction | FY26 AR, CG Report, p.108, 114-121; CIQ Board Members.xls (captures the pre-reshuffle bios) |
| Recent refreshment | 3 new IDs added within 16 months (Manish Vij, Jan-2025; Sandeep Barasia, Apr-2025; Vasuta Agarwal, Jul-2025), replacing 2 departing IDs (Aakash Chaudhry, Rajesh Sawhney) and one non-independent NED retiring by rotation (Dhruv Prakash, Jun-2026) — active, staggered refreshment, not a stagnant board | FY26 AR, CG Report, p.107-110 |

## 3. Related-Party Transactions & Group Leakage

**Scope note (quantification boundary, per MODULE_RULES):** `09_related-party-and-group-forensics` owns the primary A5/A11 item-level RPT quantification and runs in the same layer as this agent, so its output is not available here. This section stays to the **minority-protection lens** — approval process, IDs-only voting, majority-of-minority hygiene, and the shape of any dissent — citing the AR's own headline RPT figures only as context. No A5 item-level verdicts are issued here.

| RPT Type | Amount (FY26, Rs mn) | % of Revenue | % of PAT | % of Net Worth | Arm's Length? | Source |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---|---|
| Rent & related expenses to promoter-linked entity (Mansa Enterprises Pvt Ltd) | 6.85 | ~0.05% of Rs 14,428mn standalone revenue | Not computed here (09's scope) | Not computed here | Company states "transactions with related parties are entered on terms equivalent to those that prevail in arm's length transactions" | FY26 AR, Note 33, p.240 |
| Tax consultancy / litigation support to Lead ID's own firm (S R Dinodia & Co LLP) | 1.70 | ~0.01% | — | — | Same arm's-length assertion; independence-hygiene note under A1-04, not quantified as leakage | FY26 AR, Note 33, p.240 |
| Recruitment & training expenses (KMP) | 3.01 | ~0.02% | — | — | Arm's-length assertion | FY26 AR, Note 33, p.240 |
| Remuneration to relatives of KMP | 10.14 (FY25: 4.72 — **+115% YoY**) | ~0.07% | — | — | Arm's-length assertion; growth rate flagged (small base) | FY26 AR, Note 33, p.240 |
| Director's sitting fees | 8.00 (FY25: 7.80) | ~0.06% | — | — | Statutory fee schedule | FY26 AR, Note 33, p.240 |
| Dividend paid to KMP (pro-rata on equity held) | 1,413.43 (FY25: 565.39) | Not a leakage metric — pro-rata distribution on disclosed shareholding, not a preferential payment | — | — | Pro-rata, all-shareholder basis | FY26 AR, Note 33, p.240 |
| Dividend paid to relatives of KMP | 55.70 (FY25: 22.66) | Same — pro-rata | — | — | Pro-rata | FY26 AR, Note 33, p.240 |
| Investments in wholly/majority-owned subsidiaries & associates (Fleetx, IIL Digital, Tradezeal, Livekeeping) | 645.00 aggregate (85+60+90+410) | Capital deployment into consolidated/associate entities, not a leakage channel | — | — | Not a related-party leakage item — routed to 09/business-model capital allocation | FY26 AR, Note 33, p.240 |
| Outstanding trade balances with subsidiaries/associates | Low single-digit Rs mn each (e.g. Busy Infotech Rs 0.07mn, IB Monotaro Rs 1.28mn) | Immaterial | — | — | Unsecured, interest-free, settled in cash | FY26 AR, Note 33, p.241 |
| Loans / guarantees to promoter entities (Sec 185/186) | **None outstanding at FY26 year-end.** One inter-corporate loan to Mobisy Technologies Pvt Ltd (an associate, not a promoter entity) of Rs 30mn was advanced and fully repaid within FY25 | N/A | — | — | Nil balance at FYE26 | FY26 AR, Note 33, p.240-241 |

**Matched-basis check.** Every RPT figure above is drawn from Note 33's "total amount of transactions... for the relevant period" table — a **cumulative full-year flow** figure — divided against Rs 14,428.03mn, the **cumulative full-year** standalone revenue figure from the same FY26 report [FY26 AR, Note 34 (function-wise results), p.~242]. Both numerator and denominator are on the same basis (full-year cumulative, standalone), so no peak-vs-point-in-time or cap-vs-balance mismatch arises here — this is one of the more straightforward RPT disclosures in the registry sense. The aggregate of the genuinely operating (non-dividend, non-investment) RPT lines above is roughly Rs 30mn against Rs 14,428mn revenue, or **approximately 0.2%** — well inside the Green materiality band (<1%), consistent with the company's own statement that "there were no material related party transactions... that may have potential conflict with the interests of the Company at large" [FY26 AR, CG Report, p.126].

**No related financial counterparty (captive NBFC / group treasury vehicle) exists in the current related-party set** — note IndiaMART incorporated a new wholly-owned lending subsidiary, IndiaMART Finance Limited, on 04-Aug-2026 (post FY26 year-end) for MSME working-capital financing. This is too new to carry any FY26 transactions and is not yet a related-party leakage question; flagged for the NEXT run to test once it has a first reporting period [Corporate Timeline.xls, 04-Aug-2026 entry].

**RPT approval hygiene (the minority-protection question this section owns):** "All the Related Party Transactions were approved by the Independent Directors of the Committee" (Audit Committee) [FY26 AR, CG Report, p.115]. The RPT Policy was reviewed and updated by the Board on 20-Jan-2026 to align with current LODR amendments [FY26 AR, "Other Disclosures", p.126]. This is the correct process. **The one place this process was actually tested by a shareholder vote produced a genuine dissent signal:** a Postal Ballot **Ordinary Resolution** to approve a related-party "office or place of profit" appointment for **Mr. Amit Agarwal** (a disclosed relative of KMP, transacting as a KMP-category counterparty from 01-Nov-2025) was put to shareholders on 29-Aug-2025 and passed with only **78.28% in favour / 21.72% against** (33,955,911 votes polled; 7,374,238 against) [FY26 AR, "Postal Ballot", p.122-123]. This clears the **>20% votes-against** significant-dissent threshold in the registry (A5-06/A10-03), and **no disclosed board response to the dissent was found in this pool** — a gap against the registry's ">=20% against met with a disclosed response within 6 months" standard. This is the single most concrete, board-level minority-protection finding in this report: nearly a quarter of votes cast objected to a promoter-family-relative pay arrangement, and the company's own filings show no follow-up explanation. Cross-referenced: `business-model/01_disqualifier-scan` did NOT treat this as a hard disqualifier (RPT value is small; the flag is about board responsiveness to dissent, not RPT size) [business-model/01_disqualifier-scan.md 3].

## 4. Takeover Defenses & Voting Rights

| Feature | Present? | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| Poison pill | No | Not a mechanism available or disclosed under Indian company/securities law for this issuer; no shareholder-rights plan of any kind found | No hit found in pool |
| Classified / staggered board | No (statutory default only) | Executive/promoter directors and the one former non-independent NED are "liable to retire by rotation" per the Companies Act default (roughly two-thirds of the board); Independent Directors are NOT liable to retire by rotation (fixed statutory terms) — this is the ordinary Companies Act structure applied uniformly, not a bespoke entrenchment device layered on top of it | FY26 AR, Board's Report, p.~7-8 ("liable to retire by rotation" language) |
| Dual-class / unequal voting | No | Single class of equity shares, Rs 10 face value, one-share-one-vote, 100% dematerialised | FY26 AR, "General Shareholder Information", p.124-125 |
| Majority vs plurality director voting | Majority (India standard) | Director appointments pass by ordinary or special resolution on a majority-of-votes-cast basis — no plurality voting structure | FY26 AR, Postal Ballot section, p.122-123 |
| Shareholder ability to call meetings / act by written consent | Functioning channel demonstrated | The Postal Ballot mechanism (Companies Act Sec 108/110 + LODR Reg 44) was actively used twice in FY26, with e-voting, an independent scrutinizer (Deepak Kukreja, DMK Associates), and a publicly declared result — evidence the mechanism is not merely notional. Nothing in the pool suggests the statutory right to requisition an EGM (Companies Act Sec 100, >=10% holders) is restricted | FY26 AR, "Postal Ballot" and "Extra-ordinary General Meeting" sections, p.122 |

**Read:** the natural anti-takeover feature here is ownership concentration, not a formal defense — the promoter/promoter-group holds 49.17% (18 members), which alone makes a hostile change of control impractical without their consent. That concentration is an ownership-module finding (`04`, not yet run in this batch), not a board-designed defense; it is noted here only because it functionally substitutes for the formal defenses (pill, staggered board, dual class) this section tests for, none of which are present.

No AGM votes-against data beyond the two Postal Ballot resolutions exists in this pool; this is a genuine coverage gap for the broader A10-03 institutional-voting-pattern read, not a confirmed clean record.

## 5. Minority-Shareholder Protection Read

The board clears the structural minimums — a majority-independent board (55.6-62.5%, above the applicable 50% floor), 100% meeting attendance, a functioning committee structure whose chairs were actively de-concentrated in July 2025 (Vivek Gour previously chaired 4 committees, now chairs 2), a working Postal Ballot channel with an independent scrutinizer, negligible dilution, and no formal takeover defenses layered on top of the promoters' natural 49.17% control block. But "board is a real check on management" cannot be stated cleanly for two connected reasons this section is required to carry forward, not soften: (1) 07's own-record sweep grades the founder-CEO-MD and the co-founder-WTD — the company's two largest shareholders and its only two founder-directors — **Material concerns** on an unresolved 2021 criminal FIR, which is an allegation, not a conviction, but by rule caps conviction at no better than Watchlist until resolved; and (2) the one related-party transaction that was actually put to a shareholder vote this year — a promoter-family-relative office-of-profit appointment — drew **21.72% against**, comfortably past the significant-dissent line, with **no disclosed board response** found anywhere in this pool. Add to that a rotating, undesignated board chair (mitigated but not replaced by a Lead Independent Director), a skills matrix one category short of the registry's green band, a generically-worded board evaluation with no disclosed specific outcomes, and an undisclosed D&O insurance position on a company almost certainly inside India's top-1000-by-market-cap mandate, and the honest read is **adequate**, not **strong protection**: the mechanics of shareholder democracy function (the postal ballot itself proves that), but this year's one real test of them — the RPT dissent — was not visibly answered, and the two founder-directors carry an unresolved integrity question that a truly independent board would be expected to address explicitly rather than leave silent in the filings.

**Verdict: adequate.** The single biggest entrenchment/self-dealing risk to minority holders is not a formal defense (there is none) but the combination of (a) an unaddressed 22%-against vote on a promoter-relative RPT and (b) a board whose only accountable, always-present leadership figure (the Lead Independent Director) sits alongside — not above — two founder-directors currently graded Material concerns by the person-integrity sweep. `04_ownership-and-insider-behavior` has not yet run in this batch; if it independently confirms a structurally-unaligned-owner pattern (RF-OWN-004) on top of the above, this report's "adequate" read would need to be revisited downward toward "weak / entrenched" per the cross-module rule — that check could not be completed here and is flagged as the standing follow-up.

| Shareholder voting rights | 10 | 15 | No dual-class/poison pill/staggered board; a demonstrably functioning Postal Ballot channel; but D&O cover undisclosed and full 26th/27th AGM voting results not in this pool (05-016/023) |
| AGM opposition / minority protection | 4 | 10 | The RPT postal-ballot dissent (21.72% against, no visible response) is the standout negative; the ID-appointment vote (99.9968%) is the standout positive, but the negative is weighted more heavily because it is the un-actioned one |
| **Total** | **69** | **100** | Sum of above |

*Inverted-score note: none of the components above are inverted (all "higher = better"); the module-level "RPT & leakage risk" score (inverted, owned in aggregate by the `99` synthesis) is informed by, but not identical to, the "RPT and group-leakage protection" row here, which is scored on the protection/process lens this agent owns.*

## management-governance / 06_candor-and-disclosure-quality.md

_Source: `06_candor-and-disclosure-quality.md`_

# Candor & Disclosure Quality — INDIAMART

**Data availability note.** This read is built from **verbatim** earnings-call transcripts (S&P Global Market Intelligence transcripts, tier 3 per this module's source hierarchy) — 22 quarterly transcripts spanning Q3 FY21 through Q1 FY27 are present in the data pool, including the two most recent (Q4 FY26, 30-Apr-2026, and Q1 FY27, 21-Jul-2026), both read in full for this report. No shareholder letter exists in the pool (IndiaMART does not publish one; the FY26 Annual Report's Chairman/MD statements were reviewed for tone as a supplement). **No partial-data cap applies to this report** — the verbatim-transcript requirement in this module's Partial-Data Rule is met, so the tone, Q&A-evasiveness, and owning-the-miss components below are fully assessable, not capped at Insufficient Data. Cross-module inputs used: `earnings/06_earnings-quality.md` (non-GAAP read, present and read in full) and `earnings/04_guidance-consensus.md` (guidance/beat-miss record, present and read in full).

---

## 1. Owning the Misses

The checkable misses come from `01_management-and-track-record.md` §4 (Promise vs Delivery), which is not this agent's product — this section tests HOW each miss was communicated, not whether it happened.

| Miss (from 01) | How It Was Communicated | Owned or Obscured? | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| FY24 EBITDA margin: guided "28%–29%" / "target to reach 30%" (Q4 FY23 call); actual 26.54% | Not concealed inside an adjusted-metric overlay (none exists — §2). CFO and CEO discussed margin drivers on the very next calls in plain operating terms (pricing, verification-driven conversion loss, employee cost) rather than reframing the metric | **Owned** — the shortfall is visible in the same GAAP EBITDA line management itself reports every quarter; no rebasing or relabeling followed the miss | `Q4 2023 Earnings Call, Apr-28-2023` (promise); `Q4 2024/Q4 2026 Earnings Call transcripts` (subsequent commentary); `earnings/03_margin-drivers.md` §5 (outcome) |
| FY24 quarterly paying-supplier net-adds: guided "8,000–9,000 maintainable"; fell to 1,800 (the company's own "lowest ever" quarter) | CEO used the company's own superlative ("lowest ever") rather than softening language; broke the shortfall into stated, named causes (silver-tier price increase, war-related demand disruption, elevated churn) with rough attribution splits each time the trend continued (e.g., Q4 FY26: "about 1,200 customers decrease... 1,000, 1,500 could be because of the war, 1,000, 1,500 could have been because of the price") | **Owned, in granular detail** — management volunteered a cause-by-cause breakdown unprompted by the specific split, not a vague "macro headwinds" gloss | `Q4 2023 Earnings Call, Apr-28-2023` (promise); `Q4 2024 / Q4 2026 Earnings Call transcripts` (outcome + attribution); `Q1 2027 Earnings Call, Jul-21-2026` (continued decline: −1,850 suppliers, "leaky bucket" framing volunteered by an analyst and accepted by the CEO, not pushed back on) |
| Standalone/consolidated growth aspiration ("20–25%" / "33%," Q4 FY24 call) vs actual 16.01% (FY25) / 13.02% (FY26) | No restatement of the aspiration as "on track"; the underlying supplier/churn KPI that drives it continued to be reported quarter over quarter showing the shortfall in real time, and management explicitly declined to reset a new numeric target ("we would come back with a better guidance" once churn improves) | **Owned but not replaced** — the miss is visible in the reported KPI trail, but management substituted silence for a corrected number rather than either defending or restating the aspiration | `Q4 2024 Earnings Call, Apr-30-2024` (promise); `earnings/01_historical-financials.md` §6 (outcome) |
| Q4 FY26 EPS: consensus ₹21.30 (transcript's own embedded table) / ₹17.45–21.30 (Surprise tab) vs actual ₹8.33–₹11.54 (a ~52% miss, the sharpest of the last 5 quarters) | CFO attributed the swing explicitly and immediately to "mark-to-market losses on our treasury portfolio resulting from a significant increase in bond yields during the quarter," labelled them "notional losses, which would reverse on a long-term basis," and separated this from operating performance rather than blending it into a vaguer "other factors" explanation | **Owned, with a specific, checkable causal claim** — the ₹34cr negative other-income figure and its driver (bond-yield move) were stated on the call itself, not deferred to the annual report | `Q4 2026 Earnings Call, Apr-30-2026, prepared remarks (Jitin Diwan)`; cross-checked against `earnings/06_earnings-quality.md` §5, §7 (recurring treasury-gain pattern) |

**Reading the pattern.** Across four consecutive years of misses on the metrics that matter most (margin, net-adds, growth aspiration), management's real-time communication style is consistent: name the miss in the same terms as the target was set, quantify the drivers with specific splits rather than vague "headwinds," and let the underlying KPI keep printing every quarter rather than smoothing it into a blended or adjusted figure. The one genuine communication gap — flagged already in `01` (finding 01-012) — is that after the FY24 net-add and margin misses, management stopped giving ANY forward numeric target on net-adds rather than resetting one at an achievable level; that is an accountability gap (nothing to be held to next), but it is an openly stated, reasoned withdrawal ("we would come back with a better guidance" once churn improves), not a quiet one — see §4A.

---

## 2. Non-GAAP / Adjustment Aggressiveness

| Signal | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Recurring "one-off" addbacks | **None exist in the company's own presentation.** A full-text search of the FY26 Annual Report for "adjusted EBITDA," "non-GAAP," "adjusted EPS," and "adjusted net profit" returned zero matches — IndiaMART reports a single Ind AS GAAP figure set with no management-adjusted overlay to exclude items from. Stock-based compensation (₹366.20mn FY26, +95.4% YoY) is fully expensed inside GAAP Employee benefits expense, not carved out | `earnings/06_earnings-quality.md` §4 |
| GAAP-to-adjusted gap (magnitude / trend) | Not applicable in the conventional sense (no adjusted metric exists to gap against GAAP). The comparable gap that DOES exist sits inside GAAP itself: CIQ's own vendor income-statement structure buckets ₹1,906.51mn (FY26) / ₹2,669.22mn (FY25) / ₹2,047.16mn (FY24) of treasury fair-value gains as "Unusual Items" even though the item has recurred, at material size, in every one of the last 5 fiscal years — ~30% of FY26 Profit Before Tax and ~40% of diluted EPS. This is a vendor-classification/investor-communication clarity issue, not a company-manufactured non-GAAP metric: IndiaMART itself never labels the item "unusual" or excludes it from anything it reports | `earnings/06_earnings-quality.md` §5, §7 |
| Does adjusted framing flatter reality? | No — because no adjusted framing exists to flatter with. The one place management characterizes a P&L swing in its own words is the CFO's "notional losses... would reverse on a long-term basis" description of the Q4 FY26 treasury mark-to-market loss (§1) — a technically accurate description of unrealized bond mark-to-market accounting, not an exclusion or relabeling of the loss (it stayed in reported EBITDA/PBT/net profit, was quantified on the call, and reversed materially the following quarter as management said it would: Q1 FY27 other income +₹107cr) | `Q4 2026 Earnings Call, Apr-30-2026`; `Q1 2027 Earnings Call, Jul-21-2026`; `earnings/06_earnings-quality.md` §5 |

**Cross-check confirmed.** `earnings/06_earnings-quality.md` is present in the pool and gives an Earnings Quality Score of 72/100 (Strong band), with the accrual-quality battery triggering only 1 of 6 flags and no RF-EQ-001/002 emitted. The one real earnings-quality caveat that carries into candor is that the recurring treasury gain is labelled "unusual" only by the data vendor's income-statement template, not by the company — so a reader relying on CIQ's own structure (rather than the Annual Report itself) could be misled into treating a structural, recurring income source as one-off. This is a data-presentation risk downstream of the company's disclosure, not a management-manufactured adjustment; graded accordingly in §5 of the score table below (non-GAAP/adjustment discipline: 16/20, not the full 20, because the company has not proactively flagged in its own materials that this "unusual" bucket recurs every year).

**RF-DISC-002 (recurring "one-off" / aggressive non-GAAP add-backs) is NOT triggered.** IndiaMART presents no company-defined adjusted metric of any kind for the checklist item to test — the trigger targets management's own exclusion game, and none exists here.

---

## 3. Disclosure Transparency

Segment economics are disclosed with real numbers, not boilerplate: every quarterly call breaks out Busy Infotech billing, revenue-from-operations, deferred revenue, cash-from-operations, and license counts separately from the core marketplace, and the core-platform KPIs (paying-supplier count and net change, unique business inquiries, collections, deferred revenue, EBITDA margin) have been reported on a consistent basis across all 22 transcripts in the pool, including through the FY24–FY27 deterioration — management did not stop reporting the metrics that got worse, only the forward guidance built on them (§4A). One transparency reduction to note: traffic-source detail (organic vs performance-marketing vs direct) was explicitly withheld in Q4 FY26 ("I won't be able to give you how much traffic is coming through organic search... I can't provide you a split") — a real information gap, but framed candidly as "too new to disclose reliably" rather than denied outright, and it is operational granularity below the KPI level rather than a KPI itself going dark.

---

## 4. Tone in Bad Times

The worst recent print by the numbers is Q4 FY26 (Apr-30-2026): EPS missed consensus by ~52% (the sharpest miss of the last 5 quarters per `earnings/04_guidance-consensus.md` §6), net paying suppliers declined 1,200 for the quarter, and the CEO used the phrase "worry of falling into a vicious loop" — a founder-CEO volunteering the negative framing himself rather than waiting for an analyst to suggest it. Q&A was answered directly: churn rates were given by tier and cadence (Platinum <1%/month, Gold 1–1.5%/month, Silver ~7%/month vs a prior ~5–6%), the net-add shortfall was split by named cause with rough percentages, and when an analyst's question broke up mid-call ("Swapnil, your voice is breaking"), management asked for a repeat rather than letting the question drop. The subsequent Q1 FY27 call (Jul-21-2026) continued the same register under continued pressure — an analyst put the "leaky bucket" framing to the CEO directly and he accepted it without deflecting, then answered "I can't really answer that for sure" rather than manufacture false certainty on whether the churn problem is structural. No instance of a declined or redirected question was found in either transcript; every question asked was answered, including ones the company had no good answer to.

---

## 5. Candor Read

Management is **candid / high-trust** in how it communicates bad results: across four years of real, material misses on the metrics that matter most (margin, net-adds, growth), the CEO and CFO consistently used the company's own words for its own shortfalls ("lowest ever" quarter, "worry of... a vicious loop," "I'm really not confident to give you any answer"), gave granular cause-by-cause splits rather than blended "headwinds" language, and — critically — kept reporting the deteriorating KPIs every single quarter rather than redefining, rebasing, or dropping them (A7-03: Green). The strongest evidence for trust is structural: there is no non-GAAP overlay to hide behind (§2) and no declined or deflected question was found in the two most recent verbatim transcripts (§4) even as analysts pressed directly on whether the core growth problem is structural. The strongest evidence against unconditional trust is the guidance-withdrawal pattern (A7-04: Amber, RF-MGT-003 already flagged upstream in `01`) — after missing specific numeric net-add and margin targets, management chose silence over a corrected, achievable target, which protects it from a future scored miss but leaves investors with less to hold it to; this is an accountability gap, not concealment, since the withdrawal itself was stated openly and repeated with the same reasoning across multiple calls, not buried. Separately, the one routed integrity item from `business-model/01_disqualifier-scan` (two self-reported SEBI PIT/Designated-Person compliance lapses) was investigated by `01_management-and-track-record` §4A and concluded non-adverse and self-disclosed — **RF-MGT-005 was NOT emitted upstream**, so there is no unresolved adverse integrity signal here to weigh against the trust read; if a future run identifies the unnamed individuals or finds a broader pattern, this conclusion should be revisited. On balance: **candid / high-trust**, with the guidance-withdrawal accountability gap as the one qualifier worth tracking.

The single biggest candor signal: a founder-CEO who, four years running, reported the exact number that proved him wrong on his own prior quarterly call, in the same units he originally guided in, without changing what gets measured.

Ran on 2 entities; reached hop 2 on Livekeeping's chain (founder Kothari → no further adverse links found).

**Max hop reached:** 2. **Termination rule:** `no_new_subjects` — Phase 1 and the founder loop (Phase 2) on both corroborated E-A entities returned no further adverse leads requiring hop-3 extension (no Material-or-worse fact was found on any hop-2 subject), and the remaining register (Tier B/C people, E-B associate-stake entities, promoter family trusts) was swept to its tier depth without producing a subject that would justify extending depth. One entity (D-4 trademark axis; the "Voicewaves" name-ambiguity) is recorded on the Scope-Boundary Register rather than closed by this rule — see Section 5.

**A17-02 / A17-03 read (Green):** The 1996-vs-1999 gap is the D-2 founding-year mismatch LEAD the Protocol requires be chased — and it resolves cleanly. The predecessor (InterMESH Systems) is disclosed by the company's own history narrative as a sole proprietorship of the SAME founder who remains CEO today, absorbed into the newly incorporated company through a formal 01-Jan-2000 agreement with STATED consideration (equity + preference shares issued to Dinesh Chandra Agarwal). There is no separate legal entity left behind, no insolvency, no phoenix pattern, and no undisclosed party — the "predecessor" is the same person's own pre-incorporation venture, formalized. This is the Protocol's explicitly Green case: "a group founding year is a legitimate, Green claim where the group entity is disclosed — the mismatch reconciles." No RF-NET-001/002 fires. This agent's sourcing for the InterMESH Systems fact is Web-tier (secondary company-history summaries, unverified per §5) rather than the company's own DRHP text (not in this run's data pool) — the fact is flagged for confirmation against the DRHP/AR text directly, but two independent secondary summaries agree on the mechanics and consideration, which is treated as adequate corroboration for a Green read, not a Critical claim.

### 2.1 Dinesh Chandra Agarwal (DIN not confirmed this sweep) — Founder, CEO, MD & Director; Promoter — Grade: Material concerns

**Namesake caution (Hard Rule applied).** A mirror search for "Dinesh Chandra Agarwal" on a corporate-registry mirror returned a DIN (00191800) associated with unrelated entities — BD Agarwal Buildtech India Private Limited, Emjay Overseas Private Ltd, Binjrajka Steel Tubes Limited — with NO IndiaMART linkage found. This is treated as a **possible namesake and is NOT attributed** to IndiaMART's founder-CEO, consistent with the Protocol's own worked precedent for this exact company ("a 'Dinesh Agarwal' in political news was a different individual"). This agent's DIN for the actual founder-CEO could not be independently confirmed this sweep (the AR signature block almost certainly carries it, but it was not captured in this run's pool extract per triage). The adverse finding below is instead anchored on **≥2 corroborating identifiers**: consistent identification by role/title ("IndiaMART CEO", "founder Dinesh Agarwal") across five independent, dated, reputable news outlets (National Herald, The Print, DNA India, Business Standard, ETV Bharat) reporting the same Feb-2021 FIR, plus his own company's consistent public self-identification as founder-CEO since 1996/1999.

Per the allegation ≠ conviction rule this is graded Material concerns (not Disqualifying) and caps conviction at no rating above Watchlist until resolved — it does not by itself fail the Non-Negotiable Gate. This finding is routed to `12` for the canonical company-level litigation-reconciliation sweep (A9-10) to confirm current status and disclosure.

Ltd.").
