## 3B. Lineage & Phoenix Read (A17-02, A17-03)

**Does a predecessor exist?** Yes — InterMESH Systems, a sole proprietorship operated by founder Dinesh Chandra Agarwal from 1996, running the website www.indiamart.com, before the company itself was incorporated on 13-Sep-1999.

**Did it fail?** No. A sole proprietorship has no separate corporate existence to fail — it was voluntarily formalized into a company as the business scaled, a completely ordinary Indian-startup incorporation pattern.

**Did assets/brands/staff/address/customers continue into the current entity?** Yes — explicitly and by design: the whole point of the 1999 incorporation was to continue operating the same website and business under a proper corporate structure.

**On what disclosed legal basis and for what consideration?** A formal agreement dated 01-Jan-2000 transferred InterMESH Systems' assets and liabilities to the newly incorporated company effective 31-Dec-1999, with Dinesh Chandra Agarwal receiving 8,000,000 equity shares and 2,000,000 10% non-cumulative redeemable preference shares as consideration — a disclosed, arm's-reasonable, founder-to-own-company capitalization transaction, not a related-party asset-stripping or phoenix pattern.

**Is the predecessor still live and trading on the same brands?** No — it ceased to exist as a distinct operating vehicle once absorbed; there is no live "InterMESH Systems" trading in parallel with IndiaMART InterMESH Limited.

**Verdict: Green, disclosed lineage, no phoenix pattern.** This is exactly the Protocol's own example of a founding-year gap that reconciles cleanly rather than one that stays a finding — the "group founding year" is in substance the SAME person's own venture, formalized with disclosed consideration. The one caveat: this agent's source for the InterMESH Systems mechanics is secondary web company-history summaries (unverified per §5), not the company's own DRHP text (not present in this run's data pool) — flagged in Section 6 as the item worth confirming directly against the DRHP or the AR's own "brief corporate history" narrative if either becomes available in a future pool.

## 6. Provenance & Next Data Request

- **Filing-supplied vs independently discovered:** ~46 of 57 register subjects (~81%) came from the triage's own Person & Entity Register, built from the AR/CIQ extracts. This agent's own sweep independently discovered ~10 further subjects (a predecessor sole-proprietorship, three past directors of an internal venture, a founder-loop subject, a co-director-network person, a dormant subsidiary the triage extract did not enumerate, and one unresolved identity lead) — a coverage statistic, not a finding of company non-disclosure, per the A17-08 rule (Section 4). Zero subjects were user-supplied.
- **Nominee or independent directorships excluded from beneficial-interest scope:** Amit Behl (Board Observer, non-voting, no fiduciary/beneficial-interest role) is the one register subject explicitly excluded from beneficial-interest scope in this run.
- **Aggregator conflict kept in scope and unresolved (A17-10):** the "Voicewaves Technologies" name-match ambiguity (2B.9) is the one unresolved aggregator/identity conflict this run — kept on the register and the Scope-Boundary Register rather than silently dropped or silently asserted.
- **Single highest-value next data request:** **Confirm the current procedural status of the Feb-2021 Govardhan Shila FIR (IPC §295 / IT Act §66D) against founder-CEO Dinesh Chandra Agarwal and co-founder Brijesh Kumar Agrawal** — specifically, whether it was ever chargesheeted, discharged, quashed by the Allahabad High Court, or remains formally open — and whether it is disclosed anywhere in the company's own Board's Report "proceedings against directors" statement. This single fact governs both this agent's A16-04 grade for the two most powerful people at the company and the A9-10/A17-08 disclosure-reconciliation question `12` will need to close; everything else in this network reads clean.

## 7. People & Network Integrity Read

Of the 33 people swept, 31 are Clean and 2 — the founder-CEO and co-founder-WTD, who together control roughly 49% of the company and run it day to day — carry Material concerns on their own record: an apparently still-unresolved 2021 criminal FIR (a religious-offense charge tied to third-party marketplace content, not an economic-fraud allegation) that this sweep could not confirm was ever formally closed or explicitly reconciled against the company's own litigation disclosures. Per the allegation-versus-conviction rule this caps conviction — no rating above Watchlist until the matter is resolved by primary evidence — without tripping the hard gate, since nothing in this sweep rises to an adjudicated or proven fact. The discovery loop reached hop 2 (through the Livekeeping/Finlite founder chain) and terminated on `no_new_subjects`: Phase 1 ran in full except for the trademark-proprietor search (D-4, deferred to budget — Scope-Boundary #1), and Phase 2 (the founder loop) ran on both corroborated E-A entities with an external founder, returning nothing adverse on either. The one genuine network surprise is a small, dormant 2008-vintage subsidiary (Hello Trade Online Private Limited) founded by both promoters at the company's own registered address, which the triage's own extract did not enumerate — plausibly an ordinary AOC-1-schedule gap in this agent's source coverage rather than a concealment, but not yet confirmed either way. A rational minority holder should read this network as clean apart from two connected items worth resolving before raising conviction: the founders' FIR status, and the dormant-subsidiary disclosure question — both are closable with a single targeted follow-up each, and neither, on the evidence gathered here, points toward the kind of proven fraud, defaulter listing, or phoenix pattern that would justify walking away outright.

# Audit & Assurance Quality — INDIAMART

**Hard-lock deference check:** `business-model/01_disqualifier-scan.md` (INDIAMART_2026-08-22 run) found **no audit-related hard disqualifier**: "Auditor's opinion is 'Unqualified' for FY 2024, FY 2025, and FY 2026, all issued by Bharat S Raut & Co – BSR & Co (BSR & Co. LLP)... No going-concern qualification found" and "Same auditor... for FY 2024, FY 2025, and FY 2026 — no change at all in the last 3 years" and "No prior-period-error/restatement note found" [`business-model/01_disqualifier-scan.md`, Section 1, items 1, 4, 5]. No deference band applies to any item below; this report is the primary, unqualified read.

**Partial-data check:** the auditor-fee note (Note 24, "Payment to Auditors") shows only an "As auditor" line and a reimbursement line for the standalone entity, which triage flagged as a possible gap [`00_governance-data-triage.md`, Section 3]. This agent's own read of the Corporate Governance Report finds the FULL required breakdown (Statutory Audit Fee / Other Services Fee / Other Certification Fees / Out-of-pocket reimbursement, covering the Company **and its subsidiaries and the whole B S R network**) in a separate LODR Schedule V disclosure — see Section 4. **A4-06 and A4-07 are therefore answerable, not Not Applicable**; the triage's caution is resolved, not overridden silently, and is recorded as a correction here.

**Read:** every opinion in the pool, standalone AND consolidated, is unqualified across FY2024–FY2026, with no divergence between the two sets of statements — the exact case CLAUDE.md and this module's brief flag as a required check, and here it clears clean. The one disclosed IFC carve-out (4 unaudited associate companies) is explicitly sized by the auditor as immaterial to the Holding Company, and does not by itself trip the A4-09 red band ("IFC of material components not audited" — these are stated to be non-material). No restatement in the pool; the one prior-period adjustment found is a court-sanctioned merger-accounting effect, correctly distinguished from an error correction, and immaterial in size (₹70.32mn against ~₹13,340mn FY25 revenue).

Batliboi) and incoming firm (KPMG-network B S R & Co.) are different networks, not an affiliate swap; the second-term dates are explicitly disclosed against numbered AGMs, which is the opposite of an obscured extension | FY26 Annual Report, Board's Report "AUDITORS" section, p.98–99; CIQ Auditors.xls |

LLP — KPMG-network firm with a national listed-company audit practice; holds a valid SEBI-mandated peer-review certificate (disclosed in the Board's Report) | Big-4 / peer-reviewed national firm, listed-company practice sized to this company | A firm too small for the complexity, or a firm/partner with a disciplinary history | **Green**, with a noted qualifier — see A4-13: routine regulator inspection has flagged firm-level quality-control gaps, which tempers a pure "Big-4-brand = quality" read per this module's own instruction that the brand is not itself quality | FY26 Annual Report, Board's Report, p.98–99; web sweep below |

LLP (FRN 101248W/W-100022) or against David Jones (ICAI Membership 098113)** in the sources reachable by this sweep. NFRA HAS published routine, non-punitive **Audit Quality Inspection Reports** on this exact firm (2022, 2023 — released Dec-2024, and an on-site inspection of the "B S R & Affiliates" network in Nov-2025), which found: leadership-structure documentation and independence-policy improvements noted as positive, BUT deficiencies flagged in one FY22/FY23 engagement on related-party-transaction testing and Sec 143(1)(e)/185 compliance, and — from the Nov-2025 on-site review — "instances of confirmation bias in fraud risk assessment and inadequate supervision of audit workpapers." These are **quality-inspection findings under Sec 132(2), not enforcement orders**, and none is stated to relate to the IndiaMART engagement specifically. **Separately**, a different, distinctly-registered network-sibling firm — **B S R & Associates LLP, FRN 116231W/W-100024** (a different legal registration from IndiaMART's own auditor) — was fined ₹10 crore by NFRA in 2024 for the Coffee Day Enterprises (CDEL) FY2018-19 audit, with engagement partner CA Aravind Maiya (Membership 217433, unrelated to David Jones) debarred 10 years and CA Amit Somani (Membership 060154) debarred 5 years. This is **not** IndiaMART's auditor entity or partner, but it is the same "B S R" / KPMG-India family of firms and is relevant network-quality context, stated here precisely so it is not conflated with a finding against the actual engagement firm | No orders against either | Any active order or debarment (also RF-AUD-007) | **Amber** — no order or debarment against the specific firm/partner (does not trip RF-AUD-007), but NOT a clean "swept and nothing found" either: the same-FRN firm has a documented, recent history of NFRA quality-control observations, and its immediate network family has one high-severity enforcement action against a sibling entity. Recorded as Amber, not Green, and not Red | Web: taxguru.in "NFRA Inspection Report 2023: Audit Review of M/s BSR & Co. LLP" (2026, undated republication of the Dec-2024 NFRA report) — unverified secondary source, primary NFRA report cited by name; Web: taxguru.in / theauditacademy.com / india-briefing.com, "NFRA imposes monetary penalty of Rs. 10 crores on BSR & Associates LLP" (Sep-2024) — unverified secondary sources reporting a primary NFRA order (NF-23/14/2022 dated 17-Jan-2024); Web: nfra.gov.in, "Order in the matter of Coffee Day Enterprises Ltd (CDEL)... against M/s BSR & Associates LLP" — primary regulator source, all dated 2026-08-22 (search date) |

**Sweep note:** this is a scoped single-firm/partner lookup per `frameworks/GOVERNANCE_DATABASES.md` ("Who this binds" — agent 08 owes no Discovery Register for this kind of check). No ICAI Disciplinary Directorate hit was found for "B S R & Co" or "David Jones" in the reachable web search results; ICAI's own disciplinary case database (disc.icai.org) was not directly queryable through this sweep, so ICAI coverage is **coverage-limited: disc.icai.org unreachable via this run's tools, 2026-08-22** — confidence on the ICAI leg is capped accordingly (see Sweep Log).

**Read:** this is the strongest single finding in this report. The Reg 34/Schedule V table — a disclosure the triage agent had not located — settles the fee-independence question definitively for FY26: zero non-audit fees, zero certification fees, paid to the auditor OR any entity in its network, at either the standalone company or the consolidated group. No prohibited service under Companies Act Sec 144 is evidenced anywhere in the pool. The audit fee itself has risen faster than revenue in both of the last two years — the opposite of the "low-balling" red band. The only open question is the fee LEVEL against a genuine peer set, which this pool cannot answer (no comparable Indian-listed company's audit-fee note is in the data pool); this is recorded as Insufficient Data on that single leg, not guessed.

| Principal auditor's share of consolidated assets/revenue (A4-14) | Assets: **~96.0%** covered directly by B S R & Co. LLP (100% − 3.98%); Revenue: **~99.5%** covered directly (100% − 0.46%) | ≥80% | <50%, or cash/revenue-rich components at unknown firms | **Green** — comfortably above the 80% floor on both legs, and the unaudited-by-principal slice is neither cash-rich nor revenue-rich in absolute or relative terms | Computed from the figures above |
| Material subs' auditor calibre (A4-03) | The 4 subsidiaries audited by "other auditors" are collectively immaterial (3.98% of assets, 0.46% of revenue) — none individually named as a "material subsidiary" in the pool, and none appears in the Reg 24(1) list of subsidiaries requiring an IndiaMART-nominee independent director | Parent auditor or comparable firms | Unknown firms, or components left unaudited | **Amber** — not the Wirecard/Satyam pattern (no cash-or-revenue-rich component is hidden from the principal auditor), but the firm NAMES are genuinely absent from every document in this pool, which is a real disclosure-completeness gap on a checklist item this module is required to answer, not a Not Applicable | FY26 Annual Report, "Other Matters" paragraph; Note 33(a) subsidiary/associate list |

**Read:** component coverage by the principal auditor is strong on both the asset and revenue legs — this is not a structure where value could hide in an unaudited or unknown-firm corner the way it did at Wirecard or IL&FS. The one genuine gap: this pool never names WHO the "other auditors" are for the four small subsidiaries, or for the eight associates whose own CARO reports were still outstanding at the date of the principal auditor's report. Given the immateriality of the amounts involved, this does not rise to a Red finding, but it is recorded as the finding it is — an Amber disclosure-completeness gap — rather than silently passed over.

Accordingly, clause 3(xviii) of the Order is not applicable" | N/A | N/A | N/A | FY26 Annual Report, Annexure A to the Independent Auditor's Report, clause (xviii) |

**A4-04 verdict: Green.** No mid-term auditor resignation has occurred at any point in the pool's history (FY2015–FY2026); the one auditor CHANGE (S.R. Batliboi → B S R & Co., around the FY2019/FY2020 transition, coinciding with IPO) was a scheduled appointment against numbered AGMs, not a resignation, and the CARO annexure affirmatively states no resignation occurred in the current year.

**A4-15 verdict: Green.** Every gatekeeper change in the 3-year lookback (CFO, Compliance Officer, internal-audit engagement entity) has a named successor, an immediate handover, and — for both KMP-level changes — the outgoing person REMAINED with the company in another role rather than departing. No rolling-12-month cluster of CFO + auditor/CS/internal-auditor exits exists; the CFO and Compliance-Officer changes are ~7 months apart and neither coincides with any auditor event.

| Revenue Recognition — Web Services (standalone AND consolidated) | FY2025, FY2026 (full text confirmed in this pool; CIQ confirms the FIRM was unchanged back to FY2020, but full KAM text for FY2020–FY2024 is not in this pool) | Revenue growing (₹13,200.58mn → ₹14,428.03mn standalone, +9.3%); the KAM's underlying risk (high-volume IT-system-driven recognition) is structural to the subscription-marketplace model, not a deteriorating estimate | Sector-standard for a high-volume subscription/marketplace business — this KAM exists because of transaction VOLUME and IT-system dependency, not because of an aggressive judgment call | **Green** — a stable, well-explained, procedurally-addressed KAM tied to the business model itself | FY26 & FY25 Annual Reports, Key Audit Matters sections |
| Valuation of investments in subsidiaries, associates and other entities (standalone) / associates and other entities (consolidated) | FY2025, FY2026 (same 2-year window) | Investment balances have GROWN (standalone: INR 6,769.53mn + 2,792.20mn + 3,366.10mn as at Mar-2025 → INR 7,270.02mn + 4,222.62mn + 3,243.19mn as at Mar-2026 across the three investment categories) — consistent with the company's active, disclosed strategy of taking new minority stakes (Fleetx, Agillos, Truckhall, Edgewise, etc.), not with a hidden valuation problem | Company-specific — a direct consequence of IndiaMART's active associate/investee strategy, which is itself flagged elsewhere in the business-model module as a governance-relevant capital-allocation pattern (serial minority-stake investing), not an audit red flag on its own | **Amber-leaning-Green** — the KAM recurs and the underlying balances are GROWING, which is the literal condition the A4-10 red band names ("the same high-risk KAM... recurring 3+ years with growing balances"); however, only 2 of the required 3+ years are directly verified in this pool, and the growth reflects new investment activity (a capital-allocation choice already surfaced in `business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance`) rather than a stale investment quietly not being written down. Flagged for continued monitoring, not scored as a Red trigger given the 2-year evidence ceiling | FY26 & FY25 Annual Reports, Key Audit Matters sections; Notes 7 and 8 |

**Coverage caveat:** this pool contains full Key-Audit-Matter text for only 2 of the 3–5 years this module's brief asks for (FY2025 and FY2026); FY2024 and earlier auditor's-report text is not in the pool (CIQ confirms only the opinion TYPE, not KAM content, back to FY2015). The recurrence read above is stated on that 2-year basis, not extrapolated to a third year that cannot be verified.

## 8. CARO, Internal & Secretarial Audit (A4-08, A4-11)

| Test | Finding | Green band | Red band | Verdict | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| A4-11 — CARO annexure | Clean throughout: no fraud noticed or reported by/on the Company [(ix)(a)]; not a declared wilful defaulter [(ix)(b)]; no funds advanced/loaned/invested through intermediaries for round-tripping to ultimate beneficiaries [clause under Rule 11(e)]; no whistleblower complaints received [(xi)(c)]; no resignation of statutory auditors [(xviii)]; no cash losses [(xvii)]; no unrecorded income surrendered in tax assessments [(viii)]; no qualifications or adverse remarks in ANY subsidiary's own CARO report per B S R & Co.'s review of the other auditors' reports | Clean | Related-party loans, fund diversion, unrecorded transactions, benami items (RF-AUD-003) | **Green** — no adverse CARO finding anywhere in the standalone or consolidated annexures | FY26 Annual Report, Annexure A to the Independent Auditor's Report (Standalone), clauses (viii), (ix), (xi), (xvii), (xviii), (xxi) |
| A4-08 — internal audit function | M/s BDO India LLP → BDO India Services Private Limited (BTA transfer, same terms), described as conducting "an extensive internal audit program"; Board's Report states "no material weaknesses were observed" from internal audits during the year | Reputable internal auditor in place | No internal-audit function | **Green** | FY26 Annual Report, Board's Report, p.98–99; MD&A internal-controls section |
| A4-08 — secretarial audit (MR-3) | RMG & Associates, Company Secretaries (peer-reviewed PCS firm, shareholder-appointed 26th AGM, first 5-year term compliant with the Dec-2024 LODR term-cap amendment). The Board's Report states the MR-3 "does not contain any qualification, reservation, adverse remarks or disclaimer." **However**, the MR-3's own body text records two Designated-Person compliance lapses under the SEBI Insider Trading Regulations during the audit period: (1) a Designated Person pledged Company equity shares without seeking pre-clearance from the Compliance Officer; (2) a different Designated Person traded ONE equity share during a closed trading window. Both were self-reported and intimated to the stock exchanges. **The Annual Secretarial Compliance Report (the separate ≤60-day exchange filing) is not in this data pool**, so its filing timeliness cannot be verified from this run | Unqualified MR-3; peer-reviewed, shareholder-appointed; compliance report ≤60 days | Adverse/repeat secretarial remarks two years running, MR-3 absent where mandated, or a late/missing compliance report | **Green on the formal opinion** (the Board's own characterization of "no qualification... adverse remarks" stands, and these are self-reported procedural PIT lapses by unnamed individuals, not adverse remarks on the Company's compliance framework itself) — **with the two lapses recorded as a noted finding**, not scored as Red (does not meet the "adverse/repeat... two years running" threshold — this is the first and only year these two specific lapses are disclosed). **ASCR filing-timeliness leg: Not Applicable (no data)** — the compliance-report filing itself is not in this pool | FY26 Annual Report, Board's Report "AUDITORS" section p.98–99; Secretarial Audit Report (MR-3), Annexure-5 |

For a non-India company this row-set would use the local equivalent; INDIAMART is an India-domiciled, India-listed company (NSE: INDIAMART / BSE: 542726), so the CARO / MR-3 framework applies directly — no substitution needed.

## 9. Audit Committee ↔ Auditor Engagement (A4-12)

| Signal | Evidence | Verdict | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| Private sessions (committee meets auditor without management) | The Audit Committee's disclosed Terms of Reference include item 16: "Discussion with statutory auditors before the audit commences, about the nature and scope of audit as well as post-audit discussion to ascertain any area of concern" — this is the MANDATE, not a factual statement that a private session (without management present) was actually held this year. No affirmative statement of a held private session, and no contradicting statement that one was NOT held, appears anywhere in the pool | **Insufficient Data** — mandate exists and is disclosed, but the pool carries no explicit confirmation the session actually occurred this year (silence is not Red per this module's own rule) | FY26 Annual Report, Corporate Governance Report, Audit Committee "Terms of Reference", item 16 |
| Auditor attendance at committee meetings | The Audit Committee met 4 times (29-Apr-2025, 17-Jul-2025, 16-Oct-2025, 19-Jan-2026) with full attendance recorded for all 4 human committee members; no attendee list naming the Statutory Auditors' presence at any specific meeting is disclosed in this pool | **Insufficient Data** — same reasoning; the committee's own meeting-attendance table records only DIRECTOR attendance, not auditor attendance, so this is a genuine gap in what the pool discloses, not evidence either way | FY26 Annual Report, Corporate Governance Report, Audit Committee composition/attendance table |

Composition (A2-01) is `05`'s territory; this section covers only the engagement, per this module's ownership split.

## 10. Read

The assurance chain here is able and independently structured, and the two facts most likely to move a skeptic — the fee relationship and the rotation history — hold up under direct verification: zero non-audit fees to the auditor or its network (a fact the triage agent had not surfaced, found here in the LODR Schedule V table), a genuine cross-network rotation at listing (EY-network out, KPMG-network in), and no auditor resignation across the entire FY2015–FY2026 history this pool covers. CARO is clean on every clause that matters — no fraud, no fund diversion, no wilful-defaulter listing — and every gatekeeper handoff (CFO, Compliance Officer, internal-audit engagement entity) came with an immediate named successor and, in two of three cases, the outgoing person stayed with the company in another role rather than leaving.

The single weakest link is **not disqualifying but real: the identity of the auditors behind ~4% of consolidated assets and eight associate companies is never named in this pool**, and the audit committee's actual engagement with B S R & Co. (private sessions, meeting attendance) is asserted only as a policy mandate, not confirmed as a fact for this year. Layered on top of that: the auditor's own regulator, NFRA, has published routine (non-punitive) quality-inspection findings on this exact firm — documentation gaps, one flagged FY22/23 related-party-transaction test, and a Nov-2025 finding of "confirmation bias in fraud risk assessment" at the network level — and a same-family, differently-registered sibling firm (B S R & Associates LLP) was fined ₹10 crore and had two partners debarred over the Coffee Day Enterprises fraud in 2024. None of this touches IndiaMART's own engagement or its own signing partner on the evidence gathered here, but it means "Big-4-affiliate auditor" should not be read as a clean pass on its own — which is exactly this module's own instruction.

**Conclusion: assurance trustworthy.** No Critical or High red flag fires on any A4/A7-02 item. What would raise confidence further: the actual names of the component auditors for the four small subsidiaries and eight associates, and a specific factual confirmation (not just a policy mandate) that the Audit Committee held its private session with B S R & Co. this year.

## management-governance / 09_related-party-and-group-forensics.md

_Source: `09_related-party-and-group-forensics.md`_

# Related-Party & Group Forensics — INDIAMART

**Jurisdiction / regime:** India — SEBI Listing Obligations and Disclosure Requirements (LODR) Regulations 2015 + Companies Act 2013, Ind AS 24 for related-party disclosure. Reporting currency: INR million (₹mn). Fiscal year ends 31 March. All figures below are taken verbatim from the FY26 Integrated Annual Report (filed 02-Jun-2026, Note 33 "Related party transactions," Note 34 "Group information," Note 37 "Scheme of Amalgamation") and the FY25 Annual Report (filed 29-Apr-2025, for the FY24 comparative column), cross-checked against `business-model/01_disqualifier-scan.md`, `earnings/01_historical-financials.md`, and `management-governance/07_people-integrity-dossiers.md`. No `ciq_facts.json` or `relationships.json` sidecar exists for this run's `_pool_extracts/` folder — every figure below is this agent's own sourced read of the primary filing text.

## 1. RPT Intensity & the Applicable Regime (A5-01)

**Applicable materiality rule per period:** all three years read (FY24, FY25, FY26) fall under the **earlier** LODR Reg 23 regime — the lower of 10% of consolidated turnover or ₹1,000 crore — because the graded-slab regime (10% of turnover up to ₹20,000cr turnover, tapering to a ₹5,000cr hard cap) applies only from December 2025 and IndiaMART's FY26 (ended 31-Mar-2026) turnover (₹15,690.42mn / ~₹1,569cr) sits far below either the old ₹1,000cr cap or the new slab's ₹20,000cr breakpoint — the two regimes converge to the same practical outcome (10% of turnover) for a company this size, so the choice of regime does not change any verdict below. Both are named for completeness. [FY26 Annual Report, Note 33; `earnings/01_historical-financials.md` §1]

| FY | Aggregate RPTs ex-dividends (core recurring flows) | + KMP compensation | + Associate investment/loan flows | % of revenue (core only) | % of revenue (incl. all three) | Material-RPT line that year | Above the line? | Minority-approved? | Verdict | Source |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---|---|---|---|---|
| FY24 | ₹27.83mn | ~₹150–200mn (not independently re-extracted this pass; OCR-degraded in the FY25 AR's KMP-compensation table for the FY24 column — flagged as a coverage gap, not a bad-extraction guess) | ₹247.36mn (Truckhall ₹30.00 + IB Monotaro ₹137.36 + Mobisy ₹80.00, investment in associates) | 0.23% | ~2.3–4.0% (range reflects the KMP-comp gap) | Lower of 10% turnover / ₹1,000cr (≈₹1,197mn) | No | N/A (below Reg 23 threshold) | **Green** | FY25 Annual Report (29-Apr-2025), Note 33 |
| FY25 | ₹30.92mn | ₹185.07mn | ₹210.62mn (Truckhall ₹100.00 + Edgewise ₹50.00 investment; Mobisy loan ₹30.00 + interest ₹0.62 + repayment ₹30.00) | 0.22% | 3.30% | Lower of 10% turnover / ₹1,000cr (≈₹1,388mn) | No | N/A (below Reg 23 threshold) | **Green** | FY26 Annual Report (02-Jun-2026), Note 33 (FY25 comparative column) |
| FY26 | ₹37.05mn | ₹202.98mn | ₹410.00mn (Fleetx Technologies investment in associate) | 0.24% | 4.14% | Lower of 10% turnover / ₹1,569mn = ₹156.9mn(10%) vs ₹1,000cr cap → ₹156.9mn governs; new Dec-2025 slab (10% up to ₹20,000cr) → ₹1,569mn — **either way the actual RPT total (₹650.03mn incl. all three) is below the higher figure and the "core" total (₹37.05mn) is trivially below both** | No | N/A (below Reg 23 threshold; Corporate Governance Report states explicitly: "During FY 2026, there were no material related party transactions") | **Green** | FY26 Annual Report, Note 33; Corporate Governance Report, "Other Disclosures — Material Related Party Transactions" |

Green band (<1–5% of revenue, documented arm's length): **all three years clear it comfortably even on the most inclusive basis** (0.22–4.14% of revenue). No year approaches the 5–15% Red band, and none is remotely near 25% — **this does not engage `business-model/01_disqualifier-scan`'s hard lock; that scan independently computed the BRSR-disclosed ratios (Purchases 0.72% FY26/0.80% FY25 of total purchases; Sales 0.11%/0.13% of total sales) and found "N — Not Triggered," which this agent's own revenue-basis computation corroborates rather than contradicts** [`business-model/01_disqualifier-scan.md` §1, item 3]. This agent defers to that scan's lock status and does not re-adjudicate it.

These are intra-group flows between the listco and its own wholly/majority-owned subsidiaries — **not a leakage channel** (no third party or unconsolidated entity captures value), and are noted for ledger completeness only.

No counterparty is left unnamed as an "entity where KMP exercise influence" without disclosure — every counterparty in the transaction table above is individually named (A5-08 Green; see §5).

## 3. Promoter-Linked Channels

### 3A. Royalty / brand / technology fees (A5-02)

**None found.** IndiaMART trades under its own originated "IndiaMART" marketplace brand (not licensed from a promoter or controller-linked entity) [`07_people-integrity-dossiers.md` §0A]. No royalty, brand-fee, or technology-fee line appears anywhere in Note 33, the CARO annexure, or the Board's Report. Verdict: **Green (None)** — clears the A5-02 green band trivially. `07`'s A17-04 brand-ownership axis found no adverse condition (marks originated or subsidiary-owned; D-4 trademark-proprietor search itself was deferred to budget by `07`, but no licence-fee line exists in the RPT note regardless of proprietor confirmation, so there is no fee to price here) — RF-NET-004 does **not** propagate; there is no licence economics to route to this section because no licence exists. [FY26 AR Note 33; `07_people-integrity-dossiers.md` §0A, Scope-Boundary #1]

### 3B. Loans / ICDs / guarantees to the group (A5-03)

| Instrument | Counterparty | Amount | Rate vs market | Rolled over? | Counterparty health | Verdict | Source |
|---|---|---:|---|---|---|---|---|
| Sec 185/186 loans/guarantees to any body corporate | Any | **Nil** (auditor-confirmed) | N/A | N/A | N/A | **Green** | FY26 AR, CARO Annexure A, clause 3(iv): "has not given any loans, or provided guarantees or securities, as specified under section 185… has not provided any loan, security and guarantees as specified under section 186" |
| Loan to Associate | Mobisy Technologies Private Limited | ₹30.00mn (FY25), fully repaid same year with ₹0.62mn interest disclosed | Interest disclosed, no below-market signal found | No — one-off, repaid | Associate, ongoing operating entity, no distress signal in this pool | **Green** | FY26/FY25 AR Note 33 |

CARO Annexure A clause 3(iv) is auditor-attested evidence — stronger than a mere absent note line — that IndiaMART has extended **no** loans, deposits, or guarantees to promoter or director-controlled entities in FY26. This is confirmed independently by `10_contingent-liabilities-and-commitments.md` §5 (A7a-06, corporate guarantees Green, nil): the same CARO clause is cited there for the guarantees axis, and this section confirms it also covers the loans/ICDs axis. **No RF-RPT-003 trigger.** Cross-reference: guarantees also route to `10` (A7a-06) — flagged here, quantified there ("Nil… 10/10, confidence 5"), counted once, not twice. [FY26 AR, CARO Annexure A, clause 3(iv); `10_contingent-liabilities-and-commitments.md` §5, finding 10-006]

| Entity | Customer / vendor / both | Value (FY26) | % of revenue or COGS | Verdict | Source |
|---|---|---:|---:|---|---|
| Mansa Enterprises Private Limited | Vendor (rent) | ₹6.85mn | 0.04% of revenue | **Green** — trivial, promoter-family real-estate entity paid market-scale rent | AR Note 33 |

No material purchases or sales are routed through a promoter-CONTROLLED (majority-owned, non-listco) private entity that competes with or substitutes for arm's-length trade. The two KMP-influence vendors (Mansa, S R Dinodia) are immaterial in size. **Verdict: Green.**

## 4. Round-Tripping Cross-Year Name Match (A5-04)

One list, all years read (FY24–FY26), names matched across customer / vendor / investment roles:

| Name | Years as customer (revenue to listco) | Years as vendor (expense to listco) | Both roles? | Sales ≈ purchases (circular)? | Verdict | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Mansa Enterprises Private Limited | Never | FY24, FY25, FY26 (rent) | No | N/A | **Green — vendor only, no match** | AR Note 33, 3 years |
| S R Dinodia & Co LLP | Never | FY24, FY25, FY26 (professional fees) | No | N/A | **Green — vendor only, no match** | AR Note 33, 3 years |
| Simply Vyapar Apps Private Limited | FY24, FY25, FY26 (marketing services) | Never (only capital contribution as an associate investment, not an operating purchase) | Investment ≠ purchase — **No genuine two-way trade** | N/A | **Green — customer only for operating purposes; the investment leg is capital allocation, not a purchase, so this is not a customer/vendor match** | AR Note 33, 3 years |
| IB Monotaro Private Limited | FY24, FY25, FY26 (marketing services) | Never | No | N/A | **Green** | AR Note 33, 3 years |
| Mobisy Technologies Private Limited | Never (operating) | FY25 only (loan, fully repaid same year with interest) | No — a loan is not a purchase | N/A | **Green** | AR Note 33, FY25 |
| Truckhall, Edgewise, IB Monotaro (FY24), Mobisy (FY24), Fleetx | Never (operating) | Never (operating) — investment legs only | No | N/A | **Green** — all associate-investment legs, no operating purchase/sale on either side | AR Note 33 |

**No counterparty appears as BOTH an operating customer AND an operating vendor across any of the three years read.** The closest candidate (Simply Vyapar, IB Monotaro) sit only on the revenue side; their capital lines are IndiaMART's own equity/CCPS SUBSCRIPTIONS into the associate (an asset on IndiaMART's balance sheet), not a purchase FROM the associate — so there is no mirrored purchase-then-sale leg to test for circularity. **No round-tripping lead found; the DHFL/Gensol pattern (money leaving as a purchase and returning as a sale through the same counterparty) does not appear anywhere in this ledger.** Verdict: **Green, no match, nothing to escalate.**

## 5. Approval & Disclosure Hygiene (A5-05, A5-06, A5-07, A5-08)

| ID | Test | Raw finding | Band | Verdict | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| A5-05 | Arm's-length substantiation | "The transactions with related parties are entered on terms equivalent to those that prevail in arm's length transactions" — a bare assertion; no benchmarking methodology (comparable-pricing study, third-party quote, valuer's report) disclosed anywhere in the pool | Bare assertion with no methodology = Red band per the checklist test, but the underlying transactions are all immaterial (largest single line ₹6.85mn rent) | **Amber** — hygiene gap on disclosure, not a leakage finding given the sizes involved; the checklist's literal test ("a bare assertion is not evidence") is not met, but materiality caps the practical concern | FY26 AR Note 33, "Terms and conditions of transactions with related parties" |
| A5-06 | Minority dissent on RPT resolutions | The ONE RPT-adjacent shareholder resolution voted on in the FY26 governance year — "To approve related party transaction in respect to hold Ordinary office or place of profit in the Company by Mr. Amit Agarwal" (a relative of KMP, per Note 33(c)), Special Resolution, Postal Ballot, result declared 29-Aug-2025 — received **21.72% votes AGAINST** (Votes in Favour 26,581,673 / 78.2829%; Votes Against 7,374,238 / 21.7171%) | >20% against trips the Red band explicitly | **Red** | FY26 AR, "Postal Ballot" section, p.124–125 of the Integrated Annual Report; also cross-referenced in `07_people-integrity-dossiers.md` §2.6 |
| A5-07 | RPT approval hygiene | "All the Related Party Transactions were approved by the Independent Directors of the Committee in terms of Listing Regulations" — the Audit Committee (Vivek Narayan Gour [ID, Chair], Pallavi Dinodia Gupta [ID], Sandeep Kumar Barasia [ID], Rajesh Sawhney [ID]; Dhruv Prakash, a non-independent Non-Executive Director, sits on the committee for other purposes but is explicitly excluded from RPT votes) — consistent with Reg 23(2)-(3)'s only-independent-members rule. Omnibus-approval criteria laid down per the Board-approved RPT Policy, reviewed/modified 20-Jan-2026. **Slab-slicing test:** same-counterparty totals summed per year — Mansa Enterprises (₹6.85mn FY26), S R Dinodia (₹1.70mn FY26), and every associate/subsidiary line — none approach even the ₹1cr (₹10mn) "unforeseen transaction" omnibus sub-threshold, let alone the Reg 23 material-RPT line; no evidence of transactions split across periods or entities to stay under a threshold | Only-ID voting confirmed; no slicing found | **Green** | FY26 AR, Corporate Governance Report, Audit Committee section, p.114–115 |
| A5-08 | RPT counterparty transparency | Every counterparty in Note 33 is individually named (Mansa Enterprises, S R Dinodia & Co LLP, each associate/subsidiary by name, each KMP/relative by name) — no material transaction is attributed to an unnamed "entity where KMP exercise influence" | Every counterparty named | **Green** | FY26 AR Note 33 |

**Slab-slicing result:** the sums checked were Mansa Enterprises (₹6.85mn FY26 vs FY25 ₹6.12mn vs FY24 ₹5.34mn — a rising trend but each year's total, not sliced sub-transactions), S R Dinodia & Co LLP (₹1.70/₹0.96/₹1.60mn), and the associate-investment lines (each associate's annual total, not split across multiple approvals within a year). No same-counterparty total in any year approaches even 1% of the ₹1,000cr / ₹1,569mn Reg 23 threshold, so slicing to dodge a threshold is not a plausible reading of this data — nothing here is close enough to a threshold to be worth slicing.

## 8. Group-Structure Map (A11-01)

| Metric | Value | Band | Verdict | Source |
|---|---:|---|---|---|
| Layers, listco → deepest entity | **2** (e.g. IndiaMART → Biztradeshows → PlayCez Technologies; IndiaMART → Livekeeping Technologies → Livekeeping Private Limited [struck off]) | ≤2 Green | **Green** | CIQ Corporate Structure Tree; AR Note 33/34 |
| Total group entities (subsidiaries + associates + JVs) | **17** (9 subsidiaries: Tradezeal, Busy Infotech/fka Tolexo, Pay With Indiamart, Livekeeping Technologies/fka Finlite, Livekeeping Private Limited [struck off], IIL Digital, Biztradeshows, PlayCez, IndiaMART Finance Limited; + 8 associates: Simply Vyapar, Truckhall, Fleetx, Agillos, Edgewise, IB Monotaro, Adansa, Mobisy) + 1 administered trust (Indiamart Employee Benefit Trust) | Single-segment marketplace: typically <20 Green | **Green** — proportionate to a company running one core marketplace business plus adjacent SaaS/fintech bolt-ons | AR Note 33/34; CIQ Corporate Structure Tree |
| Offshore entities without evident purpose | **0** — every subsidiary and associate is India-incorporated | 0 = Green | **Green** | AR Note 34 (all "Country of incorporation: India") |
| Entities that only move money | **0** — no SPE/treasury-only vehicle found; every entity has a stated operating purpose (business facilitation, payments, software, SaaS logistics/inventory tools, or the new finance subsidiary explicitly built to extend user working-capital credit) | 0 = Green | **Green** | AR Note 34; `07_people-integrity-dossiers.md` §2B |

**Narrative — what each layer is for:**
- Tradezeal Online Private Limited — "Business facilitation services" (100% subsidiary), itself holds minority stakes in Agillos, Truckhall, Edgewise, Adansa on IndiaMART's behalf [FY26 AR, Directors' Report, "As at March 31, 2026, Tradezeal Online Private Limited… holds equity interests in the associates"].
- Busy Infotech Private Limited (fka Tolexo) — internally launched B2B wholesale marketplace, now trades under the acquired "BUSY" accounting-software brand; goodwill (₹4,122.34mn) is a disclosed Key Audit Matter, routed to `08`/`11`.
- Livekeeping Technologies Private Limited (fka Finlite) — a genuine third-party acquisition (51.09% stake, unrelated founder), fintech/expense-tracking software; goodwill (₹420.38mn) also a KAM.
- Livekeeping Private Limited — an intermediate holding layer under Livekeeping Technologies, struck off 29-Oct-2024 as routine post-acquisition cleanup (see §13).
- Pay With Indiamart Private Limited — payments-facilitation subsidiary.
- IIL Digital, Biztradeshows/PlayCez — business-facilitation and event-listing subsidiaries.
- IndiaMART Finance Limited — new (Aug-2026) wholly-owned subsidiary set up to extend working-capital credit to platform users, a strategic-scope expansion into credit risk already flagged in `business-model/12_red-flags-sweep` (severity 40/100) — a business-model matter, not a leakage or opacity finding; noted here because it is the newest layer of the group.
- Eight associates (Simply Vyapar, Truckhall, Fleetx, Agillos, Edgewise, IB Monotaro, Adansa, Mobisy) — minority-stake SaaS/e-commerce/fintech investments in the seller-ecosystem IndiaMART's marketplace customers use (inventory management, accounting software, fleet logistics), each with a stated operating business and third-party co-investors.

No layer exists solely to move money, hold debt off the consolidated balance sheet, or obscure ownership. **Overall: Green.**

No related **financial** entity (bank, NBFC, or finance company) currently holds group cash to test the equity-interest/regulatory-supervision/cap-utilisation disclosure requirement — IndiaMART Finance Limited did not exist during the period read. | **Hello Trade Online Private Limited** — the one item `07` flagged `obligation-unclear` and asked `09`/`12` to close | Historically yes (pre-merger); post-1-Apr-2023 it is a merged/dissolved entity, not a transacting counterparty | **RESOLVED THIS PASS: yes.** AR Note 37 "Scheme of Amalgamation" (disclosed in both the FY25 AR, originally, and repeated in the FY26 AR as the comparative-year note) explicitly names "Hello Trade Online Private Limited ('Hello Trade' or 'Transferor Company 2')" as one of two subsidiaries merged into Tolexo Online Private Limited (the Transferee Company, subsequently renamed Busy Infotech Private Limited) under an NCLT Chandigarh Bench-sanctioned Composite Scheme of Amalgamation, appointed date 1-Apr-2023, order dated 17-Jan-2025 | **`disclosable-and-disclosed`** — closes `07`'s open item. `07`'s registry-mirror finding that Hello Trade shows as "Active (dormant)" as of 2026-08-22 reflects a stale third-party web mirror lagging the registry's own reflection of a court-sanctioned 2025 amalgamation order, not a live undisclosed entity | The obligation IS engaged (Companies Act merger disclosure) AND it IS met — RF-PPL-005 does **not** propagate for this entity |

**Reconciliation outcome: zero `disclosable-and-omitted` entities.** Every registry-derived entity that engages a named disclosure obligation AND clears a materiality threshold is found in the filings. The one entity `07` could not resolve (Hello Trade Online Private Limited) is resolved here by reading AR Note 37 directly — it is not a live undisclosed subsidiary but a dissolved, merged-away entity whose merger itself is disclosed. **RF-PPL-005 does not propagate from this reconciliation.**

## 14. Read

Roughly ₹37–650mn a year moves through named related-party channels against ₹15,690mn of FY26 revenue (0.24% on the narrowest, operating-only basis; 4.14% on the widest basis that includes a one-off ₹410mn minority investment in an associate) — the single largest channel every year is capital allocation into associate stakes (Fleetx ₹410mn FY26, Truckhall+Edgewise ₹150mn FY25, Truckhall+IB Monotaro+Mobisy ₹247mn FY24), not operating leakage, and that money buys minority equity stakes in third-party-founded businesses, not payments to promoter pockets. The two genuinely promoter-adjacent flows — rent to Mansa Enterprises and fees to S R Dinodia & Co LLP — total under ₹9mn a year, immaterial against any denominator, though both rest on a bare "arm's length" assertion with no disclosed benchmarking (an Amber hygiene gap, not a leakage finding). The group structure (17 entities, 2 layers, zero offshore shells, zero SPEs, net cash concentrated 80.76% at the parent) would not conceal leakage even if it existed — nothing here is opaque enough to hide value transfer. The one item worth a company follow-up is not RPT quantification at all but the 21.72% shareholder dissent against the Amit Agarwal office-of-profit resolution (a real, above-threshold minority pushback this agent found in the AGM record) — routed to `05` for the shareholder-rights read, since the underlying amount at stake (his remuneration line, ₹10.14mn FY26, inside the broader "relatives of KMP" total) is itself immaterial to leakage but the dissent level says a fifth of voting minority shareholders were unconvinced by the appointment. The single disclosure this reconciliation would most want confirmed directly (rather than inferred from a stale web mirror) is whether Hello Trade Online Private Limited's MCA registry status has been updated to reflect its 2025 NCLT-sanctioned dissolution — this agent resolved the disclosure question from the Annual Report's own text, but the registry-status update itself remains unconfirmed.

## management-governance / 10_contingent-liabilities-and-commitments.md

_Source: `10_contingent-liabilities-and-commitments.md`_

# Contingent Liabilities & Commitments — INDIAMART

**Reporting currency: INR million, consolidated, Ind AS (Indian Accounting Standards) unless marked standalone. Fiscal year ends 31 March** (FY26 = year ended 31-Mar-2026). Jurisdiction: India, SEBI-LODR regime. Primary sources read directly for this report: FY26 Annual Report (`IndiaMART_InterMESH_Limited_-_Form_Annual_Report(Apr-30-2026).pdf`), Note 36 "Contingent liabilities and commitments" (consolidated, p.4367 text-stream / printed Note 36) and Note 35 (standalone); Annexure A to the Independent Auditor's Report (CARO 2020), clause 3(vii)(b) disputed statutory dues, printed p.9 of the annexure; and the equivalent notes in the FY25 Annual Report (`IndiaMART_InterMESH_Limited_-_Form_Annual_Report(Apr-29-2025).pdf`) for the FY24/FY25 comparatives, each verified visually page-by-page against the underlying scanned PDF (pdftotext's character-level OCR was unreliable on these particular tables — verified via direct page rendering).

**Cross-module input.** `balance-sheet-survival/05_off-balance-sheet-and-contingencies.md` (prior run, dated 2026-08-14) supplied the FY26 solvency-lens quantification (total max exposure ₹521.86mn, 2.17% of equity) — read here, not recomputed. This module independently re-derived the same figures directly from the primary filing (Note 36 and CARO Annexure A) and confirms an exact match; the governance lens below adds the item-by-item movement, disclosure-completeness, and provisioning-candor read that `05` does not cover.

---

## 1. Headline Exposure (A7a-01)

| Measure | Amount | Denominator (figure + source) | % | Band (Green <5% NW / Red >25% NW or >50% PAT) | Verdict | Source |
|---|---:|---|---:|---|---|---|
| Total contingent liabilities (consolidated, FY26) | ₹521.86mn | Net worth (total equity) = ₹24,003.75mn [FY26 Annual Report, Consolidated Balance Sheet] | 2.17% | Green | Green | [FY26 AR, Note 36(a); Consolidated Balance Sheet] |
| Total contingent liabilities (consolidated, FY26) | ₹521.86mn | PAT = ₹4,746.82mn [FY26 Annual Report, Statement of Changes in Equity — retained-earnings movement, cross-checked to Net Profit ratio 30.25% × Revenue ₹15,690.42mn] | 11.00% | Green (<50% PAT) | Green | [FY26 AR, Note 36(a); ratio note] |
| Total contingent liabilities (consolidated, FY25 comparative) | ₹521.86mn | Net worth FY25 = ₹21,852.90mn [FY26 AR comparative balance sheet] | 2.39% | Green | Green | [FY26 AR, Note 36(a) comparative column] |
| Total contingent liabilities (consolidated, FY24, per FY25 AR comparative) | ₹318.06mn (₹302.68mn + ₹15.38mn) | Net worth FY24 = ₹17,361.14mn [FY25 AR comparative balance sheet] | 1.83% | Green | Green | [FY25 AR, Note 36(a) comparative column] |
| Total contingent liabilities (standalone, FY26) | ₹219.18mn | Net worth (standalone) FY26 = ₹25,417.13mn [FY26 AR, Standalone Balance Sheet] | 0.86% | Green | Green | [FY26 AR, Note 35(a); Standalone Balance Sheet] |
| Total capital commitments (consolidated, separate line) | ₹3.64mn (FY26) / ₹3.26mn (FY25) / ₹8.39mn (FY24) | Net worth FY26 = ₹24,003.75mn | ~0.015% | Green | Green | [FY26 AR, Note 36(b); FY25 AR, Note 36(b)] |

Total consolidated maximum exposure is small on every measure and clears Green by a wide margin. Two of the four quantified items (₹302.68mn income-tax demand at subsidiary level) have sat completely unchanged for three straight balance-sheet dates (FY24, FY25, FY26); the other (₹219.18mn GST/service-tax) jumped 14.2× in FY25 and then also froze at an unchanged headline figure in FY26 — Section 2 below shows why that flat FY25→FY26 line is not the same thing as "nothing happened."

---

## 2. Item-by-Item Register & Movement (A7a-02, A7a-13)

| Item (as named in the note) | FY24 (as at 31-Mar-24) | FY25 (as at 31-Mar-25) | FY26 (as at 31-Mar-26) | Additions / Reversals / Crystallized | Posture change (forum, last outcome) | Explained in narrative? | Verdict | Source |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|---|---|---|---|
| (i) Income-tax demand — Busy Infotech (formerly Tolexo Online), AY2016-17 + AY2017-18, securities-premium addition | ₹302.68mn | ₹302.68mn | ₹302.68mn | No addition, no reversal, no crystallization in 3 years | Pending with CIT(Appeals) throughout; no ruling disclosed in any of the 3 years | Yes — same narrative repeated each year, unchanged | Green (stable, explained; slow-moving, first appellate level) | [FY26 AR Note 36(a)(a)-(b); FY25 AR Note 36(a)(a)-(b)] |
| Service tax/GST demand — combined line, standalone-level items | ₹15.38mn | ₹219.18mn (+1,324%) | ₹219.18mn (flat) | FY25: large ADDITION (new ₹203.80mn TRAN-1 GST matter layered onto the pre-existing ₹15.38mn service-tax penalty). FY26: no addition/reversal in the headline number | **FY25→FY26 the headline is flat, but the underlying TRAN-1 matter's appeal was DISMISSED by the Commissioner (Appeals) on 2 April 2026** — a real deterioration in posture on an unchanged number. This is the flat-total trap: the summary line tells a reader "nothing changed" when in fact the company's first appellate defeat on its largest live tax dispute happened in the same window | The FY25 addition IS narratively explained (note (d) describes the TRAN-1 demand in full); the FY26 appellate dismissal IS also disclosed — but only in the note's footnote prose, not reflected in the headline total, which a reader scanning only the summary table would miss | **Amber-to-Red on posture** (deteriorated; disclosed in the footnote, not in the number) — grade the trend on this row, not the total | [FY26 AR Note 36(a)(c)-(d); FY25 AR Note 36(a)(1)-(2)] |
| — sub-item: CESTAT service-tax matter (Finance Act 1994), gross demand incl. 100% penalty | ₹30.76mn demand / ₹15.38mn already deposited (FY25 CARO) | (same demand carried) | ₹30.77mn demand / **₹1.54mn deposited (FY26 CARO)** | **Unexplained**: the amount already deposited against this matter FELL from ₹15.38mn (FY25) to ₹1.54mn (FY26) — a ₹13.84mn drop, 10× — with no narrative anywhere in either annual report explaining a refund, reclassification, or adjustment | No ruling disclosed in either year; matter remains at CESTAT | **Not explained** — no note addresses the deposit change | Amber — unexplained movement in a disputed-dues supporting figure; immaterial in size (~0.06% of FY26 net worth) but a disclosure-consistency gap that deserves a direct question to management | [FY25 AR, CARO Annexure A, p.11 (Finance Act 1994 row); FY26 AR, CARO Annexure A, p.9 (same row)] — both verified by direct page rendering, not OCR text |
| Total consolidated contingent liabilities | ₹318.06mn | ₹521.86mn (+64.1% YoY) | ₹521.86mn (0% YoY) | See above | See above | Yes, per-item | **Amber** — the FY24→FY25 jump is large (+64%) though narratively explained and short of the literal "doubling" red trigger; the FY25→FY26 flat top-line masks a real posture deterioration on the largest item (see row above) — grade the trend item-by-item, never from the total row | [FY26 AR Note 36(a); FY25 AR Note 36(a)] |

**RF-CL-001 does not fire** on the numeric test (growth was 64%, not a doubling, and the total stays at 2.17–2.39% of net worth, far below the 25% Red band) — but the item-level deterioration on the TRAN-1 matter is the more decision-relevant fact than the aggregate, and is carried forward into Sections 3, 4 and 9 below.

---

## 3. Tax Disputes (A7a-03 direct, A7a-04 indirect)

| Dispute | Type (direct / indirect) | Amount (100%-penalty at face) | Appellate level now | Win/loss record so far | Provided? | % of net worth (₹24,003.75mn FY26 [FY26 AR, Consolidated Balance Sheet]) | Verdict | Source |
|---|---|---:|---|---|---|---:|---|---|
| Busy Infotech (fka Tolexo), AY2016-17 — securities-premium addition, tax impact ₹59.69mn | Direct | ₹59.69mn (tax impact of a loss-carry-forward reduction from ₹719.22mn to ₹482.07mn) | CIT(Appeals) — pending, no ruling in 3 years | 0 rulings yet | No (₹0 tax expense accrued) | 0.25% | Green (immaterial, single unresolved level) | [FY26 AR Note 36(a)(a)] |
| Busy Infotech (fka Tolexo), AY2017-18 — securities-premium addition | Direct | ₹242.99mn | Appellate process — pending | 0 rulings yet | No | 1.01% | Green | [FY26 AR Note 36(a)(b)] |
| Service tax — "net gain on sale of current investments," CCR 2004 rule 6(3), FY2013-14 to FY2017-18 | Indirect | ₹30.77mn gross (₹15.38mn principal already provided in FY2019-20 + ₹15.38mn 100% penalty, unprovided) | Tribunal (CESTAT) — company appealed after Commissioner (Appeals) rejected its appeal and imposed the 100% penalty | **1 loss** (Commissioner Appeals rejected the appeal) | Principal only (₹15.38mn); penalty unprovided | 0.06% (unprovided penalty portion) | Amber (one appellate loss already, penalty unprovided; below materiality thresholds in size) | [FY26 AR Note 36(a)(c)] |
| GST — Central GST Commissionerate Noida, TRAN-1 transitional-credit demand + 100% penalty | Indirect | ₹203.80mn (₹101.90mn demand + ₹101.90mn penalty, taken at full face value per the 100%-penalty rule — no haircut applied) | Appellate Tribunal — company "in the process of filing" this appeal as of the FY26 Annual Report | **1 loss** (Commissioner (Appeals), Noida, dismissed the first appeal on 2 April 2026) | No (₹0 provision; a ₹10.19mn partial pre-deposit is recorded as an asset/receivable, not a P&L provision) | 0.85% | **Amber** (single-figure size is immaterial, but this is the company's largest live tax dispute and it has now lost its first round; watch for a second loss — see Section 9) | [FY26 AR Note 36(a)(d); FY26 AR CARO Annexure A row 5] |

Direct-tax total ₹302.68mn = 1.26% of net worth — Green (well under the 15% Red band, and no loss recorded at any level). Indirect-tax total ₹219.18mn (headline) plus the smaller CARO-only items (below) = well under 5% of net worth — Green by size, but Amber by posture on the TRAN-1 item given the loss already sustained. No item anywhere approaches the >15%-of-net-worth or multi-level-loss Red trigger.

**Additional smaller disputed-dues items surfaced only in the CARO annexure, not itemized in Note 36's summary table** (all standalone-level, immaterial individually and in aggregate — total ~₹17.9mn, ~0.07% of net worth): Income Tax ₹0.26mn (AY2012-13, CIT-Appeals), Income Tax ₹3.03mn (AY2016-17, CIT-Appeals — a *different, smaller* standalone-company matter, not to be confused with the ₹302.68mn Busy Infotech items above), GST ₹1.12mn (2018-19), GST ₹0.82mn (2020-21), GST ₹12.68mn (2019-20). These are legitimately below Note 36's disclosure threshold and are covered by the note's catch-all "will not be material" language — recorded here as a **disclosure-completeness positive** (the CARO annexure and Note 36 reconcile cleanly; nothing found in CARO is missing from the company's own assessment of aggregate immateriality) rather than a gap.

---

## 4. Litigation & Claims Not Acknowledged as Debt (A7a-05)

| Claim | Counterparty | Amount | Interim rulings so far | Provided? | Verdict | Source |
|---|---|---:|---|---|---|---|
| Ordinary-course lawsuits, claims and proceedings (unquantified catch-all) | Unnamed / general | Not quantified — management states "will not, either individually or in the aggregate, have a material adverse effect" | None disclosed | Provided where individually probable (per Ind AS 37 policy); nothing specific booked | Green — no adverse interim ruling on any non-tax matter identified anywhere in the pool | [FY26 AR Note 36(a)(ii); FY25 AR Note 36(a)(iii)] |

No named litigant, no specific claim amount, and no adverse interim ruling is disclosed outside the tax/GST items already covered in Section 3. This is a standard boilerplate disclosure for a company of this size and is not itself a red flag.

## 5. Guarantees & Hidden Leverage (A7a-06, A7a-07, A7a-09)

| Instrument | Beneficiary (sub / promoter entity / third party) | Amount | % of net worth (₹24,003.75mn FY26) | Ordinary-course vs outsized? | Verdict | Red Flag | Source |
|---|---|---:|---:|---|---|---|---|
| Corporate guarantees | N/A | **Nil** — auditor-confirmed | 0% | N/A | **Green** — this is a stated Nil, not an absent line: the statutory auditor explicitly reports the Company "has not given any loans, or provided guarantees or securities, as specified under section 185… has not provided any loan, security and guarantees as specified under section 186" | None | [FY26 AR, CARO Annexure A, clause 3(iv)] |
| Bank guarantees | Not disclosed anywhere in the pool | Unknown | Unknown | Cannot assess | **Not separately disclosed** (Amber-at-best per the nil-vs-undisclosed rule — the contingent-liabilities note carries no line item for bank guarantees at all, so its absence is not the same as a stated zero) | None (below materiality; noted as a disclosure-completeness gap) | No source — absence checked across Note 36, CARO Annexure A, and the Capital IQ Capital Structure Details export |
| Letters of credit | Not disclosed anywhere in the pool | Unknown | Unknown | Cannot assess | **Not separately disclosed** — same reasoning as above | None | No source — absence checked, none found |
| Bills discounted with recourse | Not disclosed anywhere in the pool | Unknown | Unknown | Business model (subscription-prepaid, negative operating working capital, no material trade-receivables book — [`earnings/01_historical-financials.md`, footnote 4]) makes a factoring arrangement implausible, but the note itself carries no line confirming a zero | **Not separately disclosed** — recorded per the hard nil-vs-undisclosed rule, not graded Green on business-model inference alone | None | Corroborating context: [`earnings/01_historical-financials.md` §4] |

CARO clause 3(iv) is strong, auditor-attested evidence (better-sourced than a mere absence of a note line) that IndiaMART has given **no** loans, guarantees, or securities to any body corporate — including any promoter or related entity — under Sec 185/186 in FY26. **No RF-RPT-003 trigger**; there is no cross-route to `09_related-party-and-group-forensics` needed on this item, though `09` has not yet run in this cycle and should independently confirm at the RPT-note level when it does. The two "not separately disclosed" rows (bank guarantees/LCs, bills discounted) are genuine disclosure-completeness gaps under the hard rule, even though the business model makes material undisclosed exposure here unlikely.

## 9. Provided vs Only-Disclosed (A7a-14)

| Dispute / claim | Classified as (probable / possible) | Appeal record says | Provision held | Candor verdict | Source |
|---|---|---|---:|---|---|
| Busy Infotech income-tax demands (₹302.68mn combined) | "Possible to be upheld" (i.e., not probable — Ind AS 37 "possible" bucket) | Zero rulings in 3 years — genuinely undetermined, not yet tested at any level | ₹0 | **Green** — the "possible" label is consistent with the actual record; nothing here contradicts management's own characterization | [FY26 AR Note 36(a)(a)-(b)] |
| CESTAT service-tax penalty (₹15.38mn) | "The Company's position in the matter will be tenable" (possible) | **1 loss** — Commissioner (Appeals) rejected the appeal and imposed the 100% penalty | Principal (₹15.38mn) provided; penalty (₹15.38mn) not | **Amber** — one loss already on the record while still labeled tenable/possible; not yet "serial" (would need a second loss, e.g. at CESTAT itself) to trip RF-CL-002, but the label is being tested by the record, not confirmed by it | [FY26 AR Note 36(a)(c)] |
| GST TRAN-1 demand (₹203.80mn) | "The Company believes the case has strong merits… no provision has been made" (possible) | **1 loss** — Commissioner (Appeals), Noida, dismissed the appeal on 2 April 2026, a fact the company itself discloses in the same note | ₹0 (a ₹10.19mn pre-deposit sits as a receivable/asset, not a P&L provision) | **Amber — the item to watch.** This is the company's largest live contingent item and it has now failed its first appellate test while remaining fully unprovided and still labeled "possible." A second loss at the Appellate Tribunal — the next and only remaining forum before this genuinely becomes a serial-loss-unprovided pattern — would flip this row to Red and trigger **RF-CL-002** in next year's report. Management is not concealing the loss (it is stated plainly in the note), but the provisioning stance has not moved despite the adverse ruling | [FY26 AR Note 36(a)(d)] |

**No RF-CL-002 fires this year** — the rule requires losses at multiple appellate levels while unprovided, and the largest item has lost only once so far. But this is the single most decision-relevant fact in this report: a rational minority holder should treat the TRAN-1 matter as one adverse ruling away from a genuine provisioning-candor question, not as a closed, immaterial ₹203.80mn line that "hasn't moved in a year."

## 11. Read

Total contingent liabilities are genuinely small — ₹521.86mn against ₹24,003.75mn of consolidated net worth [FY26 Annual Report, Note 36; Consolidated Balance Sheet] is 2.17%, nowhere near the 5% materiality line this checklist uses, let alone the 25% Red band — so on pure solvency size this is a clean Green, consistent with `balance-sheet-survival/05`'s independent read. But the size test is the wrong place to stop: the note's biggest live item, a ₹203.80mn GST demand over transitional input-tax credit, sat at an **unchanged** ₹219.18mn combined headline figure across FY25 and FY26 while its first-ever appellate ruling — a dismissal by the Commissioner (Appeals), Noida, on 2 April 2026 — went against the company in between. Management disclosed the dismissal candidly in the note's own footnote prose (this is not concealment), but a reader who checks only the summary table would see "no change" where the real story is "lost the first round." The company's provisioning stance has not moved: still labeled "possible," still unprovided, with only the largest remaining forum (the Appellate Tribunal) left before a second loss would make this a genuine RF-CL-002 serial-appeal-loss case. Two smaller findings round out the picture: a ₹13.84mn unexplained drop in the CESTAT matter's already-deposited amount between the FY25 and FY26 auditor's reports, with no narrative anywhere addressing it, and a first-time, unquantified mention of "slight delays in a few cases of Provident Fund" in the FY26 CARO report that was absent from FY25's clean-deposits language. Neither is material in rupee terms, but both are the kind of small, unexplained movements this checklist exists to surface before they compound. What a rational minority holder should watch next: the outcome of the GST TRAN-1 appeal at the Appellate Tribunal (the single most consequential unresolved fact in this note) and whether next year's CARO report shows the PF-delay language persisting or clearing.

## management-governance / 11_accounting-forensics.md

# Accounting Forensics — INDIAMART

**Jurisdiction / regime:** India, listed NSE (INDIAMART) / BSE (542726), SEBI-LODR + Companies Act 2013. Reporting standard: Ind AS. Reporting currency: INR million. Fiscal year ends 31 March ("FY26" = year ended 31-Mar-2026). All figures consolidated unless stated otherwise. No `ciq_facts.json` or `relationships.json` sidecar exists for this run (confirmed by `management-governance/00_governance-data-triage.md`) — every figure below is this agent's own sourced read of the FY26 Annual Report (Ind AS, audited, filed 02-Jun-2026), cross-checked against the Capital IQ (CIQ) financial-statement exports (Tier 5, CLAUDE.md §4). Where a CIQ figure was independently verified against the primary filing text (as it was for every Battery Input below), the filing is cited as the authoritative source per CLAUDE.md §5.

## 0. Sector Gate & Inputs

| Item | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Sector (from triage 00) | Internet / B2B e-commerce marketplace ("Trading Companies and Distributors" / online classifieds-subscription model). **Not** a bank, NBFC, or insurer — the financials-sector overlay does **not** apply to the core business today. A newly incorporated wholly-owned subsidiary, IndiaMART Finance Limited (CIN U69200MH2025PTC440515, "Financial Services"), was certified on 04-Aug-2026 — **after** the FY26 year-end this battery is computed on, and had no reported financial statements in this pool. It is a forward capital-allocation/red-flag watch item, not a segment this run's battery tests. | `management-governance/00_governance-data-triage.md` §5A, §5E.2 row 10 |
| Financials overlay applied? | **No.** Full accrual battery (A8-01, A8-02, A8-03, A8-11, A8-12, A8-14, A8-17, A8-18) runs on the core consolidated entity. | Sector gate per Workflow step 2 |
| Years of audited annual history in pool | 5 (FY22–FY26), via the FY26 Annual Report's own comparatives plus 3 prior annual filings/CIQ history — well above the 2-year minimum. Beneish uses FY26 (t) vs FY25 (t−1); Dechow additionally uses FY24 (t−2) for ΔCASH_SALES/ΔROA, all three balance-sheet dates drawn from the CIQ workbook's own annual columns, cross-checked to the FY26 Annual Report's comparative columns and the FY25 Annual Report. | `_pool_extracts/IndiaMART-InterMESH-Limited-NSEI-INDIAMART-Financials-1__Balance-Sheet.txt`; `IndiaMART_InterMESH_Limited_-_Form_Annual_Report-Jun-02-2026.txt` |
| earnings/06 baseline present? | Yes — `earnings/06_earnings-quality.md` (CFO/PAT, accruals, non-GAAP). Consumed directly for A8-01/11/12 per the Materiality Thresholds instruction; not recomputed. | `analyses/INDIAMART_2026-08-22/earnings/06_earnings-quality.md` |
| balance-sheet-survival/01 debt stack present? | Yes — `balance-sheet-survival/01_capital-structure-and-leverage.md`. Consumed for A14-01/02; governance lens applied on top. | `analyses/INDIAMART_2026-08-22/balance-sheet-survival/01_capital-structure-and-leverage.md` |

## 2. Beneish M-Score (A8-14) — computed with python3, computation shown

Computation executed via Bash (`python3`) from the Section 1 inputs. Full script and output reproduced below for auditability:

```
DSRI = (Rec_t/Sales_t)/(Rec_t-1/Sales_t-1) = (314.86/15690.42)/(449.39/13883.44) = 0.6199
GMI  = GM%_t-1/GM%_t = 55.98%/55.28% = 1.0127
AQI  = [1-(CA+PPE+LTInv)/TA]_t / [same]_t-1
     t:   1-(31935.85+214.35+8329.60)/46246.03 = 0.1247
     t-1: 1-(29356.64+331.56+6664.25)/41336.85 = 0.1206
     AQI = 0.1247/0.1206 = 1.0341
SGI  = Sales_t/Sales_t-1 = 15690.42/13883.44 = 1.1302
DEPI = DepRate_t-1/DepRate_t, DepRate = Dep/(Dep+Net PPE)
     DepRate_t   = 284.48/(284.48+214.35) = 0.5703
     DepRate_t-1 = 329.44/(329.44+331.56) = 0.4984
     DEPI = 0.4984/0.5703 = 0.8739
SGAI = (SGA_t/Sales_t)/(SGA_t-1/Sales_t-1) = (449.06/15690.42)/(128.29/13883.44) = 3.0972
LVGI = [(LTD+CL)/TA]_t / [same]_t-1
     t:   (130.90+13649.72)/46246.03 = 0.2980
     t-1: (227.39+12215.55)/41336.85 = 0.3010
     LVGI = 0.2980/0.3010 = 0.9899
TATA = (NI_t - CFO_t)/TA_t = (4746.82 - 6942.19)/46246.03 = -0.04747

M = -4.84 + 0.920(0.6199) + 0.528(1.0127) + 0.404(1.0341) + 0.892(1.1302)
    + 0.115(0.8739) - 0.172(3.0972) - 0.327(0.9899) + 4.679(-0.04747)
M = -3.2871
```

| Component | Value FY26 | Non-manipulator mean | Manipulator zone | Verdict |
|---|---:|---:|---|---|
| DSRI (receivables outrunning sales) | 0.6199 | 1.031 | ≥1.465 | Green — receivables shrank while sales grew; the opposite of the manipulator pattern |
| GMI (gross margin deteriorating) | 1.0127 | 1.014 | ≥1.193 | Green — margin essentially flat (55.98%→55.28%) |
| AQI (asset quality softening) | 1.0341 | 1.039 | ≥1.254 | Green — near the non-manipulator mean |
| SGI (sales growth pressure) | 1.1302 | 1.134 | ≥1.607 | Green — 13.0% growth, well inside normal range and decelerating (`earnings/01_historical-financials.md` §6) |
| DEPI (depreciation rate slowing) | 0.8739 | 1.001 | ≥1.077 | Green — depreciation rate rose (base shrinking faster than the charge), the opposite of the manipulator pattern |
| SGAI (overhead vs sales) | 3.0972 | 1.054 | not banded (negative weight in M) | **Flag, not a red band.** Driven by a real, disclosed 6× jump in advertising spend (₹56.64mn→₹339.13mn, Note 24) inside a CIQ-constructed SG&A bucket that has no statutory equivalent in this filing (single-step Ind AS P&L). A genuine cost increase, not an accrual-manipulation signal — and its −0.172 weight in the composite *lowers* M, so this input cannot manufacture a false Red |
| LVGI (leverage rising) | 0.9899 | 1.037 | ≥1.111 | Green — leverage essentially flat; company remains net cash throughout (`balance-sheet-survival/01`) |
| TATA (accruals vs assets) | -0.04747 | 0.018 | ≥0.031 | Green, strongly so — CFO exceeded net income by ₹2,195mn; cash confirms profit rather than accruals inflating it |

Composite: **M = -3.2871**

| Composite / rule | Value | Green band | Amber band | Red band | Verdict |
|---|---:|---|---|---|---|
| M-score | **-3.2871** | < −2.22, no component in zone | −2.22 to −1.78 | > −1.78 | **Green** |
| Components in manipulator zone | **0 of 7 banded** | 0 | 1–2 | ≥3 | **Green** |

## 3. Dechow F-Score & Accrual Battery (A8-17, A8-18) — computed with python3, computation shown

All three balance-sheet dates required (t=FY26, t−1=FY25, t−2=FY24) are present in the pool (Section 1 + footnote). RSST accruals follow the Richardson (2005) decomposition, operationalised for this balance sheet as: **WC** = (Current assets excl. cash/ST-investments/trading securities) − (Current liabilities excl. current lease/debt); **NCO** = (Total assets − Current assets − LT investments) − (Total liabilities − Current liabilities − LT lease); **FIN** = (ST investments + Trading securities + LT investments) − (Current + LT lease liabilities). This split is this agent's own operationalisation of the standard Richardson accrual model to available line items (labelled, not a company-disclosed sub-total — CLAUDE.md §15) — shown in full below for auditability.

```
FY24: WC=-10,091.18  NCO=-609.07   FIN=27,213.35
FY25: WC=-11,479.16  NCO=-1,725.05 FIN=34,322.27
FY26: WC=-12,816.40  NCO=-2,481.08 FIN=38,497.10

dWC = WC_t - WC_t-1 = -12,816.40 - (-11,479.16) = -1,337.24
dNCO = NCO_t - NCO_t-1 = -2,481.08 - (-1,725.05) = -756.03
dFIN = FIN_t - FIN_t-1 = 38,497.10 - 34,322.27 = 4,174.83
avg TA_t = (46,246.03 + 41,336.85)/2 = 43,791.44
RSST = (dWC+dNCO+dFIN)/avg TA_t = 2,081.56/43,791.44 = 0.0475  (4.75% of avg assets)

dREC = (Rec_t - Rec_t-1)/avg TA_t = (314.86-449.39)/43,791.44 = -0.0031  (-0.31%)
dINV = 0.0  (no inventory)

SOFT_t  = (TA_t - PPE_t - Cash_t)/TA_t = (46,246.03-214.35-804.13)/46,246.03 = 0.9780  (97.80%)
SOFT_t-1 = (41,336.85-331.56-734.84)/41,336.85 = 0.9742  (97.42%)

cash_sales_t   = Sales_t - (Rec_t - Rec_t-1) = 15,690.42 - (-134.53) = 15,824.95
cash_sales_t-1 = Sales_t-1 - (Rec_t-1 - Rec_t-2) = 13,883.44 - 50.61 = 13,832.83
dCASH_SALES = (15,824.95/13,832.83) - 1 = 0.1440

avg TA_t-1 = (41,336.85+34,486.03)/2 = 37,911.44
ROA_t   = NI_t/avg TA_t     = 4,746.82/43,791.44 = 0.1084
ROA_t-1 = NI_t-1/avg TA_t-1 = 5,506.98/37,911.44 = 0.1453
dROA = ROA_t - ROA_t-1 = -0.0369

ISSUE = 0  (no material equity/debt raised in FY26 — Section 1)

pred = -7.893 + 0.790(0.0475) + 2.518(-0.0031) + 1.191(0) + 1.979(0.9780)
       + 0.171(0.1440) - 0.932(-0.0369) + 1.029(0)
     = -5.8688
probability = e^pred/(1+e^pred) = 0.002818
F = probability / 0.0037 = 0.762
```

| Test | Value | Green band | Red band | Verdict | Evidence |
|---|---:|---|---|---|---|
| Dechow F-score (1.00 = average company) | **0.762** | <1.00 | ≥1.85 (substantial; ≥2.45 high) | **Green** | Computation above |
| RSST accruals / average assets | **4.75%** | ≤3% | ≥10% | **Amber** — between bands. Driven mainly by dFIN (+₹4,175mn, the growing treasury-securities book funded by the growing customer-prepayment float), not by operating accrual build; net income remains below CFO (TATA negative, §2) | Computation above |
| Soft assets % of total assets | **97.80%, up from 97.42%** | <50% | >65% and rising | **Flag, contextualised, not treated as Red.** This company has almost no PP&E and is entirely working-capital-negative (float-funded), so nearly its whole balance sheet is, by this formula's literal definition, "not PPE, not cash" — but the dominant component (₹30.3bn of the ₹46.2bn total assets) is the third-party-held, NAV-priced treasury book of mutual funds/bonds/ETFs (Note 8), which is economically closer to "hard" (independently priced, quoted/unquoted split disclosed: ₹30,294.05mn quoted, market value = carrying value) than to the goodwill/intangible/estimate-heavy "soft" assets the test is designed to catch. Stripping the treasury book out, soft assets excl. treasury/investments = (46,246.03-214.35-804.13-30,294.05-104.47-8,329.60)/46,246.03 ≈ 14.5% — well under the 50% green line. Reported as a labelled data-fit caveat (CLAUDE.md §15), not a manipulation signal | Computation above; FY26 Annual Report, Note 8 |
| Δ receivables / average assets | **-0.31%** | <2% | ≥5% with no acquisition explaining it | **Green** — receivables fell in absolute terms while revenue grew | Computation above |
| Δ inventory / average assets | **N/A (no inventory)** | <2% | ≥5% | **Not Applicable** — services/subscription business, no inventory (`earnings/06_earnings-quality.md` §3) | — |
| A8-18 — issuance in a flagged year | **No securities issued; F=0.76 <1.85 and TATA=-0.047 <0.031, so the accrual battery is not flagged in the first place** | none while flagged; self-funded | issued while F≥1.85 or TATA≥0.031 (RF-ACC-004) | **Green** — not triggered on either leg (no flag to dress books for, and no issuance occurred) | Section 1 — Cash Flow tab, "Total Debt Issued"/"Issuance of Common Stock" |
