## 4. Cash Authenticity (A8-19)

Scope-matched per the workflow rule: the FY26 Annual Report's Note 20 (Other income, consolidated) itemises interest by source — "on bank deposits" ₹33.55mn, "on corporate deposits and loans" ₹56.57mn, "on security deposits" ₹3.10mn, "other interest income" ₹0.10mn (FY26); the equivalent FY25 split is ₹8.77mn / ₹14.75mn / ₹3.39mn / ₹2.40mn. Only the "on bank deposits" line is used as the numerator below — the corporate-deposit/loan and security-deposit interest are stripped, consistent with the scoping rule.

| Test | Value | Band | Verdict | Evidence |
|---|---:|---|---|---|
| Interest income ÷ average cash & deposits (implied yield) | Numerator: ₹33.55mn (FY26, "on bank deposits" only). Denominator: average of (Cash & equivalents + Short-term "bank deposits" investments) = average(908.60, 841.16) = ₹874.88mn. **Implied yield = 33.55/874.88 = 3.83%** | — | — | Section 1; FY26 Annual Report, Note 20 |
| Period risk-free rate (instrument, source, date) | India 364-day Treasury Bill, cut-off yield ≈5.8% (May-2026 auction print, consistent with RBI's rate-cutting cycle through FY26); used as the FY26-period proxy, labelled as a single-point estimate rather than a full fiscal-year average (a full FY26 time series was not extracted in this run) | — | — | Web: CEIC Data, "India Treasury Bills: Yield: 364 Days," accessed 2026-08-22, 5.769% pa reported for May-2026 (dated, unverified secondary source per CLAUDE.md §4 tier 10) |
| Gap | 5.8% − 3.83% ≈ 197bp (implied yield ≈66% of the risk-free rate) | Green within ~150bp; Red implied <50% of risk-free on a material pile | **Amber** — the gap (≈197bp) exceeds the 150bp Green tolerance, but the implied yield (66% of risk-free) is well above the 50%-of-risk-free Red trigger. The shortfall is structurally explained, not suspicious: of the ₹874.88mn average base, a large share sits in non-interest-bearing "current accounts" (₹573.52mn FY26) and "cheques on hand" (₹230.61mn FY26, float in transit) rather than in interest-bearing term deposits (Note 11) — a blended yield below the T-bill rate is the expected outcome of that mix, not evidence of a fabricated balance. Critically, this is **not a material cash pile** in the Satyam/Wirecard sense: cash & equivalents is ₹804.13mn, 1.7% of total assets — the company's real liquid wealth (₹30.3bn) sits in the separately-disclosed, NAV-priced treasury book of quoted mutual funds/bonds/ETFs (Note 8), which generates its own, separately itemised FVTPL income (₹1,129.48mn FY26) not scoped into this test | Computation above |
| Cash held in company's own name at major banks? | Yes — Note 11 itemises "balance with bank — on current accounts" and named-institution bonds/ICDs in Note 8 (HDFC Bank, State Bank of India, Axis Finance, PNB Housing Finance, LIC, Bajaj Finance, REC, TATA Capital, and Government of India/state securities) — a diversified, named, quoted portfolio, not a single opaque custodian | Red: "held in trust" / third-party custody | **Green** | FY26 Annual Report, Note 8, Note 11 |
| Auditor obtained independent bank confirmations? | Not itemised in the extracted Auditor's Report text (standard SA 330/505 confirmation procedures are working-paper detail, not typically reproduced verbatim in the printed opinion) — no scope limitation, qualification, or cash-related Key Audit Matter was found; the two KAMs disclosed both relate to investment/goodwill valuation, not cash existence | Red: confirmations not independently obtained | **Insufficient Data on this specific sub-item — rests on the confirmation prong per the workflow rule, graded conservatively as not a Red on the available text** (no affirmative evidence of a confirmation failure; unqualified opinion, Big-4-network auditor B S R & Co. LLP) | FY26 Annual Report, Independent Auditor's Report (consolidated + standalone), Key Audit Matters section |

**A8-19 overall verdict: Amber.** No RF-ACC-002 trigger (the Red band requires implied yield <50% of risk-free on a *material* pile, or trust custody, or an affirmative confirmation failure — none of the three conditions is met). Follow-up: obtain the full FY26 fiscal-year 364-day T-bill average (not a single-point May-2026 print) to tighten the risk-free comparison next cycle.

| Revenue growth vs cash-taxes-paid growth | Revenue +16.01%; cash tax growth n/a (base year) | Revenue +13.02%; cash taxes paid +1.26% (₹1,548.42mn→₹1,567.94mn) | Red: revenue grows, cash taxes flat | **Explained, not a manipulation signal.** Consolidated PBT (EBT Incl. Unusual Items) actually *fell* 8.2% FY25→FY26 (₹7,058.09mn→₹6,479.43mn) — driven by a ₹762.71mn drop in the (fully disclosed, recurring) FVTPL treasury gain and a larger share-of-associate-loss (-₹547.72mn vs -₹490.51mn), both non-operating. Cash tax tracks taxable PBT, not revenue; a flat cash-tax bill against a PBT decline is the *expected* relationship, not a red flag. Core operating income (EBIT) grew a modest +1.9% (₹4,922.29mn→₹5,015.93mn) against 13.0% revenue growth — a margin story (higher marketing spend, §2 SGAI), not a paper-revenue story | `earnings/06_earnings-quality.md` §5, §7; Section 1 above |
| Collections proxy (revenue − Δreceivables) vs revenue | Collections proxy ₹13,832.83mn vs revenue ₹13,883.44mn (99.6%) | Collections proxy ₹15,824.95mn vs revenue ₹15,690.42mn (100.9%) — **collections growth (+14.4%) outpaced revenue growth (+13.0%)** because receivables fell in absolute terms | Red: collections flat while revenue grows | **Green, strongly so** — cash collections grew faster than recognised revenue, the opposite of the paper-revenue pattern | Section 3 computation (dCASH_SALES) |
| Unbilled revenue + contract assets, % of revenue | Nil | Nil — "The Group has Nil contract assets as at 31 March 2026 (31 March 2025: Nil)" | Green <10%; Red >25% or mark-to-model | **Green** | FY26 Annual Report, Note 10 (consolidated), explicit statement |
| Order book: contractually binding or MOU-paper? | Not applicable in the classic project/order-book sense — the revenue model is prepaid annual/multi-year subscriptions, not order backlogs. The closest analogue, deferred revenue (Unearned Revenue), is a customer-cash-in-hand liability, not a soft pipeline claim: total Unearned Revenue grew every year FY22–FY26 (₹9,070mn→₹19,653mn), outpacing recognised-revenue growth in FY26 (+17.1% vs +13.0%) | Red: non-binding MOUs presented as demand | **Green / Not Applicable to this business model** — no MOU-style forward-demand claims found in the filings or transcripts reviewed | `earnings/06_earnings-quality.md` §3; FY26 Annual Report, Note 19 |

**A8-15 overall verdict: Green.** No RF-ACC-003 trigger.

## 12. Forensics Read

Both computed batteries clear Green with room to spare: Beneish M = **-3.29** (composite floor for Green is -2.22; zero of seven banded components sit in the manipulator zone) and Dechow F = **0.76** (Green floor is <1.00), computed from two fully-cited consecutive audited annual filings with a genuine three-balance-sheet-date history for the components that need it. The single measured fact that would worry a forensic reader most in a different company — cash from operations exceeding net income in every one of the last five years (CFO/PAT 113%–167%) alongside deferred revenue that has never once declined — is here the strongest possible signal of genuine, cash-backed earnings, not a red flag: it is the direct opposite of the Satyam pattern (profits the cash never confirms) and the direct opposite of channel-stuffing (receivables actually *fell* while revenue grew, and collections growth outpaced revenue growth). The one item that rhymes, faintly, with a governance-lens concern is the recurring ~30–40%-of-PBT treasury FVTPL gain (Section 8) that `earnings/06_earnings-quality.md` already flagged as its single biggest earnings-quality caveat — it is fully disclosed, Ind AS-mandated, and not manipulative, but it means diluted EPS is a materially noisier number than EBIT/EBITDA for this company, and a reader who anchors on EPS alone would be misreading the trend. Cash authenticity grades Amber, not Green or Red: the implied yield on the company's small (₹804mn, 1.7%-of-assets) operating cash balance runs about two-thirds of the period risk-free rate — a real but modest gap, structurally explained by a cash mix dominated by non-interest-bearing current accounts, and nowhere near the <50%-of-risk-free or trust-custody thresholds that would signal a fabricated pile; the company's real liquid wealth sits in a separately-verified, quoted, ~₹30bn treasury book, not in the tested cash line. The sector overlay does not apply (this is not a bank/NBFC/insurer); no regulator has ever found a divergence between IndiaMART's reported numbers and an inspection; and the balance sheet carries no leverage to misuse — the one leverage-adjacent item worth naming (a new ₹897mn placement of inter-corporate deposits with third-party NBFCs) is ordinary-course treasury diversification to investment-grade counterparties, not a related-party leakage channel. A rational minority holder should conclude: these books are not showing the patterns that precede blow-ups, and the one place to keep watching is the growing wedge between statutory PBT/EPS and the underlying EBIT/EBITDA trend, and the treasury-book exposure it implies.

# Regulatory, Legal & Compliance — INDIAMART

**Resume note:** This run is an exact scoped resume (`NOSTRA_EXACT_MODULE_RESUME=1`). Per the Hard Rule, no prior-dated `management-governance` folder was Globbed, searched, or read for this agent's own delta-refresh. This is a **full sweep dated 2026-08-22 — exact scoped resume; historical register not read.** `balance-sheet-survival` is allowlisted (`NOSTRA_EXACT_MODULE_INPUTS=balance-sheet-survival,business-model,earnings`) and present in this run's folder, so `01_capital-structure-and-leverage.md` was read directly (not recomputed) for A14-03.

**Jurisdiction & regime:** India — SEBI (Listing Obligations and Disclosure Requirements) Regulations, 2015 + Companies Act, 2013, Ind AS reporting, INR, fiscal year ends 31 March [`00_governance-data-triage.md`, §"Primary listing country / Exchange / Filing regime"]. Listed on **both NSE (ticker INDIAMART) and BSE (scrip 542726)** — both exchanges swept per the Hard Rule. CIN: L74899DL1999PLC101534, confirmed directly from the FY26 Integrated Annual Report's own cover page and signature block [FY26 Annual Report (Jun-02-2026), p.1, signature page].

**Cross-module deference:** `business-model/01_disqualifier-scan.md` found **no hard disqualifier triggered** (0/8, incl. the regulatory-enforcement lock) and **no compounding near-miss** — reported verbatim, not re-adjudicated here. That scan separately logged (as a "soft signal, not a lock") the same two FY2025-26 Secretarial Audit PIT lapses this report details in Section 5, and explicitly routed the integrity question to this module. `07_people-integrity-dossiers.md` (same run) flagged the single highest-value follow-up of this whole run: an apparently unresolved Feb-2021 criminal FIR against founder-CEO Dinesh Chandra Agarwal and co-founder-WTD Brijesh Kumar Agrawal, graded **Material concerns** on their own record (not an exposure floor), routed to this agent for the canonical company-level litigation-reconciliation sweep (A9-10). That investigation is the centerpiece of Section 8 below.

**Verdict vs A7-01: Green.** 13 straight quarters of results filed well inside the regulatory clock, no fines for late filing, no exchange nudges found. The one adjacent item found (Nalanda's own SAST delay) is a third party's compliance record, not IndiaMART's, and is excluded from the company's score.

There is consequently no IL&FS-style abrupt-downgrade risk, no non-cooperation tag, and no company-requested-withdrawal-with-debt-outstanding pattern to hunt for, because the underlying instrument these bands test does not exist.

| Ombudsperson / independent channel | Yes — complaints can be routed to the Ombudsperson and/or the Chairman of the Audit Committee through specified channels; affirmation that no personnel have been denied access to the Audit Committee Chairman | Same source; ombudsperson@indiamart.com listed as a contact channel |
| Complaint statistics disclosed | **Yes, and Nil.** The independent statutory auditor's CARO annexure states directly: *"As represented to us by the management, there are no whistle blower complaints received by the Company during the year"* [FY26 AR, CARO Annexure clause 3(x)(c)]. A separate investor/shareholder-complaint table (Sec 178 grievance context, near the IEPF/dividend section) shows 2 filed / 2 disposed / 0 pending during FY26 — these are investor/RTA-service complaints, not whistleblower/fraud-alleging complaints, and are distinguished here to avoid conflating the two disclosure lines | FY26 Annual Report, CARO Annexure (auditor-attested, not merely management-asserted); Corporate Governance Report investor-complaint table |
| Fraud-alleging complaints and their outcomes | None received during FY26 per the auditor-attested CARO statement above | Same |

**Verdict vs A9-03: Green.** Mechanism, ombudsperson, and complaint statistics are all disclosed, and — unusually — the "nil complaints" figure is auditor-attested (CARO), not a bare management assertion, which is a stronger evidentiary basis than most companies provide for this item.

## 5. Insider-Trading Conduct (A9-07)

| Person / entity | Violation type | Regulator or exchange action | Date | Handed to 07? | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Rajesh Sawhney** (Independent Director; PAN AICPS9783N) | Contra-trade violation — inadvertent buy orders placed via a broker app glitch while a valid sell pre-clearance for 1,000 ESOP shares was in effect | Self-reported by the company (Compliance Officer Manoj Bhargava) to BSE + NSE, 24-May-2021; company-imposed ₹1,00,000 monetary penalty remitted to SEBI's Investor Protection & Education Fund; no SEBI enforcement order | Trades executed 04-May-2021 and 10-May-2021; realised trading loss on the contra trade ₹15,890 | **Yes — named individual, hand to 07** for his person-level dossier (07's Discovery Register already lists him as subject #23, "former ID," Tier C, Scoped — this specific PIT event should be added to his dossier) | BSE/NSE corporate filing, 24-May-2021, read in full 2026-08-22 |
| **Rajesh Sawhney** (same person, prior year) | Delayed Form-C disclosure under Reg 7(2), SEBI PIT Regulations | Self-reported; company-imposed ₹6,00,000 monetary penalty (part of the profit earned on the trade), reported to SEBI 17-Feb-2020 | Trade of 1,344 equity shares executed 04-Feb-2020 | **Yes — same handoff as above** | Same 24-May-2021 filing (Item 7, "previous instances of violations") |
| Unnamed "Designated Person" #1 (FY26) | Created a pledge on Company equity shares **without seeking pre-clearance from the Compliance Officer** | Secretarial Auditor's finding (M/s RMG & Associates), FY2025-26 audit period; intimated to the Stock Exchanges | FY 2025-26 (audit period 01-Apr-2025 to 31-Mar-2026) | Individual not named in the pool — cannot hand to 07 by name; flagged for 07/99 to confirm identity if the primary Secretarial Audit annexure names them | FY26 Annual Report, Secretarial Audit Report, Annexure-A; cross-referenced against `business-model/01_disqualifier-scan.md`'s "Integrity note" |
| Unnamed "Designated Person" #2 (FY26) | Executed a trade of **one (1) equity share** during the closure of a notional trading window | Secretarial Auditor's finding; intimated to the Stock Exchanges | FY 2025-26 | Individual not named in the pool | Same source |

**Verdict vs A9-07: Amber, not Green.** Five events across three separate disclosure episodes (Feb-2020, May-2021, FY2025-26) is a genuine repeated-lapse pattern in PIT Code hygiene — the Materiality Thresholds table treats repetition as the signal even where individual amounts are immaterial (the largest single trade value here is ~₹6.8L; the ₹6L penalty on the 2020 event is the only one approaching materiality, and it is a penalty paid TO SEBI's investor-protection fund, not a loss to the company or its minority shareholders). What keeps this from being scored Red rather than Amber: **every one of the five events was self-detected, self-reported to the exchanges within the required window, and self-penalised by the company** — the opposite of the concealment pattern the Red band is calibrated against (compare: a company that never reports its own PIT lapses until a regulator finds them). RF-CMP-003 fires at the severity level appropriate to a repeated-but-transparent hygiene issue (High, not Critical — no enforcement order exists, no KMP/promoter involved, and no evidence of trading on unpublished price-sensitive information in any of the five events — each report explicitly states the DP was not in possession of UPSI).

**Red Flag: RF-CMP-003** — Insider-trading / trading-window violations by KMP or promoter (A9-07). *Note on scope:* none of the five events involved a promoter or KMP — Rajesh Sawhney is an Independent Director (not promoter/KMP), and the two FY26 Designated Persons are unnamed (likely employee-level, given "Designated Person" is a broader PIT-regulation category than KMP). The trigger is recorded here for the repeated pattern under A9-07's broader "PIT violations by KMP/promoter, or regulator orders on insider trading" band, with the caveat stated explicitly that the KMP/promoter-specific sub-condition was not met by any of the five events found.

**Verdict vs A9-09: Green.** The company itself carries no disclosed or discovered environmental/consumer penalty. Distinguishing "third parties misuse the platform" from "the company itself was penalised" avoids manufacturing a red flag out of an industry-wide, platform-liability pattern that no regulator has actually enforced against IndiaMART.

## 8. Litigation-Register Reconciliation (A9-10) — the sharpest test, and the headline finding

| Material case found in the sweep | Forum | Where found | Exposure | In filings' legal/contingency disclosures? | If absent — finding | Source |
|---|---|---|---:|---|---|---|
| **FIR against founder-CEO Dinesh Chandra Agarwal (DIN 00191800) and co-founder-WTD Brijesh Kumar Agrawal (DIN 00191760), under IPC §295 (deliberately outraging religious feelings) + IT Act §66D, filed Govardhan police station, Mathura (UP), 07-Feb-2021**, naming a third Mathura-based supplier (Ankur Agrawal) as co-accused, over the sale of "Govardhan Shila" stones by a third-party seller listed on the IndiaMART platform | Uttar Pradesh Police (Govardhan PS, Mathura district) — a **police FIR/investigation-stage matter**, not yet a court filing on the evidence found (see coverage note below) | **Dated adverse media** — 5 independent, reputable, contemporaneously-dated outlets (National Herald India, The Print, DNA India, Business Standard, ETV Bharat), all February 2021. **NOT found in court records**: an Indian Kanoon full-text search for `"Govardhan Shila"`, for `"Dinesh Agarwal" "Govardhan"`, and for `"IndiaMART" "295"` returned no matching judgment naming either founder in this matter — no §482 CrPC quashing petition, chargesheet-related order, or trial record was located on Indian Kanoon (which indexes SC/HC/tribunal orders; district-court coverage is inherently partial) | Not sizeable/quantified anywhere (an FIR carries no stated monetary exposure; hand to `10` only if/when it crystallises into a quantifiable claim — nothing to hand at this stage) | **No** — searched the full text of the FY26 Integrated Annual Report (17,695 lines) for "criminal," "IPC," "Section 295," "Govardhan," "police," "66D," "penal code," and "proceedings against director": the ONLY hit is the standard SEBI/MCA-debarment negative-certification boilerplate (RMG & Associates certificate, Annexure-9) confirming no director "has been debarred or disqualified... by virtue of any SEBI Order or any other such authority" — a narrower, different disclosure (regulatory debarment) that does not address a pending criminal FIR. Note 35/36 (contingent liabilities) discloses only two indirect-tax disputes (GST/service-tax, ₹219.18mn total) — no criminal/legal-proceedings line exists at all in the note. No mention anywhere in the Board's Report, Corporate Governance Report, or BRSR | **This is the finding.** A dated, five-outlet-corroborated, unresolved criminal FIR against the two most powerful individuals at the company — the founder-CEO (29.09% promoter stake) and co-founder-WTD (19.99% promoter stake), together holding ~49% of the company and running it day to day — is invisible anywhere in the company's own primary disclosure document. **RF-CMP-001** fires: the non-disclosure is the finding, independent of the underlying matter's eventual merits, per the Hard Rule that a material case visible in public records but absent from filings is Red regardless of case merits | Web (dated adverse media): National Herald India, The Print, DNA India, Business Standard, ETV Bharat, all Feb-2021 (retrieved and cross-checked 2026-08-22); Indian Kanoon full-text searches, 2026-08-22, no result; FY26 Annual Report (Jun-02-2026), full-text grep, 2026-08-22, no hit on any of the 8 search terms |

**Materiality call (per the Materiality Thresholds table):** this is a criminal ALLEGATION, not an adjudicated finding — no chargesheet, discharge, quashing, or conviction was located in this sweep despite a genuine and repeated attempt (Indian Kanoon full-text search across multiple phrasings; a dated-news search for any 2022–2026 case-status update; a targeted search for a §482 CrPC quashing petition at the Allahabad High Court — all returned nothing beyond the original Feb-2021 filing coverage). Per the Materiality Thresholds table, "an unresolved allegation is High, not Critical" — consistent with `07`'s own person-level grade of **Material concerns** (not Disqualifying) for both founders on this same fact, and consistent with the Non-Negotiable Gate NOT being tripped (allegations do not fail the gate; only proven facts do). **This finding is High materiality, not Critical**, and does **not** re-open or contest the disqualifier-scan's "no hard lock" conclusion (deferred to per the Disqualifier Deference rule) — it sits squarely in the spectrum below the hard lock that this module exists to cover.

**What this sweep could NOT establish (stated honestly, not glossed over):** the FIR's current procedural status beyond the original February 2021 filing. No chargesheet, no discharge order, no quashing order, and no conviction/acquittal record was found on any database or in any dated media searched. This is recorded as **coverage-limited on this specific sub-question** — the eCourts Mathura-district portal and the Allahabad High Court's case-status portal are both captcha/browser-gated and could not be queried directly by this agent (consistent with `07`'s own identical coverage note on the same axis). It remains possible that the matter was quietly closed (e.g., a negative final report / closure report filed by police, which would not necessarily generate a reported judgment) or that it remains formally open with no further public action taken — both are equally consistent with the "nothing found beyond Feb-2021" result, and this sweep cannot distinguish between them. This ambiguity is itself part of why the finding is scored High materiality (real, unresolved, and undisclosed) rather than escalated further (nothing proves it is still live, either).

**Person/company boundary, stated explicitly:** the underlying integrity fact belongs to `07` (person-level: A16-04, RF-PPL-001, already scored — Material concerns on both founders, capping People & network integrity at 50). What this agent adds, and owns, is the **disclosure-integrity question**: does the company's own filing record show this to a reader who never left the annual report? It does not. RF-CMP-001 is recorded here as the company-level companion finding to `07`'s person-level RF-PPL-001 — the same underlying fact, two different checklist items, two different owners, exactly as the module's design intends.

**No other material undisclosed case was found.** The two indirect-tax disputes (GST/service-tax) are fully itemised in the contingent-liabilities note (`10`'s territory) and are not a reconciliation gap. No other court, tribunal, or regulator record naming the company or its directors was found absent from the filings in this sweep.

---

## 9. Read

The public record adds one significant fact the filings omit entirely: an unresolved, dated, five-outlet-corroborated 2021 criminal FIR against the CEO and co-founder-WTD — the two people who together control the company and hold roughly half its equity — that a reader relying solely on the FY26 Annual Report would never learn exists; the company's only related disclosure is a narrower "not debarred by SEBI/MCA" boilerplate that does not cover this matter. Outside that one gap, the compliance machinery reads as genuinely real rather than ornamental: 13 straight quarters of results filed comfortably inside the SEBI LODR clock, zero exchange fines found across both NSE and BSE over multiple years, an auditor-attested (not merely management-asserted) nil-whistleblower-complaint record, and — unusually for this checklist — a company that **voluntarily self-reports its own PIT Code lapses** to the exchanges and penalises itself (five such events across 2020, 2021 and FY26, none escalated to a SEBI order) rather than waiting to be caught. The worst single fact in this report is the FIR non-disclosure; the second-worst is the repeated-but-transparent PIT lapse pattern, which is a real hygiene weakness but the opposite of concealment. Zero debt means A9-05/A14-03 are cleanly Not Applicable rather than merely absent. **Overall: "hygiene lapses, watch"** on the PIT pattern, plus a company-level disclosure gap on the FIR that should be surfaced to synthesis as a cap candidate under the undisclosed-matter Score Cap Rule (Disclosure candor max 50; Governance risk floor 60) — the underlying allegation does not, on the evidence gathered here, rise to the kind of proven fact that would justify walking away outright, but the non-disclosure and the still-unconfirmed procedural status both belong in front of the master synthesizer as open items, not resolved ones.
